Generalized free wreath products and their operator algebras Thanks: P.F. is partially supported by the CNRS IEA GAOA
Pierre Fima, Arthur Troupel

Abstract
. We develop a new approach on free wreath products, generalizing the constructions of Bichon and of Fima-Pittau. We show stability properties for certain approximation properties such as exactness, Haagerup property, hyperlinearity and K-amenability. We study qualitative properties of the associated von Neumann algebra: factoriality, primeness and absence of Cartan subalgebra and we give a formula for Connes’ T T -invariant. Finally, we give some explicit computations of K-theory groups for C*-algebras of generalized free wreath products.



1.
Introduction

Quantum information theory has now emerged as a powerful framework for processing information, leveraging the rich structures of quantum mechanics. Over the past decade, there has been a growing interest in understanding probability distributions arising from models of entangled quantum systems. In this context, compact quantum groups (CQG), as introduced by Woronowicz
[
Wor87
,
Wor88
,
Wor98
]
, play a fundamental role in capturing the natural notion of symmetry. More recently, deep connections have been established with long-standing problems in operator algebras. A major breakthrough in this area is the proposed resolution of the Connes Embedding Problem by Ji, Natarajan, Vidick, Wright, and Yuen in 2020
[
JNV+22
]
, which employs techniques from quantum information theory to address a fundamental question regarding the approximation of operator algebras using finite structures. Simultaneously, intriguing links have emerged between quantum information theory and compact quantum groups. In particular, the well-established connection between quantum automorphisms and quantum information theory
[
LMR20
]
provides insight into non-local games and physically observable quantum behaviors.

We believe that a detailed study of operator algebras associated with quantum automorphism groups could lead to concrete applications in quantum information theory. The present paper is dedicated to such an investigation.

Following Woronowicz’s development of compact quantum group theory—which notably includes Drinfeld and Jimbo’s
q
-deformations of compact Lie groups
[
Dri86
,
Dri87
,
Jim85
]
—new examples of compact quantum groups emerged from Wang’s work
[
Wan95
,
Wan98
]
. Among them are the quantum permutation group
S_{N}^{+}
and, more generally, the quantum automorphism group
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
of a finite-dimensional C*-algebra equipped with a faithful state
\psi

[
Ban99
,
Ban02
]
. The operator algebras associated with
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
have been extensively studied
[
Bra13
,
Voi17
]
. Additionally, the quantum automorphism group of a finite graph was introduced by Bichon in
[
Bic03
]
. It is well-known that the classical automorphism group of the graph obtained by taking the
N
-fold disjoint union of a finite connected graph
\mathcal{G}
is given by the wreath product of the classical automorphism group of
\mathcal{G}
with
S_{N}
. Motivated by this, Bichon introduced a free version of the wreath product
[
Bic04
]
, which satisfies a similar property for quantum automorphisms, when replacing the group
S_{N}
by the quantum permutation group
S_{N}^{+}
, offering a parallel geometric interpretation in the quantum setting.

This
free wreath product
introduced by Bichon has been subsequently generalized in various directions
[
Pit16
,
FP16
,
TW18
,
FS18
,
Fre22
]
, and is the central object of study in this paper. The operator algebras associated with Bichon’s free wreath product
G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}
, where
G
is a compact quantum group, are now well understood
[
FT24
]
. The primary goal of this work is to extend this understanding to a more general free wreath product, namely
G\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
as defined in
[
FP16
]
.

During our study, we found it beneficial to develop an alternative approach to defining the quantum group
G\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
, diverging from the existing methods in
[
FP16
]
and
[
TW18
]
. Compact quantum groups, are naturally categorical objects, and both previous approaches define the free wreath product via Tannaka-Krein reconstruction. While this method is well-adapted to grasp the representation theory of the free wreath product, our prior research
[
FT24
]
on operator algebras associated with Bichon’s free wreath product
G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}
revealed that the representation theory is not a mandatory step toward understanding the operator algebras. Instead, the key ingredient was the construction of a specific representation of the C*-algebra of the free wreath product, through its universal property as originally defined by Bichon rather than through Tannaka-Krein reconstruction.

This realization led us to seek a new definition of the C*-algebra of the free wreath product
G\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
, aiming for a universal property simpler than that of the Tannaka-Krein approach. In doing so, we were able to unify and generalize various constructions from
[
Bic04
,
Pit16
,
FP16
,
Fre22
]
. Specifically, given any compact quantum group
G
and any
\psi
-preserving action
H\curvearrowright B
of a compact quantum group
H
on a finite-dimensional C*-algebra
B
, we construct in Theorem
3.5
a compact quantum group
G\wr_{*,\beta}H
. This construction coincides with
G\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
when considering the universal action
\beta:{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
. Furthermore, we introduce an amalgamated version,
G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
, incorporating a dual quantum subgroup
F
of
G
, thereby unifying the constructions in
[
Pit16
,
FP16
]
with those in
[
Fre22
]
.

In the preliminary section, Section
2
after introducing the necessary background on amalgamated free products and compact quantum groups, we provide a detailed review of compact quantum group actions on finite-dimensional C*-algebras and establish key technical lemmas that will be used throughout the paper.

In Section
3
, we construct the (amalgamated) generalized free wreath product
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
and present several examples.

Section
4
develops our main analytical tool: the block decomposition technique, which we use to compute the Haar measure.

In Section
5
, we use the Haar measure to analyze the reduced C*-algebra, the von Neumann algebra, and the modular structure of
\mathbb{G}
. This, in turn, allows us to investigate approximation properties of the associated operator algebras. To state our first result, we will use the following non-standard terminology: we will always denote by
\widehat{G}
the discrete dual of a compact quantum group
G
, and we say that
\widehat{G}
has the
Haagerup property
if
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
has the Haagerup property (with respect to the Haar state, see
[
CS15
]
) and that
\widehat{G}
is
exact
when the reduced C*-algebra
C_{r}(G)
is an exact C*-algebra. When
G
is Kac, we say that its dual
\widehat{G}
is
hyperlinear
if the finite von Neumann algebra
({\rm L}^{\infty}(G),h_{G})
embeds in an ultraproduct of the hyperfinite
{\rm II}_{1}
-factor in a trace preserving way, where
h_{G}
denotes the Haar state on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
. Finally,
G
is called
coamenable
when the canonical surjection from its full C*-algebra
C(G)
to
C_{r}(G)
is an isomorphism. The main result is the following, see Theorem
5.10
.

Theorem A
.

Let
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
be an ergodic action,
G
any compact quantum group with dual quantum subgroup
F
and define
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
. The following holds.

(1)

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is exact if and only if both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are exact.

(2)

If
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite,
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
has the Haagerup property if and only if both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
have the Haagerup property.

(3)

\mathbb{G}
is Kac if and only if both
G
and
H
are Kac and in this case, assuming that
F
is coamenable,
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is hyperlinear if and only if both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are hyperlinear.

(4)

If
F
is trivial and both
G
and
H
are non-trivial then
\mathbb{G}
is coamenable if and only if
G=H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
B\in\{\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{2}\}
.

Note that this result is particularly applicable to the universal action
\beta:{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
, which is ergodic so that Theorem
A
covers in particular the case of Fima-Pittau free wreath products. The statement of Theorem
A
concerning exactness is only known for Fima-Pittau free wreath products
[
FP16
]
(see also
[
LT16
]
for the more specific case of Bichon’s free wreath-products). The other statements of Theorem
A
are only known for Bichon’s free wreath products
[
FT24
]
i.e. when considering the universal action
\beta\,:\,S_{N}^{+}\curvearrowright\mathbb{C}^{N}
. Let us however mention the works
[
Lem14
,
LT16
,
FP16
,
TW18
]
containing results in relation with the stability of the Haagerup property (actually the stability of the
central ACPAP
) for some specific free wreath products and the work
[
BCF20
]
, based on the topological generation method developed in
[
BCV17
]
, concerning the hyperlinearity of quantum reflection groups
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{s}}\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}
.

In Section
6
, we delve into the qualitative properties of the von Neumann algebra
\text{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Following a preliminary section on intertwining theory, we examine the factoriality and Connes’
T
invariant of
\text{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
.

Theorem B
.

Suppose that
\beta
is
2
-ergodic, both
G
and
H
are infinite quantum groups and
F
is trivial then
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a non amenable factor of type
{\rm II}_{1}
or
{\rm III}
and its
T
invariant is:

T({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm id}\text{ and }\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}\}.

where
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}
is the modular group of the Haar state of
G
(and the same for
H
). Moreover
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is of type
{\rm II}_{1}
if and only if both
G
and
H
are Kac.

We actually prove a more general result in Theorem
6.15
, where we allow the amalgam
F
to be non-trivial. In this case,
\text{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is not always a factor, and we explicitly compute the center. Note that Theorem
B
particularly applies to the universal action
\beta:{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
, which is 2-ergodic (see Section
2
for the definition). The statement of Theorem
B
is known only in the case of Bichon’s free wreath product
G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}
, as seen in
[
FT24
]
. Let us also mention
[
Lem14
,
Wah15
]
where factoriality is proved for some specific cases of Bichon’s free wreath products.

In Section
6
, we also study primeness and absence of Cartan subalgebras. Recall that a factor
M
is called
prime
if it is not isomorphic to the tensor product
P\otimes Q
of two diffuse factors
P
and
Q
. Also, we say that a von Neumann subalgebra
A\subset M
of a von Neumann algebra is a
Cartan subalgebra
if
A
is maximal abelian i.e.
A^{\prime}\cap M=A
,
regular
i.e.
M
is generated, as a von Neumann algebra, by the normalizer
\{u\in\mathcal{U}(M)\,:\,uAu^{*}=A\}
of
A
and
A

has expectation
i.e. there is a faithful normal conditional expectation from
M
to
A
. The result is the following, see Theorem
6.20
.

Theorem C
.

Suppose that
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 3
,
\beta
is
2
-ergodic, both
G
and
H
are infinite quantum groups and
F
is trivial then the following holds.

(1)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is prime.

(2)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
has no Cartan subalgebras.

Once again, the previous theorem applies to the particular case of the universal action
\beta:\text{Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
, thus significantly generalizing the results of
[
FT24
]
.

In Section
7
, we study the
K
-theory of C*-algebras of free wreath products. We begin by characterizing the
K
-amenability of a free wreath product. Recall that
\widehat{G}
is
K-amenable
if the canonical surjection from the full C*-algebra
C(G)
to the reduced C*-algebra
C_{r}(G)
is a KK-equivalence. It is known that
\widehat{S_{N}^{+}}
is K-amenable
[
Voi17
]
and, by using a monoidal equivalence argument, it is known
[
FM20
]
that if
G
is torsion-free and satisfies the strong Baum-Connes conjecture then
\widehat{G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}}
is K-amenable. The following result, corresponding to Theorem
7.3
, was only known in the case of Bichon’s free wreath products, see
[
FT24
, Theorem A]
.

Theorem D
.

Let
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
be any ergodic action,
G
any compact quantum group with dual quantum subgroup and
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
the free wreath product. The following are equivalent.

(1)

\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are
K
-amenable.

(2)

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is
K
-amenable.

We then provide long exact sequences to compute the
K
-theoretic groups of
C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})
(which denotes either the full or the reduced C*-algebra of
\mathbb{G}
). However, to derive explicit computations from these long exact sequences, one needs to know the
K
-theoretic groups
K_{i}(C_{\bullet}(H))
as well as explicit generators of
K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(H))
. Note that the
K
-theory of
C_{\bullet}({{\rm Aut}}^{+}(B,\psi))
has been computed by Voigt
[
Voi17
]
, but explicit generators of the
K_{0}
-group are known only for
S_{N}^{+}
. Nevertheless, we find explicit generators of the
K_{0}
-group in the case
B=M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
, which allows us to obtain some explicit computations. For
s\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we denote by
\mathbb{F}_{s}
the free group with
s
generators and
\mathbb{Z}_{s}:=\mathbb{Z}/s\mathbb{Z}
. The following result is Theorem
7.11
.

Theorem E
.

For all
s,N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and for any faithful state
\psi\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
one has:

K_{i}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{Z}_{s}\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{Z}^{s}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}&\text{if}&i=0,\\
\mathbb{Z}&\text{if}&i=1.\end{array}\right.\quad\text{and,}

K_{i}(C_{\bullet}(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}_{s}\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}&\text{if}&i=0,\\
\mathbb{Z}^{s+1}&\text{if}&i=1.\end{array}\right.

2.
Preliminaries

All von Neumann algebras are supposed to have separable predual and all C*-algebras and Hilbert spaces are supposed to be separable. The scalar product on an Hilbert space, or an Hilbert C*-module, is always right linear. We use the same symbol
\otimes
to denote the algebraic tensor product of unital
*
-algebras, the minimal tensor product of C*-algebras, the tensor product of Hilbert spaces and the tensor product of von Neumann algebras. We use the symbol
\otimes_{{\rm max}}
to denote the maximal tensor product of C*-algebras. When
H
is a Hilbert space (resp. Hilbert
A
-module), we denote by
\mathcal{L}(H)
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{A}(H)
) the C*-algebra of bounded linear operators on
H
(resp. adjointable operators on
H
).

2.1.
Operator algebras

Let
A
,
B
be unital C*-algebras and
\varphi\,:\,A\rightarrow B
a unital completely positive (ucp) map. The
GNS-construction
of
\varphi
is the unique, up to a canonical isomorphism, triple
(H,\pi,\Omega)
, where
H
is a Hilbert C*-module over
B
,
\pi\,:\,A\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{B}(H)
is a unital
*
-homomorphism and
\Omega\in H
is a unit vector such that
\varphi(x)=\langle\Omega,\pi(x)\Omega\rangle
for all
x\in A
and
\pi(A)\Omega\cdot B
is dense in
H
.
The reduced C*-algebra of
\varphi
is defined by
C^{*}_{r}(\varphi):=\pi(A)
, where
\pi
is the GNS-morphism of
\varphi
. By uniqueness of the GNS-construction, it does not depends on a particular choice of the GNS construction
(H,\pi,\Omega)
of
\varphi
. Moreover,
\varphi
is called
GNS-faithful
when its GNS-morphism
\pi
is faithful. In that case,
A
itself is the reduced C*-algebra of
\varphi
. Note that the reduced C*-algebra of
\varphi
is, up to a canonical isomorphism, the unique quotient
\pi\,:\,A\twoheadrightarrow C^{*}_{r}(\varphi)
of
A
such that there is a GNS-faithful ucp map
\varphi_{r}\,:\,C^{*}_{r}(\varphi)\rightarrow B
satisfying
\varphi_{r}\circ\pi=\varphi
. To be more precise, the uniqueness means that if
C
is any unital C*-algebra with a surjective unital
*
-homomorphism
\rho\,:\,A\twoheadrightarrow C
and a GNS-faithful ucp map
\psi\,:\,C\rightarrow B
such that
\psi\circ\rho=\varphi
then there exists a unique unital
*
-isomorphism
\widetilde{\rho}\,:\,C^{*}_{r}(\varphi)\rightarrow C
such that
\widetilde{\rho}\circ\pi=\rho
. Note that
\widetilde{\rho}
automatically intertwines the ucp maps i.e.
\psi\circ\widetilde{\rho}=\varphi_{r}
. The following elementary Remark will be useful.

Remark 2.1
.

Let
\varphi\,:\,A\rightarrow B
be a ucp map with reduced C*-algebra and GNS-morphism
\pi\,:\,A\twoheadrightarrow C^{*}_{r}(\varphi)
and let
\varphi_{r}\,:\,C^{*}_{r}(\varphi)\rightarrow B
be the unique GNS-faithful ucp map such that
\varphi_{r}\circ\pi=\varphi
. If
\varphi_{r}
is faithful then, for any unital C*-subalgebra
A_{0}\subset A
one has
C^{*}_{r}(\varphi|_{A_{0}})=\pi(A_{0})
. It is a direct consequence of the uniqueness of the reduced C*-algebra of a ucp map since
\varphi_{r}|_{A_{0}}
is still faithful hence GNS-faithful.

Let
B\subset A
be a unital C*-subalgebra. A
conditional expectation
from
A
to
B
is a ucp map
E\,:\,A\rightarrow B
such that
E(b)=b
for all
b\in B
. In particular, since
B
is in the multiplicative domain of the ucp map
E
, we have
E(xay)=xE(a)y
for all
x,y\in B
and
a\in A
.

We now quickly review some of the results of
[
FG20
]
. Let
A_{1},A_{2}
be unital C*-algebras with a common subalgebra
B\subset A_{k}
and write
A=A_{1}\underset{B}{*}A_{2}
the full amalgamated free product. For
k=1,2
, it is well known that the canonical maps
A_{k}\rightarrow A
are faithful so we will view
A_{k}\subset A
. Given two conditional expectations
E_{k}\,:\,A_{k}\rightarrow B
(not necessarily GNS faithful), an operator
a\in A
is called
reduced
(relatively to
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
) if it is of the form
a=a_{1}\dots a_{n}
with,
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and for
1\leq l\leq n
,
a_{l}\in\ker(E_{i_{l}})\subset A_{i_{l}}
and
i_{l}\neq i_{l+1}
. It is shown in
[
FG20
]
that there exist unique conditional expectations
E_{A_{k}}\,:\,A\rightarrow A_{k}
satisfying:

E_{A_{k}}(a)=0\text{ for }a=a_{1}\dots a_{n}\text{ reduced with }n\geq 2\text{ and }E_{A_{k}}|_{A_{\overline{k}}}=E_{\overline{k}},\text{where }\overline{k}\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{k\}.

It is also shown that there exists a unique conditional expectation
E_{B}\,:\,A\rightarrow B
such that
E_{B}(a)=0
for any reduced operator
a\in A
.

When both
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are GNS-faithful then the reduced C*-algebras of
E_{A_{1}}
,
E_{A_{2}}
and
E_{B}
are all canonically isomorphic to the Voiculescu’s reduced amalgamated free product denoted by
A_{r}:=(A_{1},E_{1})\underset{B}{*}(A_{2},E_{2})
. We will still denote by
E_{A_{k}}
and
E_{B}
the GNS-faithful conditional expectations
A_{r}\rightarrow A_{k}
and
A_{r}\rightarrow B
. An easy adaptation of the proof of the main result in
[
Dyk98
]
(see also
[
BO08
, Exercise 4.5.5]
), shows that if both
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are faithful then
E_{B}
is faithful on
A_{r}
. This implies that both
E_{A_{1}}
and
E_{A_{2}}
are faithful on
A_{r}
since we have
E_{B}=E_{k}\circ A_{k}
, for
k=1,2
.

Let now
M_{1},M_{2}
be two von Neumann algebras with a common subalgebra
N\subset M_{k}
and faithful normal conditional expectation
E_{k}\,:\,M_{k}\rightarrow N
. There exists a unique, up to a canonical isomorphism, von Neumann algebra
M:=M_{1}\underset{N}{*}M_{2}
generated by
M_{1}\cup M_{2}\subset M
, where the two copies of
N
are identified and with a faithful normal conditional expectation
E_{N}\,:\,M\rightarrow N
such that
E_{N}(x)=0
for any
x\in M
reduced (relative to
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
). Moreover,
M
comes equipped with two normal faithful conditional expectations
E_{M_{k}}\,:\,M\rightarrow M_{k}
such that
E_{M_{k}}(x)=0
for any
x
reduced of length at least
2
and
E_{M_{k}}|_{M_{\overline{k}}}=E_{\overline{k}}
, where
\overline{k}\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{k\}
. To specify the conditional expectations, we sometimes write
M=(M_{1},E_{1})\underset{N}{*}(M_{2},E_{2})
.

Suppose now that we have strongly continuous one parameter groups
\alpha_{k}\,:\,\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(M_{k})
for
k\in\{1,2\}
, and
\gamma\,:\,\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(N)
such that
\alpha^{(k)}_{t}|_{N}=\gamma_{t}
and
E_{k}\circ\alpha^{(k)}_{t}=\gamma_{t}\circ E_{1}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\{1,2\}
. By the universal property of the von Neumann algebraic free product, there exists, for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
, a unique
\tau_{t}\in{\rm Aut}(M)
such that
\tau_{t}|_{M_{k}}=\alpha^{(k)}_{t}
for
k\in\{1,2\}
. We sometimes write
\tau:=\alpha_{1}*\alpha_{2}
. Using the uniqueness, we see that
(t\mapsto\tau_{t})
is a group homomorphism. We will use the following well known Lemma and we include a proof for the convenience of the reader.

Lemma 2.2
.

Fix a faithful normal state (f.n.s.)
\mu\in N_{*}
and assume that
\mu\circ\gamma_{t}=\mu
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
\tau
is a strongly continuous one parameter group of
M
preserving
\mu\circ E_{N}
.

Proof.

Define the f.n.s.
\omega=\mu\circ E_{N}\in M_{*}
and note that, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
x\in M
,
\omega\circ\tau_{t}(x)=\omega(x)
. Indeed, the equality is clear when
x
is reduced and for
x\in N
it follows from the hypothesis
\mu\circ\gamma_{t}=\mu
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Define
X:=\{x\in M\,:\,(t\mapsto\tau_{t}(x))\text{ is strongly continuous}\}
and note that
X
is a vector subspace, stable by involution and contains
M_{1}\cup M_{2}
. Let
x,y\in X
and let us show that
xy\in X
. View
M\subset\mathcal{L}({\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega))
, where
\xi_{\omega}
is the cyclic vector in the GNS construction
{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)
of
\omega
. One has, for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau_{t}(xy)-\tau_{s}(xy)=\tau_{t}(x)(\tau_{t}(y)-\tau_{s}(y))+(\tau_{t}(x)-\tau_{s}(y))\tau_{s}(y)
. Hence, for all
\xi\in H
, and all sequence
(t_{n})_{n}
converging to
t
one has:

\|(\tau_{t_{n}}(xy)-\tau_{t}(xy))\xi\|\leq\|x\|\,\|(\tau_{t_{n}}(y)-\tau_{t}(y))\xi\|+\|(\tau_{t_{n}}(x)-\tau_{t}(x))\tau_{t}(y)\xi\|\rightarrow_{n}0

since both
x,y\in X
. It follows that
X
is a subalgebra of
M
containing both
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
. Hence, it suffices to show that
X
is strong* closed in
M
. Let
(x_{n})_{n}\in X
be a sequence converging strongly to
x\in M
(in particular,
(x_{n})
is norm bounded). It suffices to show that the set

Z:=\{\xi\in{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)\,:\,(t\mapsto\tau_{t}(x)\xi)\text{ is continuous }\}

is equal to
{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)
. Let
\xi=a\xi_{\omega}
, where
a\in M
is analytic with respect to the modular group
(\sigma_{t})_{t}
of
\omega
. Denoting by
J
the anti-unitary modular operator of
\omega
we have,

\displaystyle\|\tau_{t}(x_{n})\xi-\tau_{t}(x)\xi\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\tau_{t}(x_{n}-x)a\xi_{\omega}\|=\|\tau_{t}(x_{n}-x)J\sigma_{i/2}(a)^{*}J\xi_{\omega}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|J\sigma_{i/2}(a)^{*}J\tau_{t}(x_{n}-x)\xi_{\omega}\|\leq\|\sigma_{i/2}(a)\|\|\tau_{t}(x_{n}-x)\xi_{\omega}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{i/2}(a)\|\cdot\omega(\tau_{t}((x_{n}-x)^{*}(x_{n}-x)))^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{i/2}(a)\|\cdot\omega((x_{n}-x)^{*}(x_{n}-x))^{1/2}.

It follows that the sequence of continuous maps
(t\mapsto\sigma_{t}(x_{n})\xi)_{n}
converges uniformly on
\mathbb{R}
to the map
t\mapsto\sigma_{t}(x)\xi
. Hence,
a\xi_{\omega}\in Z
for all
a\in M
analytic with respect to
(\sigma_{t})_{t}
. Since this set is dense in
{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)
, it suffices to show that
Z
is norm-closed in
{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)
. Let
\xi_{n}\in Z
be a sequence converging to
\xi\in{\rm L}^{2}(M,\omega)
. Writing
\|\tau_{t}(x)\xi_{n}-\tau_{t}(x)\xi\|\leq\|x\|\|\xi_{n}-\xi\|
, we can argue as before to deduce that
\xi\in Z
. It concludes the proof.∎

Remark 2.3
.

Let
\mu\in N_{*}
be any f.n.s. and define
\varphi_{k}:=\mu\circ E_{k}\in(M_{k})_{*}
and
\varphi:=\mu\circ E_{N}\in M_{*}
. The following relation between the modular groups of
\varphi
,
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
is shown in
[
Ued99
]
:
\sigma^{\varphi}=\sigma^{\varphi_{1}}*\sigma^{\varphi_{2}}
.

Let
\omega
be a f.n.s. on a von Neumann algebra
M
with modular group
(\sigma_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, let
V_{\lambda}:=\{x\in M\,:\,\sigma_{t}(x)=\lambda^{it}x\text{ for all }t\in\mathbb{R}\}
and note that
V_{\lambda}V_{\mu}\subset V_{\lambda\mu}
and
V_{\lambda}^{*}=V_{\lambda^{-1}}
. In particular
V_{1}
, also known as the centralizer of
\omega
and denoted by
M^{\omega}
, is a unital von Neumann subalgebra of
M
and
V_{\omega}:={\rm Span}\left\{V_{\lambda}\,:\,\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\right\}
is a unital
*
-subalgebra of
M
.

The f.n.s.
\omega
is called
almost periodic
if
V_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}=M
. It is easy to check that any state on a finite dimensional von Neumann algebra is almost periodic and the tensor product as well as the amalgamated free product of almost periodic states is also almost periodic. Finally, we also note that if
G
is a compact quantum group and
M={\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
then the Haar state on
M
is almost periodic, with
V_{\omega}={\rm Pol}(G)
. The following well known remark will be useful.

Remark 2.4
.

If
\omega
is almost periodic on a diffuse von Neumann algebra
M
then its centralizer is diffuse. Indeed, if
p\in M^{\omega}
is a (non-zero) minimal projection, then the centralizer of the f.n.s. on
pMp
given
\varphi:=\omega(p)^{-1}\omega(p\cdot p)
is
M^{\varphi}=pM^{\omega}p=\mathbb{C}p
. It follows that, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
and all
x\in V^{\varphi}_{\lambda}
,
x^{*}x\in V^{\varphi}_{\lambda}V^{\varphi}_{\lambda^{-1}}\subset M^{\varphi}=\mathbb{C}p
. Hence,
V_{\varphi}\subset\mathbb{C}p
and, since
\omega
is almost periodic,
\varphi
is almost periodic on
pMp
so
pMp=\mathbb{C}p
and
p
is minimal in
M
.

2.2.
Compact quantum groups

For a CQG
G
, we denote by
C(G)
its
maximal
C*-algebra, which is the enveloping C*-algebra of the unital
*
-algebra
{\rm Pol}(G)
given by the linear span of coefficients of irreducible unitary representations of
G
. The collection of finite dimensional unitary representations is denoted by
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
and the set of equivalence classes of irreducible unitary representations is denoted by
{\rm Irr}(G)
. We will denote by
\varepsilon_{G}\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
the counit of
G
which satisfies (
{\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{G})(u)={\rm id}_{H}
for all finite dimensional unitary representations
u\in\mathcal{L}(H)\otimes C(G)
.

Let us recall below the modular ingredients of a CQG. Let
u\in\mathcal{L}(H_{u})\otimes C(G)
be a finite dimensional unitary representation (not necessarily irreducible). The contragredient representation
\overline{u}
is the unique, up to isomorphism, finite dimensional unitary representation such that there exists a pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
with
s_{u}\in\mathrm{Mor}(1,u\otimes\overline{u})
and
s_{\overline{u}}\in\mathrm{Mor}(1,\overline{u}\otimes u)
such that:

(s_{\overline{u}}^{*}\otimes{\rm id}_{H_{\overline{u}}})({\rm id}_{H_{\overline{u}}}\otimes s_{u})={\rm id}_{H_{\overline{u}}}\text{ and }(s_{u}^{*}\otimes{\rm id}_{H_{u}})({\rm id}_{H_{u}}\otimes s_{\overline{u}})={\rm id}_{H_{u}}.

We say that such a pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
solves the conjugate equation for
(u,\overline{u})
. Let
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
be a pair solving the conjugate equation for
(u,\overline{u})
and define the positive functionals
\varphi_{u},\psi_{u}
on the C*-algebra
{\rm End}(u)\subset\mathcal{L}(H_{u})
by
\varphi_{u}(T):=s_{\overline{u}}^{*}({\rm id}_{H_{\overline{u}}}\otimes T)s_{\overline{u}}
and
\psi_{u}(T):=s_{u}^{*}(T\otimes{\rm id}_{H_{\overline{u}}})s_{u}
. Note that
\psi_{u}={\rm Tr}(Q_{u}\cdot)
and
\varphi_{u}={\rm Tr}(Q_{\overline{u}}\cdot)
(where
{\rm Tr}
is the unique trace on
\mathcal{L}(H_{u})
such that
{\rm Tr}(1)={\rm dim}(H_{u})
), where
Q_{u}=J_{u}^{*}J_{u}\in\mathcal{L}(H_{u})
is positive invertible and
J_{u}\,:\,H_{u}\rightarrow H_{\overline{u}}
is the unique invertible antilinear map satisfying
\langle J_{u}\xi,\eta\rangle=\langle s_{u},\xi\otimes\eta\rangle
for all
\xi\in H_{u}
,
\eta\in H_{\overline{u}}
.
Q_{\overline{u}}=J_{\overline{u}}J_{\overline{u}}^{*}
is defined similarly, using
s_{\overline{u}}
instead of
s_{u}
. The pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
is called
standard
whenever
\varphi_{u}=\psi_{u}
and in the case of a standard pair one has
J_{\overline{u}}=J_{u}^{-1}
,
Q_{\overline{u}}=Q_{u}^{-1}
.

It is known that a standard pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
always exists and for any standard pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
one has
\|s_{\overline{u}}\|=\|s_{u}\|
and this number does not depend on the choice of a standard pair. We then define
the quantum dimension of
u
as
{\rm dim}_{q}(u):=\|s_{u}\|^{2}={\rm Tr}(Q_{u})={\rm Tr}(Q_{u}^{-1})=\|s_{\overline{u}}\|^{2}
for a standard pair
(s_{u},s_{\overline{u}})
. In what follows,
Q_{u}\in\mathcal{L}(H_{u})
will always denote the unique positive invertible operator in
\mathcal{L}(H_{u})
defined above. Such operators satisfy
SQ_{u}=Q_{v}S
for all
u,v\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)
and all
S\in\mathrm{Mor}(u,v)
.

We will always choose an orthonormal basis
(e^{u}_{i})_{i}
of
H_{u}
diagonalizing
Q_{u}
i.e.
Q_{u}e^{u}_{i}=Q_{u,i}e^{u}_{i}
(
Q_{u,i}>0
) then,
e^{\overline{u}}_{i}:=Q_{u,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}J_{u}e^{u}_{i}
is an orthonormal basis diagonalizing
Q_{\overline{u}}
and we have
Q_{\overline{u}}e^{\overline{u}}_{i}=Q^{\frac{1}{2}}_{u,i}e^{\overline{u}}_{i}
and
J_{\overline{u}}e^{\overline{u}}_{i}=Q_{u,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{u}_{i}
so that our notations are coherent with
Q_{\overline{u},i}:=Q_{u,i}^{-1}
. Note that, in this basis we have
s_{u}=\sum_{i}Q_{u,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{u}_{i}\otimes e^{\overline{u}}_{i}
(and
s_{\overline{u}}=\sum_{i}Q_{u,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\overline{u}}_{i}\otimes e^{u}_{i}
).

Let
h_{G}
be the Haar state on
C(G)
and
({\rm L}^{2}(G),\lambda_{G},\xi_{G})
its GNS construction. Let

C_{r}(G):=\lambda_{G}(C(G))\subset\mathcal{L}({\rm L}^{2}(G))

be the
reduced C*-algebra
of
G
. The surjective unital
*
-homomorphism
\lambda_{G}\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow C_{r}(G)
is called the
canonical surjection
. Recall that
G
is called
coamenable
whenever
\lambda_{G}
is injective. The
von Neumann algebra of

G
is denoted by
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G):=C_{r}(G)^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}({\rm L}^{2}(G))
. We will still denote by
h_{G}
the Haar state of
G
on the C*-algebra
C_{r}(G)
as well as on the von Neumann algebra
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
i.e.
h_{G}=\langle\xi_{G},\cdot\xi_{G}\rangle
when viewed as a state on
C_{r}(G)
or a normal state on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
. We recall that
h_{G}
is faithful on both
C_{r}(G)
and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
. The modular group
\sigma_{t}^{G}
of
h_{G}
and the scaling group
\tau_{t}^{G}
of
G
are given by
({\rm id}\otimes\sigma^{G}_{t})(u)=(Q_{u}^{it}\otimes 1)u(Q_{u}^{it}\otimes 1)
and
({\rm id}\otimes\tau^{G}_{t})(u)=(Q_{u}^{it}\otimes 1)u(Q_{u}^{-it}\otimes 1)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. It is well known that the scaling group of
G
is the unique one parameter group
(\tau_{t}^{G})_{t}
of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
such that
\Delta\circ\sigma_{t}^{G}=(\tau_{t}^{G}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{G})\circ\Delta
for all
u\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, the scaling group preserves that Haar state:
h_{G}\circ\tau_{t}^{G}=h_{G}
. The compact quantum group
G
is said to be of
Kac type
whenever
h_{G}
is a trace which happens if and only if
Q_{u}=1
for all
u\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)
.

We say that a compact quantum group
F
is a
dual quantum subgroup
of a compact quantum group
G
if there is a faithful *-morphism
C(F)\hookrightarrow C(G)
intertwining the comultiplications. We often see
C(F)
as a C*-subalgebra of
C(G)
. Recall the following proposition from
[
FT24
]
.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
G
and
F
be CQG. The following data are equivalent.

•

\iota\,:\,C(F)\rightarrow C(G)
is a faithful unital
*
-homomorphism intertwining the comultiplications.

•

\iota\,:\,{\rm Pol}(F)\rightarrow{\rm Pol}(G)
is a faithful unital
*
-homomorphism intertwining the comultiplications.

•

\iota\,:\,C_{r}(F)\rightarrow C_{r}(G)
is a faithful unital
*
-homomorphism intertwining the comultiplications.

If one of the following equivalent conditions is satisfied, we view
{\rm Pol}(F)\subset{\rm Pol}(G)
,
C(F)\subset C(G)
and
C_{r}(F)\subset C_{r}(G)
. Then, the unique linear map
E_{F}\,:\,{\rm Pol}(G)\rightarrow{\rm Pol}(F)
such that

({\rm id}\otimes E_{F})(u^{x})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}u^{x}&\text{if}&x\in{\rm Irr}(F),\\
0&\text{if}&x\in{\rm Irr}(G)\setminus{\rm Irr}(F).\end{array}\right.

has a unique ucp extension to a map
E_{F}\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow C(F)
which is a Haar-state-preserving conditional expectation onto the subalgebra
C(F)\subset C(G)
. Hence, there exists a unique conditional expectation
E_{F}^{r}\,:\,C_{r}(G)\rightarrow C_{r}(F)
such that
E_{F}^{r}\circ\lambda_{G}=\lambda_{F}\circ E_{F}
. Moreover,
E^{r}_{F}
is faithful and Haar-state-preserving and extends to a Haar-state preserving normal faithful conditional expectation
E_{F}^{\prime\prime}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
.

Remark 2.6
.

The following holds.

(1)

Note that, since
\lambda_{G}
is surjective,
E_{F}^{r}
is faithful and
E^{r}_{F}\circ\lambda_{G}=\lambda_{F}\circ E_{F}
,
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
is actually the reduced C*-algebra of the ucp map
\lambda_{F}\circ E_{F}
i.e.
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})=C^{*}_{r}(\lambda_{F}\circ E_{F})
.

(2)

If
a\in{\rm Pol}(G)
and
E_{F}(a)=0
then we can write
\Delta(a)
as a finite sum
\Delta(a)=\sum_{k}a_{k}\otimes b_{k}
with
a_{k},b_{k}\in{\rm Pol}(G)
and
E_{F}(a_{k})=0=E_{F}(b_{k})
for all
k
. Indeed, assuming that
a\neq 0
and since
a\in{\rm Pol}(G)
, there exists a finite subset
S\subset{\rm Irr}(G)
and non-zero
\lambda^{x}_{ij}\in\mathbb{C}
,
1\leq i,j\leq{\rm dim}(x)
such that
a=\sum_{x\in S,i,j}\lambda^{x}_{ij}u^{x}_{ij}
. Then,
\Delta(a)=\sum_{x\in S,i,j}\lambda^{x}_{i,j,s}u^{x}_{is}\otimes u^{x}_{sj}
and
E_{F}(a)=\sum_{x\in S\cap{\rm Irr}(F),i,j}\lambda^{x}_{ij}u^{x}_{ij}
. Since
E_{F}(a)=0
and coefficients of representations
u^{x}
for
x\in{\rm Irr}(G)
form a basis of
{\rm Pol}(G)
, we deduce that
S\subset{\rm Irr}(G)\setminus{\rm Irr}(F)
.

(3)

E_{F}
is the unique Haar state preserving conditional expectation, at the full, reduced, von Neumann or algebraic level. Indeed, if
E\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow C(F)
is a conditional expectation preserving the Haar states then there exists a unique conditional expectation
E^{r}\,:\,C_{r}(G)\rightarrow C_{r}(F)
such that
E^{r}\circ\lambda_{G}=\lambda_{F}\circ E
and
E^{r}
still preserves the Haar states. By faithfulness of the Haar states at the reduced level, we deduce that
E^{r}=E^{r}_{F}
. Indeed,
h_{F}((E^{r}_{F}(x)-E^{r}(x))b)=h_{F}(E_{F}^{r}(xb))-h_{F}(E^{r}(xb))=h_{G}(xb)-h_{G}(xb)=0
for all
b\in C_{r}(F)
and all
x\in C_{r}(G)
. Since
h_{F}
is faithful on
C_{r}(F)
it gives the result. It implies that
E=E_{F}
on
{\rm Pol}(G)
and so
E=E_{F}
on
C(G)
. For the reduced, von Neumann and algebraic level, the statement is clear since the Haar states are faithful in those cases.

2.3.
Actions on finite dimensional C*-algebras

A (right)
action
of a CQG
H
on a finite dimensional unital C*-algebra
B
is a unital
*
-homomorphism
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)
such that
({\rm id}_{B}\otimes\Delta_{H})\beta=(\beta\otimes{\rm id})\beta
and
({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{H})\beta={\rm id}_{B}
. In particular,
\beta
has to be injective. The action is called
ergodic
if the fixed point C*-subalgebra is trivial i.e.
B^{H}:=\{b\in B\,:\,\beta(b)=b\otimes 1\}=\mathbb{C}1_{B}
. Note that, by invariance of Haar measure, the map
({\rm id}_{B}\otimes h_{H})\beta
is a conditional expectation onto
B^{H}
.
The action is called
faithful
if
C(H)
is generated, as a C*-algebra, by
\{(\omega\otimes{\rm id})\beta(b)\,:\,b\in B,\,\omega\in B^{*}\}
. Given a state
\psi
on
B
, we say that the action
\beta
is
\psi
-
preserving
, or that
\psi
is a
\beta
-
invariant
state, if
(\psi\otimes{\rm id})\beta=\psi(\cdot)1
. Note that if
\beta
is ergodic then there exists a unique
\beta
-invariant state
\psi\in B^{*}
, given by
\psi(b)1=({\rm id}\otimes h_{H})\beta(b)
. In particular, since
h_{H}
is faithful on
{\rm Pol}(H)
and
\beta
is injective, if
\beta
is ergodic then its unique invariant state
\psi\in B^{*}
is faithful.

For the remainder of this paper,
\psi
will be a faithful state on the finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
. View
B
as a Hilbert space with scalar product
\langle a,b\rangle_{\psi}:=\psi(a^{*}b)
for all
a,b\in B
.
B
acts on the Hilbert space
B
by left multiplication: the map
L_{B}\,:\,B\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(B)
,
L_{B}(b)(a)=ba
is a faithful unital
*
-homomorphism.

Let
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(H)
be a
\psi
-preserving action of the CQG
H
on
B
. From the action
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(H)
we consider the unique operator
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)=\mathcal{K}_{C(H)}(B\otimes C(H))
satisfying
u(b\otimes a):=\beta(b)(1_{B}\otimes a)
for all
b\in B
,
a\in C(H)
. Let
m\,:\,B\otimes B\rightarrow B
and
\eta\,:\,\mathbb{C}\rightarrow B
be the multiplication and the unit of
B
respectively. The following simple lemma is well known
[
Ban99
]
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Given any action
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(H)
the associated unitary representation
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
satisfies:

•

u
is unitary representation of
H
on
B
.

•

m\in\mathrm{Mor}_{H}(u\otimes u,u)
.

•

\eta\in\mathrm{Mor}_{H}(1,u)
.

•

u(L_{B}(b)\otimes 1)u^{*}=(L_{B}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta(b))
for all
b\in B
.

•

The action
\beta
is faithful if and only if
C(H)
is generated by the coefficients of
u
.

•

The action
\beta
is ergodic if and only if
\mathrm{Mor}_{H}(1,u)=\mathbb{C}\eta
.

Remark 2.8
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
be the representation associated to a
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
then
u\simeq\overline{u}
. Indeed, it is easy to check that
s_{u}=s_{\overline{u}}:=m^{\star}\eta\in{\rm Mor}(1,u\otimes u)
is a pair solving the conjugate equation of
(u,u)
, where
m^{\star}\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes B
is the adjoint of
m
with respect to the
\psi
-scalar product on
B
.

Denote by
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
the quantum automorphism group of
(B,\psi)
. It is the universal CQG acting on
(B,\psi)
in the sense that there is a
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
and, if
G
is any CQG acting on
B
via
\alpha\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(G)
in a
\psi
-preserving way then, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\pi\,:\,C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))\rightarrow C(G)
such that
\pi
intertwines the comultiplications and the actions i.e.
({\rm id}\otimes\pi)\beta=\alpha
. The uniqueness, up to a canonical isomorphism, of
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
is clear, and the action
\beta
is faithful. The unitary
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
associated with
\beta
is called the
fundamental representation
of
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
. The existence of
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
is also clear (see
[
Ban99
,
Ban02
]
), it suffices to define
C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
as the universal unital C*-algebra generated by coefficients of
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
with relations:

•

u
is unitary.

•

m\in\mathrm{Mor}(u\otimes u,u)
.

•

\eta\in\mathrm{Mor}(1,u)
.

The comultiplication
\Delta_{B}\,:\,C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))\rightarrow C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))\otimes C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
is then defined, by the universal property of
C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
, as the unique unital
*
-homomorphism such that
u
is a representation i.e.
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{B})(u)=u_{12}u_{13}
. We sometimes write
\beta_{B}\,:\,{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
for the universal
\psi
-preserving action of
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
on
B
.

Write
B=\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K}M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\psi=\sum_{\kappa}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}\cdot)
, where
Q_{\kappa}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
is a positive definite matrix with
\sum_{\kappa}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa})=1
. Diagonalising
Q_{\kappa}
, we find matrix units
(e^{\kappa}_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}}
in
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
such that
Q_{\kappa}e^{\kappa}_{ij}=\lambda_{\kappa,i}e^{\kappa}_{ij}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}
and all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. Since
\psi((e^{\kappa}_{ij})^{*}e^{\zeta}_{kl})=\delta_{\kappa,\zeta}\delta_{ik}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}e^{\kappa}_{jl})=\delta_{\kappa,\zeta}\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}\lambda_{\kappa,j}
, we deduce that
(b^{\kappa}_{ij})_{\kappa,i,j}
is an orthonormal basis of the Hilbert space
B
, where
b^{\kappa}_{ij}:=\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa,j}e^{\kappa}_{ij}
. Note that
b^{\kappa}_{ip}b^{\zeta}_{qj}=\delta_{\kappa\zeta}\delta_{pq}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa,p}b^{\kappa}_{ij}
and
(b^{\kappa}_{ij})^{*}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{\kappa}_{ji}
.

Remark 2.9
.

The positive operator
mm^{\star}
is actually diagonal in the orthonormal basis
(b^{\kappa}_{rs})
of
B
. Indeed, a direct computation shows that
mm^{\star}(b^{\kappa}_{rs})={\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})b^{\kappa}_{rs}
for all
\kappa,r,s
. Writing
\delta_{1},\dots\delta_{n}>0
the pairwise distinct values taken by
{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})
we see that
{\rm Sp}(mm^{\star})=\{\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}\}
and, as C*-algebras,
B=\bigoplus_{i}B_{i}
, where

B_{i}:={\rm Span}\{b^{\kappa}_{rs}\,:\,1\leq\kappa\leq K\text{ such that }{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})=\delta_{i},\,1\leq r,s\leq N_{\kappa}\}=\bigoplus_{\kappa,{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})=\delta_{i}}M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})

is the
\delta_{i}
-eigenspace of
mm^{\star}
.

Recall that a faithful state
\psi\in B^{*}
is called a
\delta
-form
(
\delta>0
) if
mm^{\star}=\delta{\rm id}_{B}
. Note that our convention is a little bit different from the usual one
[
Ban99
,
Ban02
,
Bra13
]
.

Remark 2.10
.

The following holds.

(1)

\psi
is a
\delta
-form
\Leftrightarrow

\kappa\mapsto{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})
is constant and in that case
\delta={\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})

\forall\kappa
. In general, writing
B=\bigoplus B_{i}
, each
\psi_{i}:=\frac{1}{\psi(1_{B_{i}})}\psi|_{B_{i}}
is a
\delta_{i}
-form on
B_{i}
.

It is a direct consequence of Remark
2.9
.

(2)

\beta_{B}\,:\,{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)\curvearrowright B
is always faithful and
\beta_{B}
is ergodic if and only if
\psi
is a
\delta
-form.

Indeed, if
\psi
is a
\delta
-form then it is known
[
Ban99
,
Ban02
,
Bra13
]
that
{\mathrm{Mor}}(1,u)=\mathbb{C}\eta
so
\beta_{B}
is ergodic. If
\psi
is not a
\delta
-form then, by the spectral decomposition from Remark
2.9
,
n\geq 2
and, for
1\leq i\neq j\leq n
, one has
\delta_{i}\neq\delta_{j}
so that
p_{i}\eta
and
p_{j}\eta\in B
are linearly independent since
1_{B}=\sum_{\kappa,r}\lambda_{\kappa}^{-1/2}b^{\kappa}_{rr}
which gives
p_{k}(1_{B})=\sum_{\kappa,\,{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})=\delta_{k}}\lambda_{\kappa}^{-1/2}b^{\kappa}_{rr}
for all
1\leq k\leq n
. Note that, since
mm^{\star}
is in the C*-algebra
{\rm End}(u)\subset\mathcal{L}(B)
, we also have
p_{k}\in{\rm End}(u)
for all
k
. Hence,
p_{i}\eta,p_{j}\eta\in{\rm Mor}(1,u)
which implies that
\beta_{B}
is not ergodic.

(3)

For any
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
, if
\beta
is ergodic then
\psi
is a
\delta
-form.

Indeed, assume that
\psi
is not a
\delta
-form and write
\pi\,:\,C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))\rightarrow C(H)
the canonical map intertwining the comultiplications and such that
({\rm id}\otimes\pi)\beta_{B}=\beta
. In particular,
B^{{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)}\subseteq B^{H}
. By
(2)
,
\beta_{B}
is not ergodic so
{\rm dim}(B^{{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)})\geq 2
which implies that
\beta
is not ergodic either.

Given any
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(H)
with associated representation
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
we write:

u=\sum_{\kappa,\zeta,i,j,k,l}b^{\kappa}_{ij}\circ(b^{\zeta}_{kl})^{\star}\otimes u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta},

where we view
b\in B
as an operator in
\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C},B)
and
b^{\star}\in\mathcal{L}(B,\mathbb{C})
is the adjoint operator, the action
\beta
is then given by
\beta(b^{\zeta}_{kl})=\sum_{i,j,\kappa}b^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta}
. The relations
u
unitary,
m\in\mathrm{Mor}(u\otimes u,u)
and
\eta\in\mathrm{Mor}(1,u)
are equivalent to the following relations on the coefficients of
u
.

•

\sum_{r=1}^{N_{\gamma}}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{ir,\gamma}_{kp,\kappa}u^{rj,\gamma}_{ql,\zeta}=\delta_{\kappa\zeta}\delta_{pq}\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{ij,\gamma}_{kl,\kappa}
and
\sum_{r=1}^{N_{\gamma}}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{ip,\kappa}_{kr,\gamma}u^{qj,\zeta}_{rl,\gamma}=\delta_{\kappa\zeta}\delta_{pq}\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}
.

•

\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{ij,\kappa}_{rr,\gamma}=\delta_{ij}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{rr,\gamma}_{kl,\zeta}=\delta_{kl}\lambda_{\zeta,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

•

\left(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta}\right)^{*}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\zeta,k}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}{\lambda_{\zeta,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{ji,\kappa}_{lk,\zeta}
.

And the comultiplication is given on coefficients by
\Delta(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta})=\sum_{r,s,\gamma}u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\gamma}\otimes u^{rs,\gamma}_{kl,\zeta}
. In particular
C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
is the universal unital C*-algebra generated by elements
u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta}\in C({\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi))
for
1\leq\kappa,\zeta\leq K
,
1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}
,
1\leq k,l\leq N_{\zeta}
satisfying the above relations.

Proposition 2.11
.

Let
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
be an ergodic action on the finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
with unique invariant state
\psi\in B^{*}
and associated representation
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
. The following holds.

(1)

h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma})=\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}(\lambda_{\kappa,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

(2)

Q_{u}
is the positive modular operator
\nabla_{\psi}
of
\psi
. In particular,

(a)

{\rm dim}_{q}(u)=\delta
.

(b)

\sigma^{h}_{t}(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma})=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}
and

({\rm id}\otimes\sigma^{h}_{t})\circ\beta=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\circ\beta\circ\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
.

(c)

\tau^{h}_{t}(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma})=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,l}\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}
and

({\rm id}\otimes\tau^{h}_{t})\circ\beta=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\circ\beta\circ\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}
.

Proof.

(1)
. Since
({\rm id}\otimes h)(u)
is the orthogonal projection onto
{\rm Mor}(1,u)=\mathbb{C}\eta
and
\|\eta\|^{2}=\psi(1)=1
one has
({\rm id}\otimes h)(u)=\eta\eta^{\star}
. Writing
\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{\kappa}_{ii}
we have

\eta\eta^{\star}=\sum_{\kappa,\gamma,i,k}b^{\kappa}_{ii}\circ(b^{\gamma}_{kk})^{\star}(\lambda_{\kappa,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}=({\rm id}\otimes h)(u).

(2)
. Recall from Remark
2.8
that the pair
(m^{\star}\eta,m^{\star}\eta)
solves the conjugate equation for
(u,u)
. Let us show, under the hypothesis of ergodicity, that the pair
(m^{\star}\eta,m^{\star}\eta)
is standard. It suffices to show that, for all
T\in{\rm End}(u)
,
\varphi_{u}(T)=\psi_{u}(T)
, where
\varphi_{u}(T):=\eta^{\star}m({\rm id}_{B}\otimes T)m^{\star}\eta
and
\psi_{u}(T):=\eta^{\star}m(T\otimes{\rm id}_{B})m^{\star}\eta
. Note that both
m(T\otimes{\rm id}_{B})m^{\star}\eta
and
m({\rm id}_{B}\otimes T)m^{\star}\eta
are in
\mathrm{Mor}(1,u)
. Since the action is ergodic, there exist
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{C}
such that
m(T\otimes{\rm id}_{B})m^{\star}\eta=\lambda\eta
and
m({\rm id}_{B}\otimes T)m^{\star}\eta=\mu\eta
. It suffices to show that
\lambda=\mu
. Write, in the basis of matrix units
(e^{\kappa}_{ij})_{\kappa,i,j}
in
\mathcal{L}(B)
such that
Q_{\kappa}e^{\kappa}_{ij}=\lambda_{\kappa,i}e^{\kappa}_{ij}
,
T(e^{\kappa}_{ij})=\sum_{\zeta,k,l}T^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta}e^{\zeta}_{kl}
, where
T^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\zeta}\in\mathbb{C}
and note that
m^{\star}\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-1}e^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes e^{\kappa}_{ji}
. A direct computation gives:

m(T\otimes{\rm id}_{B})m^{\star}\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i,j}\left(\sum_{k}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{jk,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa}\right)e^{\kappa}_{ij}\quad\text{and}\quad m({\rm id}_{B}\otimes T)m^{\star}\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i,j}\left(\sum_{k}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{ki,\kappa}_{kj,\kappa}\right)e^{\kappa}_{ij}.

Since
m(T\otimes{\rm id}_{B})m^{\star}\eta=\lambda\sum_{\kappa,i}e^{\kappa}_{ii}
we find that the map
\left[(\kappa,i)\mapsto\sum_{k}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{ik,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa}\right]
is constant, equals to
\lambda
. Similarly, the map
\left[(\kappa,i)\mapsto\sum_{k}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{ki,\kappa}_{ki,\kappa}\right]
is constant, equals to
\mu
. Hence, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
and all
1\leq k\leq N_{\kappa}
, one has
\mu=\lambda_{\kappa,1}^{-1}T^{1k,\kappa}_{1k,\kappa}+\sum_{i=2}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1}T^{ik,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa}
which implies that
T^{1k,\kappa}_{1k,\kappa}=\lambda_{\kappa,1}(\mu-\sum_{i=2}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1}T^{ik,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa})
. It follows that:

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{1k,\kappa}_{1k,\kappa}=\lambda_{\kappa,1}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}(\mu-\sum_{i=2}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1}T^{ik,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa})=\lambda_{\kappa,1}\delta\mu-\lambda_{\kappa,1}\sum_{i=2}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,k}^{-1}T^{ik,\kappa}_{ik,\kappa}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\kappa,1}\delta\mu-\lambda_{\kappa,1}\sum_{i=2}^{N_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1}\lambda=\lambda_{\kappa,1}\delta\mu-\lambda_{\kappa,1}(\delta-\lambda_{\kappa,1}^{-1})\lambda=\lambda_{\kappa,1}\delta(\mu-\lambda)+\lambda.

We deduce that
\lambda=\mu
so
(m^{\star}\eta,m^{\star}\eta)
is standard. It follows that
Q_{u}=J_{u}^{*}J_{u}
, where
\langle J_{u}(a),b\rangle=\langle m^{\star}\eta,a\otimes b\rangle=\langle 1_{B},ab\rangle=\psi(ab)=\langle a^{*},b\rangle
for all
a,b\in B
. Hence,
J_{u}(a)=a^{*}=S_{\psi}(a)
, the modular operator of
\psi
. It follows, by definition, that
Q_{u}=S_{\psi}^{*}S_{\psi}=\nabla_{\psi}
. Write
S_{\psi}=J_{\psi}\nabla^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\psi}
. A direct computation shows that
J_{\psi}
is the unique anti-unitary on
B
such that
J_{\psi}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})=b^{\gamma}_{lk}
and
\nabla_{\psi}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})=\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}b^{\gamma}_{kl}
. Hence,
{\rm dim}_{q}(u)={\rm Tr}(\nabla_{\psi})=\sum_{\kappa}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}){\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})=\delta\sum_{\kappa}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa})=\delta
. Since
({\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h})(u)=(\nabla_{\psi}^{it}\otimes 1)u(\nabla_{\psi}^{it}\otimes 1)
, it follows that
({\rm id}\otimes\sigma^{h}_{t})(u)=\sum\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}b^{\kappa}_{ij}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})^{\star}\otimes u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}
which proves the first part of
(b)
. Moreover, since
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}(b)=\nabla_{\psi}^{it}b\nabla_{\psi}^{-it}
for all
b\in B
we find
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{it}b^{\gamma}_{kl}
from which we can easily check the last part of
(b)
.
(c)
is proven the same way using the relation:

({\rm id}\otimes\tau_{t}^{h})(u)=(\nabla_{\psi}^{it}\otimes 1)u(\nabla_{\psi}^{-it}\otimes 1).\qed

Remark 2.12
.

Let
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
be any ergodic
\psi
-preserving action of the CQG
H
on the finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
. It follows from statement
(b)
of Proposition
2.11
that if
H
is Kac then
\psi
is tracial. Moreover, if
\beta
is a faithful action and
\psi
is tracial then
H
is Kac.

For the remainder of this section, we fix an ergodic action
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
on the finite dimensional C*- algebra
B
with unique invariant state
\psi\in B^{*}
and associated representation
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
.

For
1\leq\kappa\leq K
consider the unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\chi_{\kappa}\,:\,B\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
such that
\chi_{\kappa}(b^{\zeta}_{ij})=\delta_{\kappa\zeta}b^{\kappa}_{ij}

and define the unital
*
-homomorphism

\beta_{\kappa}:=(\chi_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)

so that
\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})=\sum_{i,j}b^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes u^{ij,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}
. We also define the state
\psi_{\kappa}:=\frac{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}\cdot)}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa})}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
. Note that
\sigma_{t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\circ\chi_{\kappa}=\chi_{\kappa}\circ\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Note also that for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
the fact that
\psi
is a
\delta
-form implies that
{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})=\delta
.

Given a faithful state
\omega\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
,
\omega={\rm Tr}(Q\cdot)
where
Q\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
,
Q>0
of trace
1
, we define its
inverse state

\omega^{-1}:=\frac{{\rm Tr}(Q^{-1}\cdot)}{{\rm Tr}(Q^{-1})}\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
. Note that
\omega^{-1}
is faithful and
\sigma_{t}^{\omega^{-1}}=\sigma_{-t}^{\omega}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 2.13
.

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, the following holds.

(1)

({\rm id}\otimes h)\beta_{\kappa}=\psi(\cdot)1_{\kappa}
. In particular,
\beta_{\kappa}
is a faithful homomorphism.

(2)

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h})\beta_{\kappa}=\beta_{\kappa}\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
and
(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau_{t}^{h})\beta_{\kappa}=\beta_{\kappa}\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}
.

(3)

There exists a unique ucp map
E_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\rightarrow B
such that

\psi\circ E_{\kappa}=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h\quad\text{and}\quad E_{\kappa}\circ\beta_{\kappa}={\rm id}_{B}.

Moreover,
E_{\kappa}
is normal, faithful and, for all
1\leq r,s\leq N_{\kappa}
and all
a\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
,

E_{\kappa}(b^{\kappa}_{rs}\otimes a)=\frac{1}{\delta\lambda^{2}_{\kappa,s}}\sum_{\gamma,k,l}h\left(\left(u^{rs,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}\right)^{*}a\right)b^{\gamma}_{kl}.

In particular,
E_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{rs}\otimes 1)=\frac{\delta_{rs}}{\delta\lambda_{\kappa,r}}1_{B}
.

Proof.

(1)
. The unique
\beta
-invariant state
\psi
is defined by
({\rm id}\otimes h)\beta=\psi(\cdot)1
. Applying
\chi_{\kappa}
gives
(1)
. Since
\psi
is faithful, the maps
\beta_{\kappa}
also are.

(2)
. Follows from Assertion
(2.b)
and
(2.c)
in Proposition
2.11
.

(3)
. Since
\sigma_{t}^{\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}
it follows from
(2)
that the subalgebra
\beta_{\kappa}(B)\subset M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is globally invariant under the modular group of
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h
. By Takesaki’s Theorem there exists a unique
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h
-preserving conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\rightarrow\beta_{\kappa}(B)
, which is normal and faithful. Then,
E_{\kappa}:=\beta_{\kappa}^{-1}\circ\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}
satisfies
E_{\kappa}\circ\beta_{\kappa}={\rm id}_{B}
and, using
(1)
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}
is state preserving, we deduce that
\psi\circ E_{\kappa}=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h
. From this property and since
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h
is faithful, we deduce that
E_{\kappa}
is faithful. By
(1)
,
(\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl}))_{\gamma,k,l}
is a
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h
-orthonormal basis of the finite dimensional subalgebra
\beta_{\kappa}(B)
which easily implies that

\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(X)=\sum_{\gamma,k,l}(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h)(\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})^{*}X)\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})\text{ for all }X\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H).

Using the formulas
(b^{\gamma}_{kl})^{*}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{\gamma}_{lk}
and
b^{\kappa}_{ip}b^{\kappa}_{qj}=\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{pq}b^{\kappa}_{ij}
we get:

\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})^{*}(b^{\kappa}_{rs}\otimes a)=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i}b^{\kappa}_{is}\otimes u^{ir,\kappa}_{lk,\gamma}a.

Since
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(b^{\kappa}_{is})={\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda_{\kappa,s}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\delta_{is}
and
\left(u^{rs,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}\right)^{*}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,s}\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\kappa,r}\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{sr,\kappa}_{lk,\gamma}
we deduce that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(b^{\kappa}_{rs}\otimes a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma,k,l}(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h)(\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})^{*}(b^{\kappa}_{rs}\otimes a))\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,s}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{\gamma,k,l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}h(u^{sr,\kappa}_{lk,\gamma}a)\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl})

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda_{\kappa,s}^{-2}\sum_{\gamma,k,l}h\left(\left(u^{rs,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}\right)^{*}a\right)\beta_{\kappa}(b^{\gamma}_{kl}).\qed

Remark 2.14
.

We will use the same symbol to denote the restriction

E_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(H)\rightarrow B

which is still ucp and faithful, as well as
E_{\kappa}:=E_{\kappa}\circ({\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{H})\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\rightarrow B
, still ucp but not even GNS-faithful if
H
is not coamenable. Those maps satisfy the same algebraic properties as the initial map.

We will need the following Lemma.

Lemma 2.15
.

The following holds.

(1)

For all
x\in{\rm Pol}(H)
,
1\leq\kappa,\zeta,\gamma\leq K
,
1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}
,
1\leq k,l\leq N_{\gamma}
,
1\leq r,p\leq N_{\zeta}
,

\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{ql,\gamma}x)=\sum_{s,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}x_{(1)})u^{ps,\zeta}_{kl,\gamma}x_{(2)}

.

(2)

For all
X=\sum_{ij}b^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes x_{ij}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)
if
E_{\kappa}(X)=0
then

\sum_{i,j,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-1/2}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-3/2}h(u^{ji,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}(x_{ij})_{(1)})(x_{ij})_{(2)}=0,\quad\text{for all }\gamma,k,l.

Proof.

(1)
. Fix
x\in{\rm Pol}(H)
,
1\leq\kappa,\zeta,\gamma\leq K
,
1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}
,
1\leq k,l\leq N_{\gamma}
and
1\leq p,r\leq N_{\zeta}
and define
Z:=\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}(1\otimes u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma})\Delta(u^{ij,\kappa}_{ql,\gamma}x)
. Using the relation

\Delta(u^{ij,\kappa}_{ql,\gamma}x)=\sum_{\zeta^{\prime},r^{\prime},s}u^{ij,\kappa}_{r^{\prime}s,\zeta^{\prime}}x_{(1)}\otimes u^{r^{\prime}s,\zeta^{\prime}}_{ql,\gamma}x_{(2)},\,\text{we get:}

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\zeta^{\prime},r^{\prime},s}u^{ij,\kappa}_{r^{\prime}s,\zeta^{\prime}}x_{(1)}\otimes\left(\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}u^{r^{\prime}s,\zeta^{\prime}}_{ql,\gamma}x_{(2)}\right)=\sum_{s}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}x_{(1)}\otimes u^{ps,\zeta}_{kl,\gamma}x_{(2)}

Hence, using invariance of the Haar state, and the previous equation we get:

\displaystyle(h\otimes{\rm id})(Z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}(h\otimes{\rm id})\Delta(u^{ij,\kappa}_{ql,\gamma}x)=\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{ql,\gamma}x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}x_{(1)})u^{ps,\zeta}_{kl,\gamma}x_{(2)}.

(2)
. Using the formula in Proposition
2.13
, we get:

0=E_{\kappa}(X)=\delta^{-1}\sum_{\gamma,k,l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,l}}{\lambda_{\gamma,k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}h(u^{ji,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}x_{ij})\right)b^{\gamma}_{lk}.

Hence, for all
\gamma,k,l
we have
\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}h(u^{ji,\kappa}_{kl,\gamma}x_{ij})=0
. Using
(1)
we find, for all
\gamma,\zeta,k,p
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}\left(\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}h(u^{ji,\kappa}_{qk,\gamma}x_{ij})\right)=\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\sum_{q}\lambda_{\gamma,q}^{-1/2}u^{pr,\zeta}_{kq,\gamma}h(u^{ji,\kappa}_{qk,\gamma}x_{ij})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\sum_{s,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}(x_{ij})_{(1)})u^{ps,\zeta}_{kk,\gamma}(x_{ij})_{(2)}\right).

Multiplying by
\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{1/2}
and summing over
k,\gamma
we get:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\sum_{s,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}(x_{ij})_{(1)})u^{ps,\zeta}_{kk,\gamma}(x_{ij})_{(2)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\sum_{s,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\zeta,r}^{-1/2}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rs,\zeta}(x_{ij})_{(1)})\left(\sum_{k}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{1/2}u^{ps,\zeta}_{kk,\gamma}\right)(x_{ij})_{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda_{\zeta,p}}{\lambda_{\zeta,r}}\right)^{1/2}\sum_{i,j,(1),(2)}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j}^{-\frac{3}{2}}h(u^{ij,\kappa}_{rp,\zeta}(x_{ij})_{(1)})(x_{ij})_{(2)}.

This concludes the proof.∎

Definition 2.16
.

A
\psi
-preserving action
\beta\,:\,B\rightarrow B\otimes C(H)
with associated unitary
u\in\mathcal{L}(B)\otimes C(H)
is called
2
-
ergodic
if
{\rm dim}({\rm Mor}(u,u))=2
.

Remark 2.17
.

Note that if
\beta
is
2
-ergodic then
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 2
. Moreover, the following holds.

(1)

\beta
is
2
-ergodic if and only if
\mathrm{Mor}(u,u)=\mathbb{C}\eta\eta^{\star}\oplus\mathbb{C}({\rm id}_{B}-\eta\eta^{\star})
. Indeed, the two vectors
\eta\eta^{\star},{\rm id}_{B}-\eta\eta^{\star}\in\mathrm{Mor}(u,u)
are linearly independent whenever
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 2
.

(2)

As in
(1)
, we see that
\beta
is
2
-ergodic if and only if
\mathrm{Mor}(1,u\otimes u)=\mathbb{C}\eta\otimes\eta\oplus\mathbb{C}m^{\star}\eta
.

(3)

A
2
-ergodic action
\beta
is ergodic. Indeed, if
{\rm dim}({\rm Mor}(u,u))=2
then
u
is isomorphic to the sum of two non equivalent irreducible representations which implies that
{\rm dim}(1,u)\leq 1
. Since
\eta\in{\rm Mor}(1,u)
,
\beta
is ergodic.

Example 2.18
.

The following holds.

(1)

The canonical
\psi
-preserving action of
{\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
on
B
(
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 2
) is
2
-ergodic if and only if
\psi
is a
\delta
-form, in particular the action of
S_{N}^{+}
on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
for any
N\geq 2
is
2
-ergodic. Similarly, the action of
S_{N}
on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
is also
2
-ergodic for any
N\geq 2
.

(2)

For a finite group
\Lambda
, the action
\widehat{\Lambda}\curvearrowright C^{*}(\Lambda)
given by the comultiplication is ergodic and
\tau_{\Lambda}
-preserving, where
\tau_{\Lambda}\in C^{*}(\Lambda)^{*}
is the canonical (faithful) trace. This action is not
2
-ergodic whenever
|\Lambda|\neq 2
since the corresponding representation
u
is such that
{\rm dim}(\mathrm{Mor}(\varepsilon,u\otimes u))=|\Lambda|
.

In the next Lemma we show how the
2
-ergodic assumption provide a computation of the Haar state of a product of two coefficients of the representation
u
.

Lemma 2.19
.

If
\beta
is
2
-ergodic then the following holds.

\displaystyle h(u^{ij,\alpha}_{kl,\gamma}u^{rs,\kappa}_{vw,\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\delta}{\delta-1}\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}\delta_{rs}\delta_{vw}(\lambda_{\alpha,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,r}\lambda_{\zeta,v})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\delta_{\alpha\kappa}\delta_{jr}\delta_{is}\delta_{\gamma\zeta}\delta_{kw}\delta_{lv}}{\delta-1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\alpha,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k}}{\lambda_{\alpha,j}\lambda_{\gamma,l}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\delta_{ij}\delta_{rs}\delta_{\gamma\zeta}\delta_{kw}\delta_{lv}\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,l}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\delta_{\alpha\kappa}\delta_{is}\delta_{jr}\delta_{kl}\delta_{vw}\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\alpha,j}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\zeta,v}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

The action
\beta
being
2
-ergodic, it is ergodic so its unique invariant state
\psi
is a
\delta
-form. Moreover,
({\rm id}\otimes h)(u\otimes u)
is the orthogonal projection onto
\mathrm{Mor}(1,u\otimes u)=\mathbb{C}\eta\otimes\eta\oplus\mathbb{C}m^{\star}\eta
. Write
\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{\kappa}_{ii}
,
m^{\star}\eta=\sum_{\kappa,i,t}\lambda_{\kappa,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\kappa}_{it}\otimes b^{\kappa}_{ti}
, and therefore
\|m^{\star}\eta\|^{2}=\sum_{\kappa}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1}){\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa})=\sum_{\kappa}\delta{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa})=\delta
, because
\psi
is a
\delta
-form.
Since
(\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta^{\star})m^{\star}\eta=1
we have
\|m^{\star}\eta-\eta\otimes\eta\|^{2}=\|m^{\star}\eta\|^{2}-1=:\delta-1
. The orthogonal projection on the space is then the orthogonal projection that we obtain taking
(\eta\otimes\eta,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta-1}}(m^{\star}\eta-\eta\otimes\eta))
as an orthogonal base. The projection is therefore given by
p=\eta\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta\eta^{\star}+\frac{1}{\delta-1}(m^{\star}\eta-\eta\otimes\eta)(m^{\star}\eta-\eta\otimes\eta)^{\star}
. Note that:

p=\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\delta-1}\right)(\eta\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta\eta^{\star})+\frac{1}{\delta-1}m^{\star}\eta\eta^{\star}m-\frac{1}{\delta-1}(\eta\otimes\eta)\eta^{\star}m-\frac{1}{\delta-1}m^{\star}\eta(\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta^{\star}).

We have:

\displaystyle\eta\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta\eta^{\star}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,i\\
\gamma,k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa,r\\
\zeta,v\end{subarray}}(b_{ii}^{\alpha}\otimes b_{rr}^{\kappa})\circ(b_{kk}^{\gamma}\otimes b_{vv}^{\zeta})^{\star}(\lambda_{\alpha,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,r}\lambda_{\zeta,v})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle m^{\star}\eta\eta^{\star}m=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,i,x\\
\gamma,k,y\end{subarray}}(b_{ix}^{\alpha}\otimes b_{xi}^{\alpha})\circ(b_{ky}^{\gamma}\otimes b_{yk}^{\gamma})^{\star}(\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\alpha,x}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,y}^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle(\eta\otimes\eta)\eta^{\star}m=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,i\\
\kappa,r\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma,k,t\end{subarray}}(b_{ii}^{\alpha}\otimes b_{rr}^{\kappa})\circ(b_{kt}^{\gamma}\otimes b_{tk}^{\gamma})^{\star}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle m^{\star}\eta(\eta^{\star}\otimes\eta^{\star})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,i,t\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma,k\\
\zeta,v\end{subarray}}(b_{it}^{\alpha}\otimes b_{ti}^{\alpha})\circ(b_{kk}^{\gamma}\otimes b_{vv}^{\zeta})^{\star}\lambda_{\alpha,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\zeta,v}^{\frac{1}{2}}

We deduce that the coefficient associated to
(b_{ij}^{\alpha}\otimes b_{rs}^{\kappa})\circ(b_{kl}^{\gamma}\otimes b_{vw}^{\zeta})^{\star}
is:

\displaystyle\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\delta-1}\right)\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}\delta_{rs}\delta_{vw}(\lambda_{\alpha,i}\lambda_{\gamma,k}\lambda_{\kappa,r}\lambda_{\zeta,v})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\delta_{\alpha\kappa}\delta_{jr}\delta_{is}\delta_{\gamma\zeta}\delta_{kw}\delta_{lv}\frac{1}{\delta-1}(\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\alpha,j}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,l}^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle-\delta_{ij}\delta_{rs}\delta_{\gamma\zeta}\delta_{kw}\delta_{lv}\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,l}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\delta_{\alpha\kappa}\delta_{is}\delta_{jr}\delta_{kl}\delta_{vw}\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\alpha,j}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\alpha,i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\gamma,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\zeta,v}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This coefficient is equal to
h(u^{ij,\alpha}_{kl,\gamma}u^{rs,\kappa}_{vw,\zeta}).

∎

We record the following Lemma for later use.

Lemma 2.20
.

If
\beta
is
2
-ergodic,
\kappa
is such that
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
then, for any unital C*-algebra
C
,

(1)

If
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
u\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes C)
are such that
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma^{h}_{t}\otimes{\rm id}_{C}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))
then
u\in\mathbb{C}1\otimes\mathcal{Z}(C)
and
\sigma_{t}^{h}={\rm id}
.

(2)

\{u\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes C)\,:\,{\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))={\rm id}\}\subset\mathbb{C}1_{B}\otimes\mathcal{Z}(C)
.

(3)

If
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
u\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes C)
are such that
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau^{H}_{t}\otimes{\rm id}_{C}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))
then
u\in\mathbb{C}1\otimes\mathcal{Z}(C)
and
\tau^{H}_{t}={\rm id}
.

Proof.

(1)
. Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
u\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes C)
be such that
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma^{h}_{t}\otimes{\rm id}_{C}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))
. Recall that
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}={\rm Ad}(b_{t})
, where
b_{t}=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it}e^{\gamma}_{rr}=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it+1/2}b^{\gamma}_{rr}\in B
is unitary and
b_{t}^{*}=b_{-t}
. Then, for all
b\in B
and
c\in C
,

\displaystyle(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma^{h}_{t}\otimes{\rm id}_{C})(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(b\otimes c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{\kappa}(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}(b))\otimes c=\beta_{\kappa}(b_{t}bb_{-t})\otimes c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})((b_{t}\otimes 1)(b\otimes c)(b_{-t}\otimes 1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u)(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(b\otimes c)(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u(b\otimes c)u^{*}).

Since
\beta_{\kappa}
is faithful, we deduce that
(b_{t}\otimes 1)(b\otimes c)(b_{-t}\otimes 1)=u(b\otimes c)u^{*}
for all
b\in B
,
c\in C
. Hence,
v:=(b_{-t}\otimes 1)u\in\mathcal{Z}(B\otimes C)
. Since
v
is unitary and central in
B\otimes C
, we may write
v=\sum_{\gamma,r}e^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}=\sum\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{1/2}b^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}
where
\alpha_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}(C))
. Then,
u=(b_{t}\otimes 1)v=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it+1/2}b^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}
and
u^{*}=\sum_{\zeta,s}\lambda_{\zeta,s}^{-it+1/2}b^{\zeta}_{ss}\otimes\alpha^{*}_{\zeta}
. It follows that:

(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u)=\sum_{\gamma,r,k,t}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it+1/2}b^{\kappa}_{kt}\otimes u^{kt,\kappa}_{rr,\gamma}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}\,\,\text{and}\,\,(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u^{*})=\sum_{\zeta,s,t^{\prime},l}\lambda_{\zeta,s}^{-it+1/2}b^{\kappa}_{t^{\prime}l}\otimes u^{t^{\prime}l,\kappa}_{ss,\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{\zeta}^{*}.

Fix
1\leq p\leq N_{\kappa}
. Using the formula
b^{\kappa}_{kt}b^{\kappa}_{pp}b^{\kappa}_{t^{\prime}l}=\frac{\delta_{tp}\delta_{t^{\prime}p}}{\lambda_{\kappa,p}}b^{\kappa}_{kl}
, we find:

(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u)(b^{\kappa}_{pp}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u^{*})=\sum_{\gamma,\zeta,r,s,k,l}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,r}}{\lambda_{\zeta,s}}\right)^{it}\frac{(\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\zeta,s})^{1/2}}{\lambda_{\kappa,p}}b^{\kappa}_{kl}\otimes u^{kp,\kappa}_{rr,\gamma}u^{pl,\kappa}_{ss,\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}\alpha_{\zeta}^{*}.

Note that
(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h}\otimes{\rm id}_{C})(b^{\kappa}_{pp}\otimes 1\otimes 1)=b^{\kappa}_{pp}\otimes 1\otimes 1
hence,

(1)

\sum_{\gamma,\zeta,r,s}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,r}}{\lambda_{\zeta,s}}\right)^{it}\frac{(\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\zeta,s})^{1/2}}{\lambda_{\kappa,p}}u^{pp,\kappa}_{rr,\gamma}u^{pp,\kappa}_{ss,\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}\alpha_{\zeta}^{*}=1\otimes 1.

By the Haar state formula given in Lemma
2.19
we have:

h(u^{pp,\kappa}_{rr,\gamma}u^{pp,\kappa}_{ss,\zeta})=\left(\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\kappa,p}-\frac{1}{\delta-1}\right)(\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\zeta,s})^{1/2}+\delta_{\gamma,\zeta}\delta_{rs}\frac{1-\lambda_{\kappa,p}}{\delta-1}.

Applying
h\otimes{\rm id}_{C}
to Equation
(\ref{EqT})
we get:

\left(\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}-\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-1}\right)\left[\sum_{\gamma,\zeta,r,s}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,r}}{\lambda_{\zeta,s}}\right)^{it}\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\zeta,s}\alpha_{\gamma}\alpha_{\zeta}^{*}\right]+\left(\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-1}-\frac{1}{\delta-1}\right)1=1.

Define
w:=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it}\alpha_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{Z}(C)
. Note that, since
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
,
\delta=\sum_{t}\lambda_{\kappa,t}^{-1}>\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-1}
hence,

\left(\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}-\frac{1}{\delta-1}\lambda_{\kappa,p}^{-1}\right)\neq 0

and
ww^{*}=\sum_{\gamma,\zeta,r,s}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\gamma,r}}{\lambda_{\zeta,s}}\right)^{it}\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\zeta,s}\alpha_{\gamma}\alpha_{\zeta}^{*}=1
. We conclude that
w
is unitary so the convex combination
w=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it}\alpha_{\gamma}
is an extreme point in the unit ball of
C
which implies that
\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it}\alpha_{\gamma}=\lambda_{\zeta,s}^{it}\alpha_{\zeta}
for all
\gamma,\zeta,r,s
.
Writing
a\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}(C))
the unitary satisfying
a=\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it}\alpha_{\gamma}
for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
and all
1\leq r\leq N_{\gamma}
one has

u=\sum_{\gamma,r}\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{it+1/2}b^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes\alpha_{\gamma}=\sum\lambda_{\gamma,r}^{1/2}b^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes a=1\otimes a.

Hence, for all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
, one has:

\displaystyle 1\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h}(x)\otimes 1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes x\otimes 1)={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))(1\otimes x\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1\otimes x\otimes aa^{*})=1\otimes x\otimes 1.

It follows that
\sigma_{t}^{h}={\rm id}
.

(2)
follows from
(1)
(with
t=0
).

(3)
can be obtained through the same reasoning as for
(1)
, replacing the modular group relation
(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h})\beta_{\kappa}=\beta_{\kappa}\circ\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
by the scaling group relation
(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau_{t}^{H})\beta_{\kappa}=\beta_{\kappa}\circ\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}
.∎

When
N_{\kappa}=1
for all
\kappa
, so that
B=\mathbb{C}^{K}
, we obtain the following Lemma.

Lemma 2.21
.

If
\beta
is
2
-ergodic and
N_{\kappa}=1
for all
\kappa
(so that
B=\mathbb{C}^{K}
) and
K\geq 3
then, for any unital C*-algebra
C
and any
1\leq\gamma,\kappa\leq K
with
\kappa\neq\gamma
,

\mathbb{C}1\otimes C=\beta_{\gamma}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C\cap\beta_{\kappa}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C\subset C(H)\otimes C.

Proof.

Since
\beta
is ergodic,
\psi
is a
\delta
-form. It is easy to check that the unique
\delta
-form on
\mathbb{C}^{K}
is the uniform normalized trace and
\delta=K
. Write
u=\sum_{i,j=1}^{K}e_{ij}\otimes u_{ij}
, where
e_{ij}
are the canonical matrix units, the unitary representation of
H
associated with
\beta
. Note that
\beta_{i}(\mathbb{C}^{K})={\rm Span}\{u_{ij}\,:\,1\leq j\leq K\}
. By Lemma
2.19
and since
\delta=K
we find:

\displaystyle h(u_{ij}u_{kl})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{K(K-1)}+\dfrac{1}{K-1}\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}-\dfrac{1}{K(K-1)}\delta_{jl}-\dfrac{1}{K(K-1)}\delta_{ik}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\dfrac{1}{K}&\text{if $i=k$ and $j=l$}.\\
0&\text{if $i=k$ and $j\neq l$, or $i\neq k$ and $j=l$}.\\
\dfrac{1}{K(K-1)}&\text{otherwise}.\\
\end{cases}

Let
E_{i}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{K}
be the ucp map from Proposition
2.13
. Using the formula above for the Haar state as well as the formula given in Proposition
2.13
we obtain:

E_{i}(u_{jk})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{K-1}\sum_{l\neq k}e_{l}&\text{if}&i\neq j,\\
e_{k}&\text{if}&i=j.\end{array}\right.

Let
i\neq j
and
w\in\beta_{i}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C\cap\beta_{j}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C
be a unitary (
i\neq j
), then we can write

w=\sum_{k}u_{jk}\otimes\mu_{k}=\sum_{l}u_{il}\otimes\nu_{l}\text{ where, for all }k,l,\,\,\mu_{k},\nu_{l}\in\mathcal{U}(C).

Applying
E_{i}\otimes{\rm id}_{C}
to
w
, we get that for any
l
,
\nu_{l}=\frac{1}{K-1}\sum_{k\neq l}\mu_{k}
. In particular,
\frac{1}{K-1}\sum_{k\neq l}\mu_{k}
is a unitary so it is an extreme point in the unit ball of
C
. It follows that
\mu_{k}=\mu_{k^{\prime}}
for all
k,k^{\prime}\neq l
. Since this holds for all
l
and
K\geq 3
, we deduce that
\mu_{k}
does not depend on
k
hence
w
is of the form
w=1\otimes v
, with
v\in\mathcal{U}(C)
. The C*-algebra
\beta_{i}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C\cap\beta_{j}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C
being the linear span of its unitaries, we get
\beta_{i}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C\cap\beta_{j}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes C=\mathbb{C}1\otimes C
.∎

3.
Generalized Free Wreath Products

In this section, we introduce a novel approach to free wreath products. Recall that for any groups
\Gamma
and
\Lambda
, and any action
\beta:\Lambda\curvearrowright X
on a set
X
, the generalized wreath product is defined as
\Gamma\wr_{\beta}\Lambda:=\Gamma^{X}\rtimes\Lambda
. In this definition, the group action
\Lambda\curvearrowright\Gamma^{X}
is by shifting the coordinates according to the action
\beta
. Our aim is to extend this construction, in a free manner, to any compact quantum groups
G
and
H
with an action
\beta:H\curvearrowright B
on a unital C*-algebra
B
. Below, we outline some motivations for our construction and address certain limitations inherent to the C*-algebra
B
.

3.1.
Motivations

Let’s begin by reviewing Bichon’s original construction
[
Bic04
]
. Let
G
be a compact quantum group and
u\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(S_{N}^{+})
the generating magic unitary. The C*-algebra of the free wreath product is defined by
C(G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+}):=C(G)^{*N}*C(S_{N}^{+})/I
, where
I
is the closed two sided ideal generated by
\{\nu_{i}(a)u_{ij}-u_{ij}\nu_{i}(a)\,:\,a\in A,\,1\leq i,j\leq N\}
, with
\nu_{i}\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow C(G)^{*N}
being the
i^{th}
-copy of
C(G)
in the free product.

We consider now a new way to describe this C*-algebra. Let
\beta\,:\,\mathbb{C}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes C(S_{N}^{+})
be the universal action of
S_{N}^{+}
on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
i.e.
\beta(e_{j}):=\sum_{i}e_{i}\otimes u_{ij}
and
\rho\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes C(G)^{*N}
be the unital
*
-homomorphism defined by
\rho(a):=\sum_{i}e_{i}\otimes\nu_{i}(a)
. Viewing both
C(S_{N}^{+}),C(G)^{*N}\subset C(G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+})
we have
\rho(a)\beta(e_{j})=\sum_{i}e_{i}\otimes\nu_{i}(a)u_{ij}=\sum_{i}e_{i}\otimes u_{ij}\nu_{i}(a)=\beta(e_{j})\rho(a)
. It follows that for all
a\in C(G)
and
b\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
one has
\rho(a)\beta(b)=\beta(b)\rho(a)
. The C*-algebra
C(G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+})
is actually the universal unital C*-algebra generated by
C(S_{N}^{+})
and the coefficients of a unital
*
-homomorphism
\rho\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes C(G\wr_{*}S_{N}^{+})
satisfying
[\rho(a),\beta(b)]=0
for all
a\in C(G)
and
b\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
in the following precise sense.

Proposition 3.1
.

If
A
is a unital C*-algebra with unital
*
-homomorphisms
\iota\,:\,C(S_{N}^{+})\rightarrow A
and
\pi\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A
such that
[\pi(a),({\rm id}\otimes\iota)(\beta(b))]=0
for all
a\in C(G)
and
b\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
then, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
