4.2
. Define the ucp map
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}:=E_{2}\circ\varphi_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
.

If
x=X_{0}\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1})X_{1}\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n})X_{n}
(
n\geq 1
) is reduced then

\varphi_{\kappa}(x)=\mu_{\kappa}(X_{0})\nu_{\kappa}(1\otimes a_{1})\dots\nu_{\kappa}(1\otimes a_{n})\mu_{\kappa}(X_{n})

is reduced in
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}
of length at least
2
so
E_{2}(y)=0
. It follows that
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(x)=E_{2}(y)=0
. We prove in the same way that
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(x)=0
for all types of reduced operators. Hence,
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(\mathcal{C}_{\kappa})=\{0\}
. Recall that
\varphi_{\kappa}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes C(F)}=\mu_{\kappa}
and
E_{2}\circ\mu_{\kappa}={\rm id}
. Hence,
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}={\rm id}
. It remains to show that
\ker(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa})\subset\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}}
and this follows from
(1)
and the fact that
\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}
is the identity on the subalgebra
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
.

(3)
By definition
\Delta
we have, writing
\Delta_{G}(a)=\sum a_{(1)}\otimes a_{(2)}
, with
a_{(1)},a_{(2)}\in{\rm Pol}(G)
,

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(\rho_{\kappa}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum(\chi_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})(a_{(1)}\otimes\rho(a_{(2)}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma,k,l}(\chi_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}(a_{(1)}\otimes e^{\gamma}_{kl})\otimes\rho^{\gamma}_{kl}(a_{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma,k,l}\left[\rho_{\kappa}(a_{(1)})(\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma}_{kl})\otimes 1_{C(F)})\right]\otimes\rho^{\gamma}_{kl}(a_{(2)})

Since
\Delta(C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F))=\Delta_{H\times F}(C(H\times F))\subset(C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F))\otimes(C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F))
we conclude easily that
\Delta(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\subset C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
.

Let
x
be a reduced operator and let us show that
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
. We prove our statement for
x
a reduced operator of the form
x=\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1})X_{1}\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n})X_{n}
, the proof for other types of reduced operator is the same.

Claim.

If for all
s
,
X_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes\mathbb{C}1_{C(F)}
then
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
.

Proof of the Claim.
Write
x=\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1})(X_{1}\otimes 1)\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n})(X_{n}\otimes 1)
, where
X_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)
is such that
E_{\kappa}(X_{s})=0
. Writing
X_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)
as
X_{s}=\sum_{i_{s},j_{s}}b^{\kappa}_{i_{s}j_{s}}\otimes x_{i_{s}j_{s}}
and
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(X_{s}\otimes 1)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{H})(X_{s})\otimes 1_{C(F)}=\sum(X_{s})_{(1)}\otimes(X_{s})_{(2)}
where

(X_{s})_{(1)}=b^{\kappa}_{i_{s}j_{s}}\otimes(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)}\otimes 1\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)\text{ and},

(X_{s})_{(2)}=(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(2)}\otimes 1\in{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)

with
\Delta_{H}(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})=\sum(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)}\otimes(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(2)}
, we obtain:

({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)=\sum\rho_{\kappa}((a_{1})_{(1)})(\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma_{1}}_{k_{1}l_{1}})\otimes 1)(X_{1})_{(1)}\dots\rho_{\kappa}((a_{n})_{(1)})(\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma_{n}}_{k_{n}l_{n}})\otimes 1)(X_{n})_{(1)}

\otimes\rho^{\gamma_{1}}_{k_{1}l_{1}}((a_{1})_{(2)})(X_{1})_{(2)}\dots\rho^{\gamma_{n}}_{k_{n}l_{n}}((a_{n})_{(2)})(X_{n})_{(2)}.

Where
\Delta_{G}(a_{k})=\sum(a_{k})_{(1)}\otimes(a_{k})_{(2)}
and
E_{F}((a_{k})_{(1)})=0
. If
(E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})((X_{s})_{(1)})=0
, for all
s
, then
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
, since

(E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})((\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma_{s}}_{k_{s}l_{s}})\otimes 1)(X_{s})_{(1)})=e^{\gamma_{s}}_{k_{s}l_{s}}(E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})((X_{s})_{(1)})=0.

Otherwise, we write
(X_{s})_{(1)}=(X_{s})_{(1)}^{\circ}+(\beta_{\kappa}E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})((X_{s})_{(1)})
, where
(E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})((X_{s})_{(1)}^{\circ})=0
so that we may decompose
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)
as the sum of two terms
Y_{s}+Z_{s}
where in
Y_{s}
is the same as
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)
but replacing
(X_{s})_{(1)}
by
(X_{s})_{(1)}^{\circ}
and the second term is:

Z_{s}=\sum\rho_{\kappa}((a_{1})_{(1)})\dots(\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma_{s}}_{k_{s}l_{s}})\otimes 1)(\beta_{\kappa}E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})(b^{\kappa}_{i_{s}j_{s}}\otimes(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)}\otimes 1)\dots(X_{n})_{(1)}

\otimes\rho^{\gamma_{1}}_{k_{1}l_{1}}((a_{1})_{(2)})(X_{1})_{(2)}\dots\rho^{\gamma_{n}}_{k_{n}l_{n}}((a_{n})_{(2)})(X_{n})_{(2)}.

By Proposition
2.13
we have:

E_{\kappa}(b^{\kappa}_{i_{s}j_{s}}\otimes(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)})=\delta^{-1}\lambda_{\kappa,i_{s}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j_{s}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{\beta,r,t}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\beta,r}}{\lambda_{\beta,t}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}h_{H}(u^{j_{s}i_{s},\kappa}_{tr,\beta}(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)})b^{\beta}_{rt}

Replacing this expression in
Z_{s}
we get:

Z_{s}=\delta^{-1}\sum\left(\frac{\lambda_{\beta,r}}{\lambda_{\beta,t}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{\kappa}((a_{1})_{(1)})\dots(\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\gamma_{s}}_{k_{s}l_{s}}b^{\beta}_{rt})\otimes 1)\rho_{\kappa}((a_{s+1})_{(1)})\dots

\otimes(\rho)^{\gamma_{1}}_{k_{1}l_{1}}((a_{1})_{(2)})\dots\left(\sum_{i_{s},j_{s},(1)^{\prime},(2)^{\prime}}\lambda_{\kappa,i_{s}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{\kappa,j_{s}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}h_{H}(u^{j_{s}i_{s},\kappa}_{tr,\beta}(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(1)^{\prime}})(x_{i_{s}j_{s}})_{(2)^{\prime}}\right)\dots

Since
E_{\kappa}(X_{s})=0
, we may now use Lemma
2.15
to deduce that
Z_{s}=0
. Doing that for all
s
, we conclude that
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
.
∎

End of the proof of
(3)
.
Fix a basis
(y_{k})_{k}
of
{\rm Pol}(F)
. If now
x
is an arbitrary reduced operator of the form
x=\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1})X_{1}\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n})X_{n}
, with
X_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
, we may write
X_{s}
as a finite sum
X_{s}=\sum_{k}X_{s,k}\otimes y_{k}
, where
X_{s,k}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)
. Since
x
is reduced one has
\sum_{k}E_{\kappa}(X_{s,k})y_{k}=(E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(X_{s})=0
. Since the family
(y_{k})_{k}
is linearly independent we obtain
E_{\kappa}(X_{s,k})=0
for all
k
. Write
x
as a finite sum:

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},\dots k_{n}}\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1})(X_{1,k_{1}}\otimes y_{k_{1}})\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n})(X_{n,k_{n}}\otimes y_{k_{n}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},\dots k_{n}}\rho_{\kappa}(a_{1}y_{k_{1}})(X_{1,k_{1}}\otimes 1)\dots\rho_{\kappa}(a_{n}y_{k_{n}})(X_{n,k_{n}}\otimes 1),

where, in the last equality, we used the relations
\rho_{\kappa}(f)=1\otimes 1\otimes f
, for all
f\in C(F)
. Since
E_{F}(a_{s}y_{k_{s}})=E_{F}(a_{s})y_{k_{s}}=0
for all
s
, we deduce from the Claim that
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}
.

(4)
. By
(1)
, it suffices to prove the formula for
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}
and for
X\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
. If
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}
we have
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(X)\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
by
(3)
. It now follows from
(2)
that

(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{C(\mathbb{G})})({\rm id}_{M_{N_{\kappa}}}\otimes\Delta)(X)=0=\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(X)\otimes 1_{C(\mathbb{G})}.

If now
X\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
then, since
\Delta|_{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}=\Delta_{H\times F}
,

({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(X)\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F),

and therefore
(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{C(\mathbb{G})})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(X)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(X)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}
.

(5)
. It suffices to apply
({\rm id}\otimes h_{H}\otimes h_{F}\otimes{\rm id})
to the equation proved in
(4)
and use the fact that the Haar state of
H\times F
is
h_{H}\otimes h_{F}
.∎

4.3.
Block-extended C*-algebra

We use the map
(x\mapsto 1\otimes x)
to view

C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)\subset M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})

for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. Define:

C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=(M_{N_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{1}(\mathbb{G}))\underset{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}{*}\dots\underset{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}{*}(M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{K}(\mathbb{G})).

C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is called the
block-extended C*-algebra
. We will view
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\subset C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Note that, viewing
C(\mathbb{G})=C_{1}(\mathbb{G})\underset{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}{*}\dots\underset{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}{*}C_{K}(\mathbb{G})
(by Theorem
4.2
) and using the universal property of the amalgamated free product
C(\mathbb{G})
, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\iota\,:\,C(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\iota(x)=1\otimes x
, for all
x\in C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
and all
\kappa
.

Consider the conditional expectation

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}:=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F).

Note that
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\subset\ker(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa})
. Consider the unique ucp map
E\,:\,C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
such that
E(\iota(x))=x
for all
x\in C(H)\otimes C(F)
and
E(x)=0
for any reduced operator in
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
i.e. of the form
x=x_{1}\dots x_{n}
where
n\geq 1
,
\kappa_{s}\neq\kappa_{s+1}
,
x_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa_{s}}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa_{s}}(\mathbb{G})
with
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa_{s}}(x_{s})=0
.

By Lemma
4.3
we may consider:

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\kappa}:={\rm id}_{M_{N_{\kappa}}}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})\subset C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})

By the universal property of the amalgamated free product
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\widetilde{\Delta}\,:C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
such that
\widetilde{\Delta}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\kappa}
for all
\kappa
.

Remark 4.4
.

We have
(\iota\otimes{\rm id})\circ\Delta=\widetilde{\Delta}\circ\iota
and
(\widetilde{\Delta}\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)\widetilde{\Delta}
. Indeed,
x\in C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, we have
\widetilde{\Delta}\circ\iota(x)=\widetilde{\Delta}(1\otimes x)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(1\otimes x)=(\iota\otimes{\rm id})\Delta(x)
and, since
\Delta(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\subset C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
and
\Delta
is co-associative, one has, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Delta}\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Delta}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)\widetilde{\Delta}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}.

Theorem 4.5
.

One has
(E\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}=\Delta_{H\times F}\circ E
. In particular:

(1)

((E\circ\iota)\otimes{\rm id})\Delta=\Delta_{H\times F}\circ(E\circ\iota)
so
h_{\mathbb{G}}:=(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ(E\circ\iota)
and
E\circ\iota=E_{H\times F}
is the conditional expectation
C(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(H\times F)
of the dual quantum subgroup
H\times F
.

(2)

({\rm id}_{B}\otimes E\circ\iota)\rho=1_{B}\otimes 1_{C(H)}\otimes E_{F}(\cdot)
therefore
({\rm id}_{B}\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})\rho=h_{G}(\cdot)1_{B}
.

Proof.

We start with the following Claim.

Claim.

\ker(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa})=\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}+\ker(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)}
.

Proof of the Claim.
Let
x\in\ker(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa})
. By Lemma
4.3

(1)
, there are sequences
(x_{n})
and
(y_{n})
such that
x_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}
and
y_{n}\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
such that
x_{n}+y_{n}\rightarrow x
. Hence,

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(x_{n})+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(y_{n})=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(y_{n})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}(x)=0.

Define
y_{n}^{\circ}:=y_{n}-1_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(y_{n})
so that
(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(y_{n}^{\circ})=0
. Note that
y_{n}^{\circ}\in\ker(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
and
x_{n}+y_{n}^{\circ}\rightarrow x
.
∎

End of the proof of Theorem.
Suppose that
x\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
then, since
\widetilde{\Delta}(x)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
one has, by Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle(E\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(E\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)=(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes\Delta)\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(x)=\Delta\circ E(x)=\Delta_{H\times F}\circ E(x).

In particular,
(E\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}(x)=\Delta_{H\times F}\circ E(x)
for
x\in C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)\subset C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and it remains to show that the formula holds for any reduced operator
x
in
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Write such a reduced operator
x=x_{1}\dots x_{n}\in C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
,
x_{s}\in M_{N_{\kappa_{s}}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa_{s}}(\mathbb{G})
with
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa_{s}}(x_{s})=0
and
\kappa_{s}\neq\kappa_{s+1}
so that
E(x)=0
. It suffices to show that
(E\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}(x)=0
. By linearity, continuity and the Claim, we may and will assume that, for all
s
,
x_{s}\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa_{s}}\cup\ker(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H)\otimes{\rm Pol}(F)
. Since

\widetilde{\Delta}(x)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x_{1})\dots({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(x_{s}),

\widetilde{\Delta}(x)
is an alternating product from
({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(\mathcal{C}_{\kappa_{s}})\subset\mathcal{C}_{\kappa_{s}}\otimes C(\mathbb{G})\subset\ker(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa_{s}})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
(Lemma
4.3
) and we deduce from:

({\rm id}\otimes\Delta)(\ker(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H\times F))\subset\ker(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H\times F)\otimes C(\mathbb{G})\subset\ker(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\kappa_{s}})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})

that
(E\otimes{\rm id})\widetilde{\Delta}(x)=0
. It proves the first statement of the Theorem.

(1)
. We use Remark
4.4
and the previous statement to deduce the first equation. Then, applying
h_{H}\otimes h_{F}\otimes{\rm id}
shows that
(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ E\circ\iota
is
\Delta
-invariant hence,
h_{\mathbb{G}}=(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ E\circ\iota
. We conclude the proof of
(1)
by Remark
2.6

(3)
.

(2)
. Let
a\in C(G)
and write
\rho(a)=\sum_{\kappa,i,j}e^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a)
. We use the notations of Theorem
4.2
, Lemma
4.3
and their proofs. By construction of
E
we have:

\displaystyle E\circ\iota(\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(1\otimes\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\mathbb{E}_{\kappa}(1\otimes\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})E_{2}\circ\varphi_{\kappa}(1\otimes\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{\kappa}}(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})E_{2}\left(\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{si}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\nu_{\kappa}(1\otimes a)\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{js}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{\kappa}}(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})((e^{\kappa}_{si}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes E_{F})(1\otimes a)(e^{\kappa}_{js}\otimes 1\otimes 1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{ij}1_{C(H)}\otimes E_{F}(a).

Hence,
({\rm id}_{B}\otimes E\circ\iota)\rho(a)=\sum_{\kappa,i}e^{\kappa}_{ii}\otimes 1_{C(H)}\otimes E_{F}(a)=1_{B}\otimes 1_{C(H)}\otimes E_{F}(a)
.∎

4.4.
Relations with fundamental quantum groups

Let us now construct a dual quantum subgroup of
G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
from a dual quantum subgroup of
G
. Assume that
\Lambda
is a CQG such that
C(F)\subset C(\Lambda)\subset C(G)
with the inclusion intertwining the comultiplications. Define
\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda}:=\Lambda\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
with canonical homomorphism
\rho_{\Lambda}\,:\,C(\Lambda)\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})
and view
C(H),C(F)\subset C(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})
. Consider, by the universal property of
C(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})
, the unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\iota_{\Lambda}\,:\,C(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})
such that
({\rm id}\otimes\iota_{\Lambda})\rho_{\Lambda}=\rho|_{C(\Lambda)}
and
\iota_{\Lambda}|_{C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}
is the inclusion.

Proposition 4.6
.

The map
\iota_{\Lambda}
is faithful and intertwines the comultiplications so that
\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda}
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
.

Proof.

Consider the two canonical maps
\beta_{G}\,:\,C(G)\otimes B\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
and
\beta_{\Lambda}\,:\,C(\Lambda)\otimes B\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})
and note that, by definition,
({\rm id}\otimes\iota_{\Lambda})\beta_{\Lambda}=\beta_{G}|_{C(\Lambda)\otimes B}
. It follows easily that

({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\circ\iota_{\Lambda})\rho_{\Lambda}=({\rm id}\otimes(\iota_{\Lambda}\otimes\iota_{\Lambda})\circ\Delta_{\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda}})\rho_{\Lambda}

which implies that
\iota_{\Lambda}
intertwines the comultiplications. Let us now show that
\iota_{\Lambda}
is faithful. By Proposition
2.5
, it suffices to show that
\iota_{\Lambda}
intertwines the Haar states (since it will imply that it is faithful at the reduced level). Note that, for
1\leq\kappa\leq K
,
\iota_{\Lambda}(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda}))\subset C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
. Hence, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\widetilde{\iota}_{\Lambda}\,:\,C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
such that:

\widetilde{\iota}_{\Lambda}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}_{\Lambda})}={\rm id}_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes\iota_{\Lambda}|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})},\text{ for all }1\leq\kappa\leq K.

By construction,
\widetilde{\iota}_{\Lambda}
maps reduced operators in
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{\Lambda})
to reduced operators in
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and is the identity on
C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
. By uniqueness property, we have
E\circ\iota_{\Lambda}=E_{\Lambda}
, where
E\,:\,C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
and
E_{\Lambda}\,:\,C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{\Lambda})\rightarrow C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)
are the canonical ucp maps constructed in Section
4.3
. It follows from Theorem
4.5
that
\iota_{\Lambda}
intertwines the Haar states.∎

Using this dual quantum subgroup construction, we can now study the decomposition of a free wreath product
G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
as a fundamental quantum group when
G
itself is a fundamental quantum group.

Let
G:=\pi_{1}(G_{p},\Lambda_{e},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{T})
be a fundamental quantum group with graph maps
s_{e}\,:\,C(\Lambda_{e})\rightarrow C(G_{s(e)})
so that
C(G)=\langle C(G_{p}),u_{e}\,|\,p\in V(\mathcal{G}),e\in E(\mathcal{G})\rangle
. Following
[
FT24
, Definition 6.1]
, the
loop subgroup
of
G
is the subgroup
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{U}(C(G))
generated by
\{u_{e}\,:\,e\in E(\mathcal{G})\}
. It is shown in
[
FT24
, Proposition 6.2]
that the unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\pi\,:\,C^{*}(\Gamma)\rightarrow C(G)
extending the inclusion
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{U}(C(G))
is faithful and intertwines the comultiplications so that
\widehat{\Gamma}
is a dual quantum subgroup of
G
.

Define, for
p\in V(\mathcal{G})
and
e\in E(\mathcal{G})
,
\mathbb{G}_{p}:=G_{p}\wr_{*,\beta}H
and
\mathbb{G}_{e}:=\Lambda_{e}\wr_{*,\beta}H
. Write
\rho_{p}\,:\,C(G_{p})\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{p})
and
\rho_{e}\,:\,C(\Lambda_{e})\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{e})
the canonical morphisms and view
C(H)\subset C(\mathbb{G}_{p})
and
C(H)\subset C(\mathbb{G}_{e})
. As in Proposition
4.6
we consider, for each
e\in E(\mathcal{G})
, the unique map
s^{\prime}_{e}\,:\,C(\mathbb{G}_{e})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G}_{s(e)})
such that
({\rm id}\otimes s_{e}^{\prime})\rho_{e}=\rho_{s(e)}\circ s_{e}
and
s_{e}^{\prime}|_{C(H)}
is the inclusion, which is, by Proposition
4.6
, faithful and intertwines the comultiplications. We obtain a graph of quantum groups
(\mathbb{G}_{p},\mathbb{G}_{e},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{T})
with graph maps
s^{\prime}_{e}
.

The next result should be compared with Theorem
3.16
where we studied such a decomposition when
H
is a fundamental quantum group. It is also a generalization of
[
FT24
, Theorem 6.3]
.

Theorem 4.7
.

There is a canonical isomorphism
G\wr_{*,\beta,\widehat{\Gamma}}H\simeq\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{p},\mathbb{G}_{e},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{T})
.

Proof.

Write
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta,\widehat{\Gamma}}H
with canonical morphism
\rho\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
and view
C(H),C^{*}(\Gamma)\subset C(\mathbb{G})
. We also write
\mathbb{G}^{\prime}:=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{G}_{p},\mathbb{G}_{e},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{T})
, we view
C(H)\subset C(\mathbb{G}_{p})\subset C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
and we denote

C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})=\langle C(\mathbb{G}_{p}),v_{e}\,|\,p\in V(\mathcal{G}),e\in E(\mathcal{G})\rangle\text{ and }C(G)=\langle C(G_{p}),u_{e}\,|\,p\in V(\mathcal{G}),e\in E(\mathcal{G})\rangle.

View, for all
p\in V(\mathcal{G})
,
\rho_{p}\,:\,C(G_{p})\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{p})\subset B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
and note that, for all
b\in C(\Lambda_{e})
,

\rho_{s(e)}(s_{e}(b))=({\rm id}\otimes s_{e}^{\prime})\rho_{e}(b)=(1_{B}\otimes v_{e}^{*})({\rm id}\otimes r_{e}^{\prime})(\rho_{e}(b))(1_{B}\otimes v_{e})=(1_{B}\otimes v_{e}^{*})\rho_{r(e)}\circ r_{e}(b)(1_{B}\otimes v_{e}).

The universal property of
C(G)
gives a unique unital
*
-homomorphism

\rho^{\prime}\,:\,C(G)\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})\text{ such that }\rho^{\prime}|_{C(G_{p})}=\rho_{p},\,\,\rho^{\prime}(u_{e})=1_{B}\otimes v_{e}\text{ for all }p\in V(\mathcal{G}),e\in E(\mathcal{G}).

By construction of
\rho^{\prime}
, one has
\rho^{\prime}(C^{*}(\Gamma))\subset\mathbb{C}1_{B}\otimes C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
hence, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\iota_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\,:\,C^{*}(\Gamma)\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
such that
\rho^{\prime}(f)=1_{B}\otimes\iota_{\widehat{\Gamma}}(f)
for all
f\in C^{*}(\Gamma)
. Note that for all
p\in V(\mathcal{G})
,
a\in C(G_{p})
and
b\in B
,
\rho^{\prime}(a)\beta(b)=\rho_{p}(a)\beta(b)=\beta(b)\rho_{p}(a)=\beta(b)\rho^{\prime}(a)
. Moreover, since each
s_{e}^{\prime}
is the identity on
C(H)
, each
v_{e}
commutes with
C(H)
inside
C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
hence,
\rho^{\prime}(u_{e})\beta(b)=(1_{B}\otimes v_{e})\beta(b)=\beta(b)(1_{B}\otimes v_{e})
for all
b\in B
and
e\in E(\mathcal{G})
. Also,
C(H)
commutes with
\iota_{\widehat{\Gamma}}(C^{*}(\Gamma))
. By the universal property of the free wreath product C*-algebra
C(\mathbb{G})
, there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\pi\,:\,C(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
such that
({\rm id}\otimes\pi)\rho=\rho^{\prime}
,
\pi|_{C(H)}
is the inclusion and
\pi|_{C^{*}(\Gamma)}=\iota_{\widehat{\Gamma}}
. Writing
\beta_{G}\,:\,C(G)\otimes B\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
and
\beta_{G_{p}}\,:\,C(G_{p})\otimes B\rightarrow B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}_{p})\subset B\otimes C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
the canonical morphisms we have, by construction,
\beta_{G_{p}}=({\rm id}\otimes\pi)\circ\beta_{G}|_{C(G_{p})\otimes B}
. For
a\in C(G_{p})\subset C(G)
one has:

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\pi)\rho(a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}})\rho^{\prime}(a)=({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}_{p}})\rho_{p}(a)=(\beta_{G_{p}}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho_{p})\Delta_{G_{p}}(a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\pi\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho^{\prime})\Delta_{G_{p}}(a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\pi\otimes\pi)(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho)\Delta_{G}(a)=({\rm id}\otimes(\pi\otimes\pi)\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})\rho.

Since
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\pi(u_{e})=\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}(v_{e})=v_{e}\otimes v_{e}=(\pi\otimes\pi)\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(u_{e})
and,

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\circ\pi|_{C(H)}=\Delta_{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}|_{C(H)}=\Delta_{H}=(\pi\otimes\pi)\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}|_{C(H)}

we deduce that
\pi
intertwines the comultiplications.

We now show that
\pi
is an isomorphism by constructing its inverse morphism
\pi^{-1}\,:\,C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})
. We first note that, by the universal property of the free wreath product C*-algebra
C(\mathbb{G}_{p})
, there exists a unique
\pi_{p}^{-1}\,:\,C(\mathbb{G}_{p})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})
such that
({\rm id}\otimes\pi_{p}^{-1})\rho_{p}=\rho|_{C(\mathbb{G}_{p})}
and
\pi^{-1}_{p}|_{C(H)}
is the inclusion. Viewing
C^{*}(\Gamma)\subset C(\mathbb{G})
, we have the relation
\rho(u_{e})=1_{B}\otimes u_{e}
, for all
e\in E(\mathcal{G})
. Note that, for all
e\in E(\mathcal{G})
and
b\in C(\Lambda_{e})
one has,

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\pi^{-1}_{s(e)}\circ s_{e}^{\prime})\rho_{e}(b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\pi^{-1}_{s(e)})\rho_{s(e)}\circ s_{e}(b)=\rho\circ s_{e}(b)=\rho(u_{e})^{*}\rho(r_{e}(b))\rho(u_{e})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho(u_{e})^{*}({\rm id}\otimes\pi^{-1}_{r(e)})\rho_{r(e)}\circ r_{e}(b)\rho(u_{e})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho(u_{e})^{*}({\rm id}\otimes\pi^{-1}_{r(e)}\circ r_{e}^{\prime})\rho_{e}(b)\rho(u_{e})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1\otimes u_{e}^{*})({\rm id}\otimes\pi^{-1}_{r(e)}\circ r_{e}^{\prime})\rho_{e}(b)(1\otimes u_{e}).

Moreover,
\pi_{s(e)}^{-1}\circ s_{e}^{\prime}|_{C(H)}
is the inclusion of
C(H)\subset C(\mathbb{G})
and since
C(H)
commutes with
C^{*}(\Gamma)
in
C(\mathbb{G})
, the universal property of
C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})
gives a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\pi^{-1}\,:\,C(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})
such that
\pi^{-1}|_{C(\mathbb{G}_{p})}=\pi_{p}^{-1}
and
\pi^{-1}(v_{e})=u_{e}
. It is easy to check that
\pi^{-1}
is the inverse of
\pi
.∎

5.
The reduced C*-algebra and the von Neumann algebra

We fix, for the entire section, an ergodic action
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
on the finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
with unique invariant state
\psi\in B^{*}
and we write
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
.

5.1.
The reduced C*-algebra

For
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, we use the following notation:

C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}):=(B\otimes C(G))\underset{B\otimes C(F)}{*}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F))\text{ and,}

C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}):=(B\otimes C_{r}(G),{\rm id}_{B}\otimes E^{r}_{F})\underset{B\otimes C_{r}(F)}{*}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(H)\otimes C_{r}(F),E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{C_{r}(F)}),

relatively to the inclusion map on the left and
\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{C_{r}(F)}
on the right. Note that the conditional expectations involved in the Voiculescu’s reduced amalgamated free product are faithful which implies that the canonical conditional expectation

E_{\kappa,2}\,:\,C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(H)\otimes C_{r}(F)

is faithful. Consider the unique surjective homomorphism
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}\,:\,C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
such that:

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}|_{B\otimes C(G)}={\rm id}_{B}\otimes\lambda_{G}\text{ and }\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)}={\rm id}_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes\lambda_{H\times F}.

Note that
C_{r}(H\times F)=C_{r}(H)\otimes C_{r}(F)
and
\lambda_{H\times F}=(\lambda_{H}\otimes\lambda_{F})\circ p
, where

p\,:\,C(H)\otimes_{{\rm max}}C(F)\rightarrow C(H)\otimes C(F)

is the canonical surjection. Recall that we have, from Theorem
4.2
, an isomorphism
\pi_{\kappa}\,:\,C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})

with inverse
\varphi_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
.

By universal property, there exists a unique surjective homomorphism

\widetilde{\lambda}\,:\,C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C_{r}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}):=\underset{C_{r}(H)\otimes C_{r}(F)}{*}\left(C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}),(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\circ E_{2,\kappa}\right)

such that
\widetilde{\lambda}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}\circ\varphi_{\kappa}
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. Note that that the conditional expectations
(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\circ E_{\kappa,2}
are faithful which implies that the canonical conditional expectation
E_{r}\,:\,C_{r}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow C_{r}(H)\otimes C_{r}(F)
is also faithful.
Recall that we have an inclusion
\iota\,:\,C(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and define
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}):=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}\circ\varphi_{\kappa}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\subset C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
.

Theorem 5.1
.

The following holds.

(1)

One has
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota(C(\mathbb{G}))
with GNS map
\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota
. Moreover,
E_{r}|_{C_{r}(\mathbb{G})}=E^{r}_{H\times F}
is the reduced conditional expectation associated to the dual quantum subgroup
H\times F
.

(2)

One has
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})=\underset{C_{r}(H\times F)}{*}\left(C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}),E_{r}|_{C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}\right)
.

(3)

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, there is a unique unital
*
-isomorphism:

\pi_{r,\kappa}\,:\,C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})

such that
\pi_{r,\kappa}\circ\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}=({\rm id}\otimes(\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota)|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})\pi_{\kappa}
.

Proof.

(1)
. By construction,
\lambda_{H\times F}\circ E=E_{r}\circ\widetilde{\lambda}
. Since
E_{r}
is faithful and
\widetilde{\lambda}
is surjective, we deduce that
C_{r}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is the reduced C*-algebra of
\lambda_{H\times F}\circ E
and it follows from Remark
2.1
that
\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota(C(\mathbb{G}))
is the reduced C*-algebra of
\lambda_{H\times F}\circ E\circ\iota=\lambda_{H\times F}\circ E_{H\times F}
. We conclude the proof of
(1)
using Remark
2.6

(1)
.

(2)
. Since
C(\mathbb{G})
is generated by the algebras
C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, for
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, we conclude that
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota(C(\mathbb{G}))
is generated by
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
(
1\leq\kappa\leq K
). Moreover, since the
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
are free with amalgamation with respect to
E_{r}|_{C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\circ E_{2,\kappa}
and since
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\subset C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
the result follows.

(3)
. We construct the inverse isomorphism
\varphi_{r,\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
satisfying
\varphi_{r,\kappa}\circ({\rm id}\otimes(\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota)|_{C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}\circ\varphi_{\kappa}
. We view both
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\subset C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\subset C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
in the obvious way. Note that, since
\varphi_{\kappa}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})^{\prime}\cap C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
we deduce that
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})^{\prime}\cap C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Hence, there exists a unique faithful unital
*
-homomorphism
\varphi_{r,\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
, such that
\varphi_{r,\kappa}(b\otimes x)=bx
for all
b\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
and
x\in C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}).

Since
C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is generated by
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\varphi_{\kappa}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
and since
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}
is surjective we deduce that
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is generated by
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\kappa}(\varphi_{\kappa}\circ\iota(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})))=C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
. Both these C*-subalgebras are in the image of
\varphi_{r,\kappa}
so
\varphi_{r,\kappa}
is surjective.∎

We obtain the following interesting Corollary.

Corollary 5.2
.

The following are equivalent.

(1)

\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are exact.

(2)

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is exact.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
. Suppose that
C_{r}(G)
and
C_{r}(H)
are exact then, since
C_{r}(F)\subset C_{r}(G)
is a C*-subalgebra,
C_{r}(F)
is also exact. Theorem
5.1
and
[
Dyk04
, Theorem 3.2]
implies that
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
is exact.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
. Assume that
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
is exact and fix
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. Since
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\subset C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
is a C*-subalgebra, we deduce that
C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
is exact so
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\simeq M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
is also exact. Finally, since both
C_{r}(G),C_{r}(H)\subset C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
are C*-subalgebras, it concludes the proof.∎

5.2.
The von Neumann algebra

For
1\leq\kappa\leq K
define:

L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G),{\rm id}\otimes E_{F}^{\prime\prime})\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{\ast}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F),E_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}).

Let
E_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}\,:\,L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
be the canonical normal faithful conditional expectation. By uniqueness of the reduced C*-algebra of the conditional expectation

E_{B\otimes C_{r}(F)}\,:\,C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow B\otimes C_{r}(F)

we may and will view
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\subset L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
in the canonical way. Note that
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime\prime}=L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Let
E^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa,2}\,:\,L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
be the canonical normal faithful conditional expectation and consider the faithful normal conditional expectation

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}:=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})\circ E^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa,2}\,:\,L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F).

Define

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}):=\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{\ast}(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}),\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}).

By uniqueness of the reduced C*-algebra of
\lambda_{H\times F}\circ E
we may and will view
C_{r}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
in the obvious way and we have
C_{r}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime\prime}={\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Write
\widetilde{E}^{\prime\prime}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
and
\widetilde{E}_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
the canonical normal faithful conditional expectations.

Define
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}):=C_{r,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime\prime}\subset C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime\prime}=L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
so that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\subset L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
.

Theorem 5.3
.

The following holds.

(1)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{\ast}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}),E^{\prime\prime}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and
E^{\prime\prime}_{H\times F}:=\widetilde{E}^{\prime\prime}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}
is the conditional expectation of the dual quantum subgroup
H\times F
.

(2)

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, there is a unique unital normal
*
-isomorphism:

\pi^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}\,:\,L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})

such that
\pi^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}|_{C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}=\pi_{r,\kappa}
.

Proof.

(1).
Let
M:=\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{\ast}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}),E^{\prime\prime}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. By uniqueness of the reduced C*-algebra of
E^{r}_{H\times F}
we may and will view
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})\subset M
. Note that
C_{r}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime\prime}=M
and
E^{\prime\prime}|_{C_{r}(\mathbb{G})}=E^{r}_{H\times F}
.
(1)
follows.

(2)
. Can be proved as statement
(3)
in Theorem
5.1
.
∎

Define the state
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
by
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}:=(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ\widetilde{E}^{\prime\prime}
and note that the Haar state on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is
h_{\mathbb{G}}=(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ E_{H\times F}^{\prime\prime}=h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}
. We also consider the states

\varphi_{\kappa}:=(\psi\otimes h_{F})\circ E_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}\in L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})_{*}\text{ for all }1\leq\kappa\leq K,

and
h_{\kappa}:=\varphi_{\kappa}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}
.

Proposition 5.4
.

The following holds.

(1)

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
one has
\varphi_{\kappa}=(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}
and, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)}=\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}\text{ and }\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{F}}.

(2)

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
one has
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}=\varphi_{\kappa}\circ\widetilde{E}_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}
. In particular,
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}=\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

(3)

For all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
one has
\varphi_{\kappa}=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h_{\kappa})\circ\pi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}=\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}}

(4)

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}=\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}
. In particular, there exists a unique normal faithful conditional expectation
E_{\mathbb{G}}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
such that
h_{\mathbb{G}}\circ E_{\mathbb{G}}=h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
.

Proof.

(1)
and
(2)
. The first equality follows from
\psi\circ E_{\kappa}=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h_{H}
(Proposition
2.13
). The equality about modular groups follows from Remark
2.3
as well as statement
(2)
.

(3)
. Fix a system of matrix units
(e^{\kappa}_{ij})_{ij}
in
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})
diagonalizing
Q_{\kappa}
i.e.
Q_{\kappa}e^{\kappa}_{ij}=\lambda_{k,i}e^{\kappa}_{ij}
. We note that
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})^{\prime}\cap L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is generated by
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\subset L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and

\{\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a)\,:\,1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa},\,a\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\},\text{ where}

\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a):=\sum_{r}(e^{\kappa}_{ri}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1_{B}\otimes a)(e^{\kappa}_{jr}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\in L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. In particular, the linear span of

\{x_{0}\rho^{\kappa}_{i_{1}j_{1}}(a_{1})\dots\rho^{\kappa}_{i_{n}j_{n}}(a_{n})x_{n}\,:\,n\geq 1,1\leq i_{s},j_{s}\leq N_{\kappa},a_{s}\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G),\,x_{s}\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\}

is a unital weakly dense
*
-subalgebra of
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
. Hence, it suffices to show that, for any
X
of the form
X=x_{0}\rho^{\kappa}_{i_{1}j_{1}}(a_{1})\dots\rho^{\kappa}_{i_{n}j_{n}}(a_{n})x_{n}
as above, one has
\varphi_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{st}X)=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})\varphi_{\kappa}(X)
, for all
1\leq s,t\leq N_{\kappa}
. Note that
e^{\kappa}_{st}X=(e^{\kappa}_{si_{1}}\otimes x_{0})(1\otimes a_{1})(e^{\kappa}_{j_{1}i_{2}}\otimes x_{2})\dots(1\otimes a_{n})(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}t}\otimes x_{n})
. Then,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{st}X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ E_{\kappa,2}^{\prime\prime}(e^{\kappa}_{st}X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\left((e^{\kappa}_{si_{1}}\otimes x_{0})E_{\kappa,2}^{\prime\prime}(X^{\prime})(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}t}\otimes x_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{si_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}t})=\frac{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1}e^{\kappa}_{si_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}t})}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}=\frac{{\rm Tr}(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}t}Q_{\kappa}^{-1}e^{\kappa}_{si_{1}}Y)}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{s,t}\lambda_{\kappa,s}^{-1}{\rm Tr}(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}i_{1}}Y)}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st}){\rm Tr}(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}i_{1}}Y)

where
X^{\prime}:=(1\otimes a_{1})(e^{\kappa}_{j_{1}i_{2}}\otimes x_{2})\dots(1\otimes a_{n})
and

Y:=({\rm id}\otimes h_{H}\otimes h_{F})((1\otimes x_{0})E_{\kappa,2}^{\prime\prime}(X^{\prime})(1\otimes x_{n})).

Note that
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\left(\sum_{r}e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r}\right)=(\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\otimes h_{H}\otimes h_{F})E^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa,2}(X)=\varphi_{\kappa}(X)
.

Writing
e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}i_{1}}=\frac{\sum_{r}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{-1}e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r}e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}
we get:

\displaystyle\varphi_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{st}X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}\sum_{r}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{-1}{\rm Tr}(e^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r}e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}Y)=\frac{\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}\sum_{r}\lambda_{\kappa,r}^{-1}{\rm Tr}(e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})}{{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1})}\sum_{r}{\rm Tr}(Q_{\kappa}^{-1}e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r})=\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}\left(\sum_{r}e^{\kappa}_{ri_{1}}Ye^{\kappa}_{j_{n}r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}(e^{\kappa}_{st})\varphi_{\kappa}(X).

For the last part of
(3)
we note that, under the identification
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
,
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}}
so, for all
x\in L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
,
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}}(x)=\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}(x)=(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}})(1\otimes x)=\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}}(x)
.

(4)
. Since for all
x\in L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
,
(h_{H}\otimes h_{F})\circ E^{\prime\prime}(x)=\varphi_{\kappa}(x)=h_{\kappa}(x)
, we deduce from general properties of the amalgamated free product that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)=\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}}(x)
. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is generated by the
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, for
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, it concludes the proof.∎

The following corollary is a direct consequence of Proposition
5.4
.

Corollary 5.5
.

\mathbb{G}
is Kac of and only if both
G
and
H
are Kac.

We now compute the scaling group of
\mathbb{G}
. Note that the scaling group of
G
,
\tau_{t}^{G}
, preserves the Haar state of
G
and satisfies
\tau^{G}_{t}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}=\tau_{t}^{F}
. Given
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, Proposition
2.13

(2)
allows to use the universal property of the amalgamated free product
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
which implies that there exists a unique
\varphi_{\kappa}
-preserving automorphism
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}
of
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}|_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\tau_{t}^{G}
and
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau_{t}^{H}\otimes\tau_{t}^{F}
. Then, since
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}
is
\varphi_{\kappa}
-preserving and
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}=\tau_{t}^{F}
for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, using the universal property of the amalgamated free product
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
, there is a unique
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
-preserving automorphism
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
such that, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
,
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}=\widetilde{\tau}_{t}^{\kappa}
. It is not difficult to check that
(t\mapsto\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})
is a one parameter automorphism group of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
which is strongly continuous, by Lemma
2.2
.

In the sequel, we will also denote by
\rho\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
the unital normal
*
-homomorphism defined by
\rho(a)=\sum_{\kappa,i,j}e^{\kappa}_{ij}\otimes\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a)
, where:

\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a):=\sum_{r}(e^{\kappa}_{ri}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1_{B}\otimes a)(e^{\kappa}_{jr}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\in L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}).

Proposition 5.6
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))={\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, the scaling group of
\mathbb{G}
is
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}=\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}
and,
({\rm id}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\tau_{t}^{G}
and
({\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
5.4
, we note that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is generated by
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
and
\{\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a)\,:\,1\leq\kappa\leq K,\,1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa},\,a\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\}
.
Since
H\times F
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
one has, by definition of
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
,
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}(x)=(\tau_{t}^{H}\otimes\tau_{t}^{F})(x)=\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}(x)
for all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}(\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}(\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))=\sum_{r}(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}(e^{\kappa}_{ri})\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1_{B}\otimes\tau_{t}^{G}(a))(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}(e^{\kappa}_{jr})\otimes 1\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}(e^{\kappa}_{ri}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1_{B}\otimes\tau_{t}^{G}(a))(e^{\kappa}_{jr}\otimes 1\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(\tau_{t}^{G}(a)).

If follows that
\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))={\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. A similar computation gives

\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}(\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a))=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}(a)).

It follows that
({\rm id}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\tau_{t}^{G}
and
({\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}
.

Claim.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and all
X\in{\rm Pol}(G)\otimes B
one has
\beta_{G}\circ(\tau_{t}^{G}\otimes\sigma_{-t}^{\psi})(X)=(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})\circ\beta_{G}(X)
.

Proof of the Claim.

We may and will assume that
X=a\otimes b
, with
a\in{\rm Pol}(G)
and
b\in B
. Then, using the computation above and Proposition
2.11

(2.c)
we find:

\displaystyle\beta_{G}\circ(\tau_{t}^{G}\otimes\sigma_{-t}^{\psi})(X)

\displaystyle=\rho(\tau_{t}^{G}(a))\beta(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}(b))=(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})(\rho(a))(\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\tau_{t}^{H})(\beta(b))

\displaystyle=(\sigma^{\psi}_{-t}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})(\rho(a)\beta(b))=(\sigma^{\psi}_{-t}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})\circ\beta_{G}(a\otimes b).\qed

End of the proof of Proposition
5.6
.
To finish the proof, it suffices to show that, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, one has
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\circ\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}(x)=(\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})\circ\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x)
, for all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
and all
x\in L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
,
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. If
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
the equality is clear, since
H\times F
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
. Moreover, using the claim we find, for all
x\in{\rm Pol}(G)
,

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\circ\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})\rho(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}(x)=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})\rho(\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}(x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho)\Delta_{G}(\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}(x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho)(\tau_{t}^{G}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}})\Delta_{G}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id}\otimes{\rm id})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})(\tau_{t}^{G}\otimes\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})({\rm id}\otimes\rho)\Delta_{G}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})(\beta_{G}\otimes{\rm id})({\rm id}\otimes\rho)\Delta_{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm id}\otimes({\rm id}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}})\rho(x).

It finishes the proof.∎

We can now compute the invariant
T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G}):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}={\rm id}\}
of
\mathbb{G}
introduced in
[
KS24
]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

The map
\rho:{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is faithful.

Proof.

We first remark that the map restricted to
{\rm Pol}(G)
is faithful by the formula proved in Theorem
4.5
,

(\psi\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})\rho=h_{G},

and the fact that all the states are faithful. Moreover, on
{\rm Pol}(G)
, it is defined as

\rho(a)=\sum_{\kappa,ij}e_{ij}^{\kappa}\otimes\sum_{s=1}^{N_{\kappa}}\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{si}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\nu_{\kappa}(1\otimes a)\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{js}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\in B\otimes{\rm Pol}(\mathbb{G}),

therefore, the extension of
\rho
to
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is also normal, and therefore the intertwining of the states still holds, and implies that
\rho
is still faithful at the von Neumann algebraic level.
∎

Corollary 5.8
.

One has
T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}(G)\cap T^{\tau}(H)
.

Proof.

Since
H
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
, we get
T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})\subset T^{\tau}(H)
and, using the formulas
\tau^{H}_{t}(u^{ij,\kappa}_{kk,\gamma})=\left(\dfrac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}u_{kk,\gamma}^{ij,\kappa}
and
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}(b^{\kappa}_{ij})=\left(\frac{\lambda_{\kappa,i}}{\lambda_{\kappa,j}}\right)^{it}
we deduce that
T^{\tau}(H)\subset B^{\psi}
, where
B^{\psi}
denotes the centralizer of
\psi
. Fix
t\in T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})
then
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}={\rm id}
and,

\rho=({\rm id}\otimes\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\tau_{t}^{G}=\rho\circ\tau_{t}^{G}.

From this we get that
\tau_{t}^{G}={\rm id}
by faithfulness of the map
\rho
. Therefore
T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})\subset T^{\tau}(G)\cap T^{\tau}(H)
.
Assume now that
t\in T^{\tau}(G)\cap T^{\tau}(H)
, then
t\in B^{\psi}
and, using that
({\rm id}\otimes\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t})\rho=(\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes{\rm id})\rho\circ\tau_{t}^{G}=\rho
, we get that
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}
is trivial on the coefficients of
\rho
. Since
t\in T^{\tau}(H)
we deduce that
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}={\rm id}
.
∎

We can now explore various approximation properties of
\mathbb{G}
. To facilitate this study, the following remark will be instrumental.

Remark 5.9
.

View
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{*}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{*}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
, where the inclusion maps and the conditional expectations are the ones described before. Let us denote by
\nu_{\kappa}\,:\,B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and
\mu_{\kappa}\,:\,M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
,
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, the canonical inclusions. We also write
\mu\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
,
\mu(x):=\mu_{\kappa}(1\otimes x)
for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
and all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. Let
M\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
be the von Neumann subalgebra defined by:

M_{0}:=\left\{\mu_{\kappa}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))\,:\,1\leq\kappa\leq K\right\}^{\prime\prime}.

Remark that

M_{0}\simeq(\ast_{\kappa}M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C}),\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F).

Then we define inductively the algebras

M_{\kappa}:=\left(M_{\kappa-1}\cup\nu_{\kappa}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}),

for any
1\leq\kappa\leq K
.
Note that, by uniqueness of amalgamated free product of von Neumann algebras, one has that

M_{\kappa}\simeq M_{\kappa-1}\ast_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}\nu_{\kappa}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)),

where the amalgamated free products are with respect to the maps
\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id}_{F}
and the obvious corresponding conditional expectations. This also shows that we have

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\simeq M_{K}.

Observe that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is the von Neumann subalgebra generated by

\{\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a)\,:\,1\leq\kappa\leq K,1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa},a\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\}\cup\mu({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)),

where
\rho^{\kappa}_{ij}(a):=\sum_{r=1}^{N_{\kappa}}\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{ri}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\nu_{\kappa}(1\otimes a)\mu_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{jr}\otimes 1\otimes 1)
.

Theorem 5.10
.

The following holds.

(1)

If
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
has the Haagerup property then both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
have the Haagerup property. If moreover
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite, then
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
has the Haagerup property if and only if both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
have the Haagerup property.

(2)

Assume that
\mathbb{G}
is Kac (so that both
G
and
H
are Kac). If
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is hyperlinear then both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are hyperlinear. Moreover, if
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is amenable, then
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is hyperlinear if and only if both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are hyperlinear.

Proof.

(1)
. Assume that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
has the Haagerup property. Since
H
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is a subalgebra with expectation, by Lemma
2.5
. Therefore
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
has the Haagerup approximation property. By the decomposition of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
as a free product, we get for any
\kappa
a faithful conditional expectation onto the algebra
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, which implies that the algebra
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
and therefore
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})=L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
have the Haagerup approximation property. Since there is a conditional expectation
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
,
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
has the Haagerup property, and therefore
\widehat{G}
has it too.
For the converse, assume that
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite and that
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
have the Haagerup property, then we can apply
[
CKS+21
, Corollary 8.2]
to the decomposition of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
as an inductive amalgamated free product over
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
as described in Remark
5.9
. First, we remark that by the result of
[
CKS+21
, Corollary 8.2]
, the algebra
\ast_{\kappa}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C}),\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})
has the Haagerup property and therefore, if
\widehat{H}
has the Haagerup approximation property, then so does
M_{0}
. Moreover if
\widehat{G}
has the Haagerup property, then so does
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
, and an induction shows that
M_{K}\simeq{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
has the Haagerup property. Finally, using the conditional expectation
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, we get that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
has the Haagerup property.

(2)
. We already know thanks to Proposition
5.5
that
\mathbb{G}
is Kac if and only
G
and
H
are Kac. Under these condition,
h_{G},h_{H},\psi,h_{\mathbb{G}}
are all traces. Assume first that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is hyperlinear then, as
H
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
,
\widehat{H}
is also hyperlinear. Note that
B
being finite dimensional
(B,\psi)
is Connes embeddable (CE). Hence,
(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),\psi\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})
also is, by
[
Pop95
]
. Since the map
\rho\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is faithful and satisfies
(\psi\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})\rho=h_{G}
,
\widehat{G}
is hyperlinear. Conversely, assume that both
\widehat{G}
and
\widehat{H}
are hyperlinear and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is amenable. Then,
[
BDJ08
, Corollary 4.5]
implies that
*_{\kappa}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C}),\psi_{\kappa}^{-1})
is CE hence,
M_{0}
is CE. Applying
[
BDJ08
, Corollary 4.5]
(and since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is amenable) inductively in Remark
5.9
, we deduce that
({\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}),h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}})
is CE. Since the inclusion
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is state preserving, we deduce that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is hyperlinear.
∎

We now study coamenability. We need the following elementary Lemma.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
H
be a CQG. If
|{\rm Irr}(H)|=3
then
H\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{3}
or
H\simeq S_{3}
.

Proof.

Write
{\rm Irr}(H)=\{1,x_{1},x_{2}\}
, where
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
are non-trivial irreducibles. Since
x_{1}
is non-trivial we have either
\overline{x}_{1}=x_{1}
or
\overline{x}_{1}=x_{2}
. Assume that
\overline{x}_{1}=x_{1}
, so that
\overline{x}_{2}=x_{2}
. Hence,
\mathrm{Mor}(1,x_{1}\otimes x_{2})=0
and it follows that there exists
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0
such that
x_{1}\otimes x_{2}=n_{1}x_{1}+n_{2}x_{2}
. Also,
\dim\mathrm{Mor}(1,x_{k}\otimes x_{k})=1
,
\dim\mathrm{Mor}(x_{1},x_{2}\otimes x_{2})=\dim\mathrm{Mor}(x_{2},x_{1}\otimes x_{2})=n_{2}
and
\dim\mathrm{Mor}(x_{2},x_{1}\otimes x_{1})=\dim\mathrm{Mor}(x_{1},x_{1}\otimes x_{2})=n_{1}
. Hence, there exists
s,t\geq 0
such that
x_{1}\otimes x_{1}=1+sx_{1}+n_{1}x_{2}
and
x_{2}\otimes x_{2}=1+n_{2}x_{1}+tx_{2}
. It follows that
d_{1}^{2}=1+sd_{1}+n_{1}d_{2}
and
d_{2}^{2}=1+n_{2}d_{1}+td_{2}
, where
d_{k}:=\dim(x_{k})
. In particular,
{\rm gcd}(d_{1},d_{2})=1
. Note that
(n_{1},n_{2})
is a solution to the Diophantine equation
d_{1}d_{2}=n_{1}d_{1}+n_{2}d_{2}
and since
{\rm gcd}(d_{1},d_{2})=1
and
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 0
we deduce that
(n_{1},n_{2})\in\{(d_{2},0),(0,d_{1})\}
. Assume that
n_{1}=d_{2}
and
n_{2}=0
. Then,
d_{1}^{2}=1+sd_{1}+d_{2}^{2}
and
d_{2}^{2}=1+td_{2}
. From the equation
d_{2}^{2}-td_{2}-1=0
we deduce that
\Delta_{2}:=t^{2}+4
is a perfect square, which happens if and only if
t=0
. We conclude that
d_{2}=1
. From the equation
d_{1}^{2}-sd_{1}-2=0
we deduce that
\Delta_{1}:=s^{2}+8
is a perfect square, which happens if and only if
s=1
. Hence,
d_{1}=2
. It follows that
\dim(C(H))=1+d_{1}^{2}+d_{2}^{2}=6
. Assuming now that
n_{1}=0
and
n_{2}=d_{1}
, the same proof, exchanging the role of
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
also shows that
\dim(C(H))=6
. However, the only compact quantum groups whose associated C*-algebras have dimension
6
are
\mathbb{Z}_{6}
,
S_{3}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{3}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and its dual
\widehat{S_{3}}
.
\mathbb{Z}_{6}
and
\widehat{S_{3}}
both have
6
irreducibles, each of dimension
1
, and therefore
H\simeq S_{3}
. It remains to check the other case, that is
\overline{x}_{1}=x_{2}
and
\overline{x}_{2}=x_{1}
. Let
s,t\geq 0
be such that
x_{2}\otimes x_{1}=1+tx_{1}+sx_{2}
and write
d=\dim(x_{1})=\dim(x_{2})
.
Hence,
d^{2}=1+(t+s)d
and, this implies that
1=0
modulo
d
so
d=1
and
x_{2}\otimes x_{1}=1
. Letting
u\in\mathcal{U}(C(H))
the group like unitary
u:=x_{1}\neq 1
we have
u^{*}=x_{2}\neq u
so
u^{3}=1
and we deduce that
H=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
.
∎

We will also use the following well known Lemma.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
p\geq 2
be a integer. The following are equivalent.

(1)

p
is prime.

(2)

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
has, up to isomorphism, exactly 2 ergodic actions on a finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
:

•

B\simeq\mathbb{C}
and
\beta
is the trivial action,

•

B\simeq\mathbb{C}^{p}
and
\beta
is isomorphic to the action
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\curvearrowright C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
by left translation.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
. View
\beta\,:\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(B)\subset\mathcal{L}(B)
and write
\mathbb{Z}_{p}=\langle\sigma\rangle
where
\sigma^{p}=1
. Hence
\beta(\sigma)^{p}={\rm id}_{B}
so
{\rm Sp}(\beta(\sigma))\subseteq\{\xi^{k}\,:\,0\leq k\leq p-1\}
, where
\xi=e^{2i\pi/p}
and we may write
B=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{p-1}B_{k}
as vector spaces, where
B_{k}=\{b\in B:\beta(\sigma)b=\xi^{k}b\}
. Since
\beta
is ergodic we have
B_{0}=\mathbb{C}1_{B}
. Note that
B_{k}^{*}=B_{-k}
and
B_{k}B_{l}\subset B_{k+l}
, where the sum is modulo
p
. If
B_{1}\neq\{0\}
, take a nonzero
a\in B_{1}
. Then,
a^{*}a\in B_{0}=\mathbb{C}1
and is non-zero. Let
\lambda\neq 0
be such that
a^{*}a=\lambda 1
. Take
\lambda^{-1/2}
be any square root of
\lambda^{-1}
and define
u:=\lambda^{-1/2}a
. Then
u
satisfies
u^{*}u=1_{B}
and, since
B
is finite dimensional, we also have
uu^{*}=1
. Note that, for any
1\leq k\leq p-1
, any
b\in B_{k}
, one has
b(u^{k})^{*}\in B_{k}B_{-k}\subset B_{0}=\mathbb{C}1_{B}
which implies that
B_{k}=\mathbb{C}u^{k}
. We also have
u^{p}\in(B_{1})^{p}\subset B_{0}=\mathbb{C}1
. Hence, up to replacing
u
by a modulus one multiple of
u
, we may and will assume that
u^{p}=1
. Hence, the unique map
C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow B=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{p-1}\mathbb{C}u^{k}
which map
\sigma
onto
u
is an isomorphism intertwining the left translation on
C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with the action
\beta
. Assume now that
B_{1}=\{0\}
. Fix
2\leq k\leq p-1
and assume that
B_{k}\neq 0
. Let
a\in B_{k}
be non-zero and, as before, we may further assume by ergodicity that
a^{*}a=aa^{*}=1
. Then, by Fermat’s Little Theorem,
a^{k^{p-1}-1}\in B_{k}^{k^{p-1}-1}\subset B_{k^{p-1}}=B_{1}=\{0\}
. Hence,
a^{p^{k-1}-1}=0
and
a^{*}=(a^{*})^{p^{k-1}}a^{p^{k-1}-1}=0
so
a=0
a contradiction. We deduce that
B_{k}=0
for all
1\leq k\leq p-1
. So
B=\mathbb{C}1
and
\beta
is the trivial action.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
. if
p>2
is not prime write
p=nm
with
n,m\geq 2
and consider the ergodic action
\mathbb{Z}_{n}\curvearrowright\mathbb{C}^{n}=C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{n})
by left translation. Then, composing with the canonical surjection
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{n}
sending the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
on the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, we get an ergodic action
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\curvearrowright\mathbb{C}^{n}
. However, this action is not faithful so it can not be isomorphic to the left translation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, which is faithful.
∎

In the sequel we write
S_{3}=\langle\sigma,\tau|\sigma^{3}=1,\tau^{2}=1,\tau\sigma\tau^{-1}=\sigma^{-1}\rangle
. We view
\mathbb{Z}_{3}=\langle\sigma\rangle<S_{3}
so
C(\mathbb{Z}_{3})=C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{3})\subset C^{*}(S_{3})
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle\tau\rangle<S_{3}
so
C(\mathbb{Z}_{2})=C^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})\subset C^{*}(S_{3})
.

Lemma 5.13
.

There are, up to isomorphism, exactly
4
ergodic actions of
\widehat{S_{3}}
on a finite dimensional C*-algebra
B
:

•

The trivial action
\beta_{1}
on
B=\mathbb{C}
;

•

The ergodic action
\beta_{2}\,:\,\mathbb{C}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes C(\mathbb{Z}_{2})\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes C^{*}(S_{3})
on
B=\mathbb{C}^{2}
coming from the unique ergodic action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
.

•

The ergodic action
\beta_{3}\,:\,\mathbb{C}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes C(\mathbb{Z}_{3})\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}\otimes C^{*}(S_{3})
on
B=\mathbb{C}^{3}
coming from the unique ergodic action of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
.

•

The ergodic action
\beta_{4}:=\Delta_{\widehat{S_{3}}}\,:\,C^{*}(S_{3})\rightarrow C^{*}(S_{3})\otimes C^{*}(S_{3})
on
B=C^{*}(S_{3})
given by the comultiplication.

Proof.

Write
B=B_{1}\oplus B_{\sigma}\oplus B_{\sigma^{2}}\oplus B_{\tau}\oplus B_{\tau\sigma}\oplus B_{\sigma\tau}
, where
B_{g}:=\{b\in B\,:\,\beta(b)=b\otimes g\}
. The order
2
elements are
\{\tau,\tau\sigma,\sigma\tau\}
. Note that
A_{\sigma}:=B_{1}\oplus B_{\sigma}\oplus B_{\sigma^{2}}
is a subalgebra of
B
and
\beta(A_{\sigma})\subset A_{\sigma}\otimes C^{*}(\sigma)
so we get an ergodic action
\mathbb{Z}_{3}\curvearrowright A_{\sigma}
. Similarly, for any order two element
\omega\in\{\sigma,\tau\sigma,\sigma\tau\}
,
A_{\omega}:=B_{1}\oplus B_{\omega}
is a subalgebra with an ergodic action
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\curvearrowright A_{\omega}
.

Assume first that
\mathbb{Z}_{3}\curvearrowright A_{\sigma}
is ergodic then there is an element
u\in B_{\sigma}
such that
u^{3}=1
and
B_{\sigma}=\mathbb{C}u
,
B_{\sigma^{2}}=\mathbb{C}u^{2}
. Now, if for some
\omega\in\{\tau,\sigma\tau,\tau\sigma\}
the action
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\curvearrowright A_{\omega}
is also ergodic then we have
B_{\tau}=\mathbb{C}v
with some
v
satisfying
v^{2}=1
. Since
B_{g}B_{h}\subset B_{gh}
and
\langle\sigma,\omega\rangle=S_{3}
we deduce that
B_{g}\neq\{0\}
for all
g\in S_{3}
. It follows that
\dim(B)=6
and
\beta
is isomorphic to
\beta_{4}
. If now
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\curvearrowright A_{\omega}
is trivial for all
\omega\in\{\tau,\tau\sigma,\sigma\tau\}
. It follows that
\dim(B)=3
and
\beta
is isomorphic to
\beta_{3}
.

Assume now that
\mathbb{Z}_{3}\curvearrowright A_{\sigma}
is the trivial action. Since for any
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\{\tau,\tau\sigma,\sigma\tau\}
with
\omega_{1}\neq\omega_{2}
we have
\langle\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\rangle=S_{3}
and since
B_{\sigma}=\{0\}
, there at most one
\omega\in\{\tau,\sigma\tau,\tau\sigma\}
such that
\mathbb{Z}^{3}\curvearrowright B_{\omega}
is nontrivial. If there is such a
\omega
then
\beta
is isomorphic to
\beta_{2}
and if there is no such
\omega
then
\beta
is the trivial action
\beta_{1}
on
B=\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Note that if
G
is trivial then
\mathbb{G}=H
and if
H
is trivial then, since the action is ergodic, we must have
B=\mathbb{C}
so
\mathbb{G}=G
. In the non-trivial cases we obtain the following result concerning coamenability. Recall that
H\curvearrowright B
is always assumed to be ergodic with unique invariant state
\psi
.

Theorem 5.14
.

Assume that
F
is trivial and both
G
and
H
are non-trivial then the following are equivalent.

(1)

\mathbb{G}
is coamenable.

(2)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is amenable.

(3)

G\simeq H\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
B\in\{\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{2}\}
.

Moreover, in this case we have either
\mathbb{G}=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2}}
or
\mathbb{G}=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
follows from the general theory
[
Tom06
]
.

(2)\Rightarrow(3)
. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is amenable, each
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
is amenable. Write
C=B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
and
D=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
so that
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=C\underset{B}{*}D
. Let
p:=e^{\gamma}_{jj}\in B
be a minimal projection and note that
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}(p)=p
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Let us show that
pCp={\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
and
pDp=\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p)
are
*
-free in
pL_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})p
with respect to the state
\omega:=(\varphi_{\kappa}(p))^{-1}\varphi_{\kappa}(p\cdot p)
. Let
x\in pL_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})p
be a word alternating from
pCp
and
pDp
of the form
x=(pc_{1}p)(pd_{1}p)\dots(pc_{n}p)(pd_{n}p)
, with
c_{k}\in C
,
d_{k}\in D
and
\omega(pc_{k}p)=\omega(pd_{k}p)=0
. Since
p\in B
is a minimal projection, we have for
e_{k}\in\{c_{k},d_{k}\}
,
E_{B}(pe_{k}p)=pE_{B}(e_{k})p\in\mathbb{C}1_{B}
. Hence,
E_{B}(pe_{k}p)=\psi(E_{B}(pe_{k}p))1_{B}=\varphi_{\kappa}(pe_{k}p)1_{B}=0
. It follows that
x
is a reduced operator in the amalgamated free product
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=C\underset{B}{*}D
. Hence,
\varphi_{\kappa}(x)=0=\psi(p)\omega(x)
so
\omega(x)=0
. One proves similarly that
\omega(x)=0
for other alternating products
x
. This shows that
pCp*pDp\simeq\langle pCp,pDp\rangle\subset pL_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})p
, where the free product is with respect to the state
\omega
. Note that, since
p\in B^{\psi}\subset(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))^{\varphi_{\kappa}}
, one has
\sigma_{t}^{\omega}(pxp)=p\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}(x)p
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\in L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. In particular,
\sigma^{\omega}_{t}(pCp)=pCp
and
\sigma^{\omega}_{t}(pDp)=pDp
. Hence,
\sigma_{t}^{\omega}(\langle pCp,pDp\rangle)=\langle pCp,pDp\rangle
and, by Takesaki’s Theorem, the subalgebra
\langle pCp,pDp\rangle\subset pL_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})p
has a faithful normal conditional expectation. Hence,
pCp*pDp={\rm L}^{\infty}(G)*\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p)
is amenable. Note that if
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is infinite dimensional, then
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is diffuse by
[
FT24
, Lemma 2.4]
. Hence,
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is also diffuse and
\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p)
must be infinite dimensional. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\neq\mathbb{C}
, the results of
[
Ued11
]
imply that
pCp*pDp
is not amenable. Hence,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
is finite dimensional. Let
q_{x}
be the minimal central projections of
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
, which are parameterized by
x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})
and note that
\varepsilon_{H}(a)q_{1}=aq_{1}
for all
a\in C(H)
, where
1\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})
is the trivial representation. Note that

\dim(\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p))\geq 2.

Indeed, if the dimension is
1
, then
\beta_{\kappa}(p)
is a minimal projection and it implies that
\beta_{\kappa}(p)(1_{\kappa}\otimes p_{1})=\beta_{\kappa}(p)
. Hence,

\beta_{\kappa}(p)=\beta_{\kappa}(p)(1_{\kappa}\otimes p_{1})=({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{H})(\beta_{\kappa}(p)(1_{\kappa}\otimes p_{1}))=\chi_{\kappa}(p)\otimes p_{1}=\delta_{\gamma,\kappa}(p\otimes p_{1}).

Since
\beta_{\kappa}
is faithful, we deduce that
K=1
and
\beta(p)=p\otimes p_{1}
. Applying
\psi\otimes{\rm id}
we get
\psi(p)1=\psi(p)p_{1}
so
p_{1}=1
and
H
is trivial, a contradiction.

Since
pCp,pDp\neq\mathbb{C}
and
pCp*pDp
is amenable, the results of
[
Ued11
]
imply that:

\dim({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\leq 2\text{ and }\dim(\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p))\leq 2.

We deduce that
\dim({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))=2=\dim(\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p))
which implies that
G\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Hence both
G
and
H
are Kac, by
[
VD97
, Theorem 2.2]
. Hence,
\mathbb{G}
is Kac and, since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is amenable and
\mathbb{G}
is Kac,
[
Tom06
]
implies that
\mathbb{G}
is amenable so
G^{*K}
, as a compact quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
, is also amenable.
[
FT24
, Proposition 2.8]
implies that
K\leq 2
. Write

\beta(p)=\sum_{x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})}\beta(p)(1\otimes q_{x}).

Write, for
1\leq\kappa\leq K
,
I_{\kappa}:=\{x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H}):\beta_{\kappa}(p)(1\otimes q_{x})\neq 0\}
and note that, since
(q_{x})
are central and pairwise orthogonal,
\{\beta_{\kappa}(p)(1\otimes q_{x}):x\in I_{\kappa}\}
is a family of non-zero pairwise orthogonal projections in
\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p)
. Hence,

|I_{\kappa}|\leq\dim(\beta_{\kappa}(p)(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H))\beta_{\kappa}(p))=2\text{ for all }1\leq\kappa\leq K.

Note that
{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})=\cup_{\kappa=1}^{K}I_{\kappa}
. Indeed, writing
I:=\{x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H}):\beta(p)(1\otimes p_{x})\neq 0\}
one has:

\psi(p)1=(\psi\otimes{\rm id})(\beta(p))=\sum_{x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})}(\psi\otimes{\rm id})(\beta(p)(1\otimes q_{x}))=\sum_{x\in I}\psi(p)q_{x}.

Since
\psi(p)>0
it follows that
\sum_{x\in I}p_{x}=1
so
I={\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})
. Let
x\in{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})
, since
\beta(p)(1\otimes q_{x})\neq 0
, there exists
\kappa
such that
x\in I_{\kappa}
and this shows that
{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})=\cup_{\kappa=1}^{K}I_{\kappa}
.

If
K=1
this shows that
|{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})|=2
. Arguing as in the proof of
[
FT24
, Proposition 2.8]
, we deduce that
\widehat{H}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Hence,
H\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

If
K=2
. Since
({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{H})\beta={\rm id}_{B}
, the trivial representation
1
is in
I_{1}\cap I_{2}
. Hence,
|{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})|=|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|-|I_{1}\cap I_{2}|\leq|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|-1\leq 3
so either
|{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})|=2
in which case we deduce again that
H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
or
|{\rm Irr}(\widehat{H})|=3
and Lemma
5.11
implies that
\widehat{H}\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{3},S_{3}\}
so
H\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{3},\widehat{S}_{3}\}
. In conclusion, we have
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and either
K=1
and
H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
or
K=2
and
H\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{2},\mathbb{Z}_{3},\widehat{S_{3}}\}
. However, in the case
K=2
and
H=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
, there is no ergodic action of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
on
B
, by Lemma
5.12
. If
K=2
and
H=\widehat{S_{3}}
then, by Lemma
5.13
we have
B=\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
\beta
is the action
\mathbb{C}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes C(\mathbb{Z}_{2})=\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes C^{*}(\omega)\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes C^{*}(S_{3})
coming from the unique ergodic action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
and an order two element
\omega\in S_{3}
. We deduce from Proposition
3.8
that
\mathbb{G}\simeq(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\wr_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{2})\underset{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}{*}\widehat{S_{3}}
. By Proposition
3.11
and
[
FT24
, Proposition 2.23]
we have
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\wr_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Hence,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=({\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})\otimes{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}))\underset{{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})}{*}{\rm L}(S_{3})
, where the amalgamated free product is with respect to the conditional expectations on the dual quantum subgroup
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Write
S_{3}=\langle\sigma,\tau|\sigma^{3}=1,\tau^{2}=1,\tau\sigma\tau=\sigma^{-1}\rangle
and let
p:=\frac{1}{2}(1+\tau)\in{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
and note that
p=p^{*}=p^{2}
and
p\tau=p
so
p
is a minimal projection in
{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. However,
p
is not minimal in
{\rm L}(S_{3})
since
p{\rm L}(S_{3})p=\mathbb{C}p\oplus\mathbb{C}p\sigma p
. It is easy to check that
{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})=p({\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})\otimes{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}))p
and
p{\rm L}(S_{3})p
are
*
-free in
p{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
with respect to
h_{\mathbb{G}}(p)^{-1}h_{\mathbb{G}}|_{p{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}
. Hence,
{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})*p{\rm L}(S_{3})p
is isomorphic to a von Neumann algebra of
p{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
which is impossible since
{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})
is diffuse and
\dim(p{\rm L}(S_{3})p)\geq 2
so
{\rm L}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2})*p{\rm L}(S_{3})p
is not amenable. It follows that the only possible case is
G=H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and Lemma
5.12
implies that
B\in\{\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{2}\}
.

(3)\Rightarrow(1)
. If
G=H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
B=\mathbb{C}
then
\beta
is the trivial action on
\mathbb{C}
and it follows from Remark
3.7
that
\mathbb{G}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2}}
which is coamenable. If
B=\mathbb{C}^{2}
then Lemma
5.12
implies that
\beta
is the unique ergodic action on
B=\mathbb{C}^{2}
so that it is isomorphic to the universal action
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=S_{2}^{+}\curvearrowright\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Proposition
3.11
and
[
FT24
, Proposition 2.23]
imply that
\mathbb{G}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{*2}}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which is coamenable by
[
FT24
, Proposition 2.17]
.
∎

Remark 5.15
.

The fact that, for
G
a CQG of Kac type, the coamenability of
G
is equivalent to the amenability of the von Neumann algebra
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is well known (see Corollary 3.17 in
[
Tom06
]
and the discussion after its proof) and was used in the previous proof. However, the equivalence for general CQG is open. Theorem
5.14
shows that in the class of CQG which are non-amalgamated generalized free wreath product of non-trivial CQG with ergodic action, the equivalence between coamenability and amenability of the von Neumann algebra is true.
