Since this class of CQG contains in particular the class of free products of non-trivial CQG, it extends
[
FT24
, Remark 2.9]
.

Remark 5.16
.

The ergodicity is necessary to obtain
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
in Theorem
5.14
. Indeed, consider the free action
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\curvearrowright\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and let
B=M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
with
N\geq 2
and
B^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\oplus M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
with the action
\beta\,:\,\mathbb{Z}_{2}\curvearrowright B^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
defined before Proposition
3.9
. This action is non-ergodic and preserves the faithful state
\psi
defined from any faithful state
\omega\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
before Proposition
3.9
. Let
\mathbb{G}
be the free wreath product
\mathbb{G}:=G\wr_{*,\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Note that, by Proposition
3.9
,
\mathbb{G}\simeq G^{*2}\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Hence,
\mathbb{G}
is coamenable if and only if
G
is trivial or
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, by
[
FT24
, Propositions 2.8 and 2.17]
. Hence, taking
G=H=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, we obtain a coamenable free wreath product
\mathbb{G}
but with
B=M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\oplus M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
.

6.
Qualitative properties of the von Neumann algebra

We will now study the main properties of the von Neumann algebra of a generalized free wreath product: factoriality, type, Connes’ invariant
T
, primeness and absence of Cartan subalgebra. We start with a preliminary section concerning intertwining theory.

6.1.
Intertwining theory

We use
[
HI17
]
as a reference concerning basics on intertwining theory. Let
1_{A}\in M
be a projection. A von Neumann subalgebra
A\subset 1_{A}M1_{A}
is called
with expectation
if there is a faithful normal conditional expectation
1_{A}M1_{A}\rightarrow A
.

Remark 6.1
.

If
A\subset M
is with expectation then its relative commutant
A^{\prime}\cap M
, the von Neumann algebra
A\vee(A^{\prime}\cap M)\subset M
generated by
A
and
A^{\prime}\cap M
and its normalizer algebra
\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}\subset M
, where
\mathcal{N}_{M}(A):=\{v\in\mathcal{U}(M)\,:\,vAv^{*}=A\}
, are all with expectation. Indeed, fix a faithful normal conditional expectation
E_{A}\,:\,M\rightarrow A
, fix a f.n.s.
\psi\in A_{*}
and define the f.n.s.
\psi\in M_{*}
by
\psi:=\psi\circ E_{A}
. Since
A
is globally invariant under the modular group
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
, it is easy to see that
A^{\prime}\cap M
also is. It implies that
A\vee(A^{\prime}\cap M)
is globally invariant under
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
. Moreover, it is shown in
[
BHV18
, Lemma 4.1]
that, for all
u\in\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, one has

u^{*}\sigma_{t}(u)\in A\vee(A^{\prime}\cap M)\subset\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime},

In particular,
\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}
is globally invariant under
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}
. It follows from Takesaki’s Theorem that there exists a unique
\psi
-preserving conditional expectation from
M
to
A^{\prime}\cap M
, from
M
to
A\vee(A^{\prime}\cap M)
and from
M
to
\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}
, these conditional expectations are moreover normal and faithful.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
A\subset 1_{A}M1_{A}
and
B\subset 1_{B}M1_{B}
be von Neumann subalgebras with expectation. We say that
A

embeds in

B

inside

M
and we write
A\prec_{M}B
if there exist projections
e\in A
,
f\in B
a non-zero element
v\in eMf
and a unital, normal
*
-morphism
\theta:eAe\rightarrow fBf
such that the inclusion
\theta(eAe)\subset fBf
is with expectation and
av=v\theta(a)
for all
a\in eAe
.

Remark 6.3
.

Let
A\subset 1_{A}M1_{A}
and
B\subset 1_{B}M1_{B}
be von Neumann subalgebras with expectations.

(1)

If
A
is diffuse and
B
is finite dimensional then
A\nprec_{M}B
. Indeed, assume that
A\prec_{M}B
, it follows from
[
HI17
, Remark 4.2 (3)]
that there exist projections
e\in A
,
f\in B
, a non-zero element
v\in eMf
and a
faithful
unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\theta\,:\,eAe\rightarrow fBf
such that
av=v\theta(a)
for all
a\in eAe
. If
B=\mathbb{C}1_{B}
then, since
\theta
is faithful,
eAe=\mathbb{C}e
and
e
is minimal. In general, since
fBf
is finite dimensional and
v\in eMf
, there exists a minimal (and central) projection
q\in fBf
such that
vq\neq 0
. Consider
w=vq\in eMq
and
\psi\,:\,eAe\rightarrow qBq=\mathbb{C}q
,
\psi(a)=\theta(a)q=q\theta(a)
. Then,
w\psi(a)=vq\theta(a)=v\theta(a)q=avq=aw
. Hence,
A\prec_{M}\mathbb{C}
and we can apply the first part of the proof.

(2)

Let
p\in\mathcal{Z}(M)
be a projection. Then,
Ap\subset 1_{A}Mp1_{A}
and
Bp\subset 1_{B}Mp1_{B}
are with expectation and if
Ap\prec_{Mp}Bp
then
A\prec_{M}B
(see
[
HI17
, Remark 4.2 (2)]
).

(3)

A\prec_{M}B
if and only if
A\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})\prec_{M\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})}B\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})
for some
k\geq 1
(or for any
k\geq 1
). Indeed, assume that for some
k\geq 1
one has
\mathcal{A}\prec_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{B}
, where
\mathcal{A}:=A\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})
,
\mathcal{B}:=B\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathcal{M}:=M\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})
and consider the projection
p:=1_{A}\otimes e_{11}\in\mathcal{A}
. Note that its central support in
\mathcal{A}
is
z_{\mathcal{A}}(1_{A}\otimes e_{11})=1_{A}\otimes 1
. It follows from
[
HI17
, Remark 4.2 (4)]
that
p\mathcal{A}p=A\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}\prec_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{B}
. The same argument, by using this time
[
HI17
, Remark 4.5]
, shows that
A\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}\prec_{\mathcal{M}}B\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}
. Hence, there exist projections
e\in A
,
f\in B
, a non-zero
w\in(e\otimes e_{11})(M\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C}))(f\otimes e_{11})=eMf\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}
and a unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\psi\,:\,eAe\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}\rightarrow fBf\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}
such that
\psi(eAe\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11})\subset fBf\otimes\mathbb{C}e_{11}
is with expectation and
(a\otimes e_{11})w=w\psi(a\otimes e_{11})
for all
a\in eAe
. Let
\theta\,:\,eAe\rightarrow fBf
be the unique unital normal
*
-homomorphism such that
\psi(a\otimes e_{11})=\theta(a)\otimes e_{11}
for all
a\in eAe
and
v\in eMf
be the unique (non-zero) element such that
w=v\otimes e_{11}
. Then
\theta(eAe)\subset fBf
is with expectation and
av=v\theta(a)
for all
a\in eAe
. For the converse, it is clear from Definition
6.2
that
A\prec_{M}B
implies
A\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})\prec_{M\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})}B\otimes M_{k}(\mathbb{C})
for all
k\geq 1
.

(4)

If
A\prec_{M}B
then, for any
unital

D\subset A
with expectation one has
D\prec_{M}B
by
[
HI17
, Lemma 4.8]
.

We will freely use the following result.

Proposition 6.4
.

[
HI17
, Theorem 4.3 and Lemma 4.10]

Let
A\subset 1_{A}M1_{A}
and
B\subset 1_{B}M1_{B}
with expectations and suppose that
A
is finite or type
{\rm III}
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

A\prec_{M}B
.

(2)

There exist
n\geq 1
, a projection
q\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes B
, a non-zero partial isometry
w\in(e_{11}\otimes 1_{A})(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M)q
and a unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\pi\,:\,A\rightarrow q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes B)q
such that
\pi(A)\subset q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes B)q
is with expectation and
(1\otimes a)w=w\pi(a)
for all
a\in A
.

If
A
is finite
(1)
and
(2)
are also equivalent to:

(3)

There is no net of unitaries
(w_{n})_{n}
in
\mathcal{U}(A)
such that
E_{B}(x^{*}w_{n}y)\rightarrow 0
in the
\sigma
-strong topology, for all
x,y\in 1_{A}M1_{B}
.

Moreover, for any
A
(not necessarily finite or type
{\rm III}
), we have
(2)\Rightarrow(3)
.

Remark 6.5
.

Let
A\subset 1_{A}M1_{A}
and
B\subset 1_{B}M1_{B}
with expectations and
\varphi
a f.n.s. on
A
. If there exists a net of unitaries
(w_{n})_{n}
in
\mathcal{U}(A^{\varphi})
such that
E_{B}(x^{*}w_{n}y)\rightarrow 0
in the
\sigma
-strong topology, for all
x,y\in 1_{A}M1_{B}
then,
A\nprec_{M}B
. Indeed, by Proposition
6.4
applied to the finite von Neumann algebra
A^{\varphi}
, we deduce that
A^{\varphi}\nprec_{M}B
. Note that
A^{\varphi}\subset A
is unital and with expectation (by Takesaki’s Theorem). Hence, Remark
6.3

(4)
implies that
A\nprec_{M}B
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
M
be a von Neumann algebra and
(u_{n})_{n}
a sequence in
M
going to
0

\sigma
-weakly. Then, for any finite dimensional subalgebra
N\subset M
and any normal conditional expectation
E\,:\,M\rightarrow N
,
E(xu_{n}y)\rightarrow 0
in the operator norm topology, for any
x,y\in M
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in N_{*}
be a faithful state and fix an orthonormal basis
(e_{i})_{i}
of
N
for the
\varphi
scalar product. Then,
E(a)=\sum_{i}\varphi(e_{i}^{*}E(a))e_{i}=\sum_{i}\varphi\circ E(e_{i}^{*}a)e_{i}
. For all
x,y\in M
,

\displaystyle\|E(xu_{n}y)\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i}\varphi\circ E(e_{i}^{*}xu_{n}y)e_{i}\right\|\leq\sum_{i}|\varphi\circ E(e_{i}^{*}xu_{n}y)|\cdot\|e_{i}\|,

and this goes to
0
, because
u_{n}
goes to
0
weakly and the sum is finite. The same holds for the conjugate, because
a\mapsto a^{*}
is weakly continuous. It concludes the proof.∎

Remark 6.7
.

The previous Lemma applies in particular when
M
is diffuse,
\varphi
is almost periodic. In that case
M^{\varphi}
is diffuse and the Lemma shows that there exists a sequence of unitaries
(u_{n})_{n}
in
M^{\varphi}
such that, for any finite dimensional subalgebra
N
of
M
and any normal conditional expectation
E\,:\,M\rightarrow N
,
E(xu_{n}y)\rightarrow 0
in norm.

The following Lemma is
[
HU16a
, Lemma 2.6]
and
[
HU16b
, Lemma 2.7]
.

Lemma 6.8
.

[
HU16a
, Lemma 2.6]

[
HU16b
, Lemma 2.7]

Let
M=(M_{1},E_{1})\ast_{N}(M_{2},E_{2})
be an amalgamated free product and
\psi\in M_{*}
a f.n.s. such that
\psi\circ E_{M_{k}}=\psi
for
k=1,2
. If
(w_{n})_{n}
is a net in the unit ball of
(M_{1})^{\psi}
such that
E_{1}(xw_{n}y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in M_{1}
then,

(1)

E_{M_{1}}(xw_{n}y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in M\ominus M_{1}
.

(2)

E_{M_{2}}(xw_{n}y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in M
.

The following Lemma is
[
HU16a
, Proposition 2.7]
but we need it in the amalgamated case, with a finite dimensional amalgam. A more general statement could be given but we choose to include all the hypotheses we will have later so that we can write a shorter proof. Recall that, by Remark
6.1
, if
Q\subset M
is with expectation then,
\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}\subset M
also is.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
M=M_{1}\ast_{N}M_{2}
with
N
is finite dimensional. Let
Q\subset M
be any finite or type
{\rm III}
diffuse von Neumann subalgebra with expectation. If
Q\prec_{M}M_{1}
then
\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M}M_{1}
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 1
, a projection
q\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1}
, a non-zero partial isometry
w\in(M_{1,n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M)q
and a unital normal
*
-homomorphism
\pi\,:\,Q\rightarrow q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})q
such that
\pi(Q)\subset q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})q
is with expectation and
xw=w\pi(x)
for all
x\in Q
.

Claim:

One has
w^{*}w\in qM_{n}(M_{1})q
and
w^{*}\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}w\subset w^{*}wM_{n}(M_{1})w^{*}w
.

Proof of the Claim.
We work in the amalgamated free product:

M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M=M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1}\underset{M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes N}{*}M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{2},

with respect to the conditional expectation
\widetilde{E}_{k}:={\rm id}_{M_{n}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes E_{k}
,
k=1,2
.
Since
\pi(Q)
is diffuse we may apply
[
HU16a
, Lemma 2.1]
to get a f.n.s.
\varphi
on
\pi(Q)
with diffuse centralizer
\pi(Q)^{\varphi}
and note that
q=\pi(1)\in\pi(Q)^{\varphi}
. Define the normal state
\psi_{1}:=\varphi\circ E(q\,\cdot\,q)\in(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})_{*}
, where
E\,:\,q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})q\rightarrow Q
is any faithful normal conditional expectation. Fix any f.n.s.
\omega\in[(1-q)M(1-q)]_{*}
and define the f.n.s.
\psi_{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{1}(\cdot)+\frac{1}{2}\omega((1-q)\cdot(1-q))\in(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})_{*}
. Then, the f.n.s.
\psi:=\psi_{2}\circ({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})\in(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M)_{*}
satisfies the following:
\psi=\psi\circ({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})
,
q\in(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})^{\psi}
and
\pi(Q)\cap q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})^{\psi}q
is diffuse. By Lemma
6.6
there is a sequence of unitaries
(u_{k})_{k}\in\pi(Q)\cap q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})^{\psi}q
such that
\widetilde{E}_{1}(xu_{k}y)\rightarrow 0
in norm, for all
x,y\in q(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})q
. Viewing the sequence
(u_{k})_{k}
in the unit ball of
(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})^{\psi}
we can apply Lemma
6.8
and deduce that
({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(xu_{k}y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly, for all
x,y\in q(M_{n}(M)\ominus M_{n}(M_{1}))q
. Fix a normalizing unitary
x\in\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)
and observe that, for all
a\in Q
, one has
w^{*}xw\pi(a)=w^{*}xaw=\pi(xax^{*})w^{*}xw
. Define the element
v:=w^{*}xw-({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(w^{*}xw)\in qM_{n}(M)q
and note that
({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(v)=0
. Moreover, for all
a\in Q
, one has
v\pi(a)=\pi(xax^{*})v
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, fix a unitary
a_{k}\in Q
such that
\pi(a_{k})=u_{k}
. Then, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, since
\pi(xa_{k}x^{*})
is a unitary in
\pi(Q)
,

\displaystyle\|({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(vv^{*})\|_{\psi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\pi(xa_{k}x^{*})({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(vv^{*})\|_{\psi}=\|({\rm id}\otimes E_{M_{1}})(vu_{k}v^{*})\|_{\psi}\rightarrow_{k}0.

It follows that
vv^{*}=0
so
w^{*}xw\in M_{n}(M_{1})
for all
x\in\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)
. This proves the Claim. ∎

End of the proof of Lemma
6.9
.
By Remark
6.3

(2)
, it suffices to show that

M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M}M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1}.

Since
xw=w\pi(x)
for all
x\in Q
, we deduce that
ww^{*}\in Q^{\prime}\cap M\subset\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}
. Define the projections
e:=e_{11}\otimes ww^{*}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}
,
f:=w^{*}w\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1}
and note that, since
w
is a partial isometry we have, viewing
w\in(e_{11}\otimes 1)(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M)
,

ew=(e_{11}\otimes ww^{*})w=ww^{*}w=w\text{ and }wf=ww^{*}w=w

so
w\in e(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M)f
. By the Claim, we have a unital normal
*
-homomorphism

\theta\,:\,e(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathcal{N}_{N}(Q)^{\prime\prime})e=\mathbb{C}e_{11}\otimes ww^{*}\mathcal{N}_{N}(Q)^{\prime\prime}ww^{*}\rightarrow w^{*}w(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})w^{*}w

defined by
\theta(e_{11}\otimes x)=w^{*}xw
for all
x\in ww^{*}\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}ww^{*}
. Moreover,
w\theta(x)=ww^{*}xw=xw
for all
x\in ww^{*}\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}ww^{*}
. Finally, note that the image of
\theta
is exactly
w^{*}\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}w
and, given any faithful normal conditional expectation
E\,:\,M\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}
, the map

\widetilde{E}\,:\,w^{*}w(M_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M_{1})w^{*}w\rightarrow w^{*}\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}w,\quad\widetilde{E}(x):=w^{*}E(wxw^{*})w

is obviously a normal faithful conditional expectation. ∎

We also record the following result for later use.

Lemma 6.10
.

Special case of
[
HI20
, Theorem 5.2]
Let
M=M_{1}\underset{N}{*}M_{2}
and
Q\subset M_{1}
a unital subalgebra with expectation. If
Q\nprec_{M_{1}}N
then
\mathcal{N}_{M}(Q)^{\prime\prime}\subset M_{1}
.

Proof.

Since
Q\subset M_{1}
is unital with expectation and
Q\nprec_{M_{1}}N
we deduce from Remark
6.3

(2)
that
M_{1}\nprec_{M_{1}}N
so that we can directly apply
[
HI20
, Theorem 5.2]
.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
M=M_{1}\underset{N}{*}M_{2}
with
N
finite dimensional. Then, for any finite diffuse and amenable subalgebra
A\subset M
with expectation, one of the following statements holds:

•

There is a
k\in\{1,2\}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{M}M_{k}
,

•

\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}
is amenable.

Proof.

Assume that
P:=\mathcal{N}_{M}(A)^{\prime\prime}\nprec_{M}M_{k}
for
k=1,2
and let us show that
P
is amenable. By Lemma
6.9
we have
A\nprec M_{k}
for
k=1,2
. Hence, we may apply
[
HU16b
, Theorem 4.4]
and deduce that
A^{\prime}\cap M
is amenable. Let
\mathcal{A}:=A\vee(A^{\prime}\cap M)\subset M
and note that
\mathcal{A}
is amenable and diffuse. Also, by Remark
6.1
,
\mathcal{A}\subset M
is with expectation. Define
\mathcal{P}:=\mathcal{N}_{M}(\mathcal{A})^{\prime\prime}
and note that
P\subset\mathcal{P}
. By Remark
6.1

P\subset M
is with expectation so
P\subset\mathcal{P}
is with expectation and it suffices to show that
\mathcal{P}
is amenable. Note that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\cap M\subset A\subset\mathcal{A}
and since
\mathcal{A}
is diffuse and
N
is finite dimensional,
\mathcal{A}\nprec_{M}N
. Also, since the inclusion
P\subset\mathcal{P}
is unital with expectation, Remark
6.3

(2)
implies that
\mathcal{P}\nprec_{M}M_{k}
for
k=1,2
. Note that the statement of
[
Iso22
, Theorem G]
is still valid, using normalizers instead of stable normalizers (and the proof is even easier, by using
[
HU16b
, Theorem A.4]
instead of
[
Iso22
, Lemma 6.1]
). Hence, we deduce that
\mathcal{P}
is amenable.∎

The following Theorem is
[
HI20
, Theorem A]
. We state a slightly more general version for our purpose. An inclusion of von Neumann algebras
N\subset M
is called
entirely non-trivial
if for any non-zero projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(N)
one has
Nz\neq Mz
(as sets). Note that if
N\subset M
has expectation and if
M\nprec_{M}N
then the inclusion
N\subset M
is entirely non-trivial.

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
M=\underset{N,1\leq i\leq K}{*}M_{i}
be an amalgamated free product. Suppose that there exists
1\leq i\neq j\leq K
such that
M_{i}\nprec_{M_{i}}N
and the inclusion
N\subset M_{j}
is entirely non trivial. Then, the following holds.

(1)

\mathcal{Z}(M)=M^{\prime}\cap N
.

(2)

For all f.n.s.
\varphi\in M_{*}
such that
\varphi=\varphi\circ E_{N}
, we have

T(M):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(N)\text{ such that }\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}={\rm Ad}(u)\}.

(3)

M
is semifinite if and only if there exists a faithful normal trace
{\rm Tr}_{N}
on
N
such that, for all
1\leq k\leq K
,
{\rm Tr}\circ E_{k}
is a trace on
M_{k}
.

(4)

For every non-zero projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(M)
,
Mz
is not amenable relative to
M_{i}
inside
M
. In particular,
Mz
is not amenable.

Proof.

We write
M=M_{i}\underset{N}{*}M_{i}^{\prime}
. Let us show that the inclusion
N\subset M_{i}^{\prime}
is entirely non trivial. If there is a non-zero projection
z\in\mathcal{Z}(N)
such that
M_{i}^{\prime}z=Nz
then
M_{j}z\subset M_{i}^{\prime}z=Nz
which implies that
M_{j}z=Nz
. Since
N\subset M_{j}
is entirely non-trivial, we deduce that
N\subset M_{i}^{\prime}
also is. Hence, we may apply
[
HI20
, Theorem A]
to deduce
(1)
,
(2)
and
(4)
. It remains to show
(3)
for which we can repeat the proof of
[
Ued13
, Theorem 4.3 (3)]
: fix any faithful normal state
\omega\in N_{*}
and consider the state
\varphi:=\omega\circ E_{N}
. Then,
M
is semifinite if and only if there exists one-parameter group of unitaries
u_{t}\in M
such that
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}={\rm Ad}(u_{t})
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Fix
i
such that
M_{i}\nprec_{M_{i}}N
. Applying Lemma
6.10
we deduce that
u_{t}\in M_{i}
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Let now
j\neq i
be such that
N\subset M_{j}
is entirely non-trivial. Let
y\in M_{j}
such that
E_{j}(y)=0
and note that
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)(u_{t}-E_{i}(u_{t}))+\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)E_{i}(u_{t})=\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)u_{t}=u_{t}y=E_{i}(u_{t})y+(u_{t}-E_{i}(u_{t}))y
. Writing
v:=u_{t}-E_{i}(u_{t})
we have, by freeness of
M_{i}
and
M_{j}
and since
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)\in\ker(E_{j})
and
v\in\ker(E_{i})
,

E_{N}(vyy^{*}v^{*})=E_{N}(\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)vy^{*}v^{*})+E_{N}(\sigma_{t}^{\varphi}(y)E_{i}(u_{t})y^{*}v^{*})-E_{i}(u_{t})E_{N}(yy^{*}v^{*})=0.

It follows that
E_{N}(vyy^{*}v^{*})=E_{N}(vE_{j}(yy^{*})v^{*})=0
and since
E_{N}
is faithful,
vE_{j}(yy^{*})=0
, for all
y\in\ker(E_{j})
. Hence,
vs(E_{j}(yy^{*}))=0
, for all
y\in\ker(E_{j})
, where
s(z)
denotes the support projection of the self-adjoint operator
z
. By
[
Ued13
, Lemma 4.2 (3)]
, there is a family
(y_{i})_{i\in I}
of elements in
\ker(E_{j})
such that
\sum_{i\in i}s(E_{j}(y_{i}y_{i}^{*}))=1
. It implies that
v=0
i.e.
u_{t}=E_{i}(u_{t})\in N
. Write
u_{t}=h^{it}
with
h
a positive non-singular operator affiliated with the centralizer
N^{\omega}\subset N
of
\sigma_{t}^{\omega}
. Then, the semifinite faithful normal weight
\omega_{h^{-1}}
on
N
is the desired trace.
∎

6.2.
Factoriality and type

We fix an ergodic action
\beta\,:\,H\curvearrowright B
with unique invariant faithful state
\psi\in B^{*}
. Let’s begin with the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.13
.

Suppose that
{\rm Irr}(G)
is infinite and
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite then, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, and all projection
q\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
one has:

L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})(1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes q)\nprec_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})(1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes q)}{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q.

Proof.

Since
{\rm Irr}(G)
is infinite, it follows from
[
FT24
, Lemma 2.4]
that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is diffuse. Hence,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
is also diffuse. Moreover, since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
is a finite dimensional subalgebra and
h_{G}^{\prime}:=h_{G}(q)^{-1}h_{G}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}
is an almost periodic state on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
(note that
q
is in the centralizer of
h_{G}
), Remark
6.7
implies that there exists a sequence of unitaries
(u_{n})_{n}
in
({\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)^{h^{\prime}_{G}}
such that
E_{F}(xu_{n}y)\rightarrow 0
in norm, for all
x,y\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
. Considering the amalgamated free product:

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))(1\otimes q)\simeq(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}{*}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle M_{1}\underset{N}{*}M_{2},

Lemma
6.8

(2)
implies that
E_{M_{2}}(x(1\otimes u_{n})y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strong for all
x,y\in M
. We conclude from Remark
6.5
that
M\nprec_{M}M_{2}
. Remark
6.3

(3)
implies that

L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})(1\otimes q)\nprec_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})(1\otimes q)}{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q.\qed

Lemma 6.14
.

Suppose that
\beta
is
2
-ergodic,
{\rm Irr}(G)
and
{\rm Irr}(H)
are infinite and
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite. For any
1\leq\kappa\leq K
such that
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
one has:

(1)

L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
is has no amenable direct summand and:

\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))=\mathbb{C}1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)).

(2)

For all non-zero projection
p\in\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
one has:

T(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p)=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)\}\cap\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}\},

where
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is the unique projection such that
p=1\otimes q
.

Proof.

(1)
. It suffices to prove the statement for
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
instead of
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
. Since
{\rm Irr}(G)
and
{\rm Irr}(H)
are infinite, it follows from
[
FT24
, Lemma 2.4]
that both
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
are diffuse. Hence, since
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is finite dimensional,

M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\nprec_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}\beta_{\kappa}(B)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)

and,
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\subset B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is entirely non-trivial.

From the discussion above, Theorem
6.12

(1)
and Lemma
2.20

(2)
we find that

\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})))\subseteq\{u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)))\,:\,{\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))={\rm id}\}\subset\mathbb{C}1\otimes\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)).

Let
u\in\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))
and write
u=1\otimes v
, with
v\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
. Then, for all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
,
u(1_{B}\otimes x)u^{*}=1_{B}\otimes vxv^{*}=1_{B}\otimes x
, which implies that
v\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. The converse inclusion being trivial, this gives the formula for the center. Note that
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
has no amenable direct summand by Theorem
6.12

(4)
.

(2)
. Let
p\in\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
be a non-zero projection. Write
p=1\otimes q
with
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. Note that:

L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)=M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p=(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}{*}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q).

Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)
are diffuse and
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
is finite dimensional, we can apply Theorem
6.12

(2)
and we find:

T(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p)=T(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p))=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists v\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)}={\rm Ad}(v)\},

Fix
t\in T(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p))
and let
v\in\mathcal{U}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
be such that
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)}={\rm Ad}(v)
. Note that, since
F
is a finite quantum group, the Haar state of
F
is tracial by
[
VD97
, Theorem 2.2]
so
\sigma_{t}^{h_{F}}={\rm id}
hence, restricting the modular group of
\varphi_{\kappa}
to
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, we find that
\sigma^{\psi_{\kappa}}_{-t}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}\otimes{\rm id}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(v))
. Lemma
2.20
implies that
v=1\otimes u
for some unitary
u\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
and
\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}
. In particular,
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}={\rm id}
and, restricting this time the modular group of
\varphi_{\kappa}
to
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
gives
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(1\otimes u)
. Hence,
T(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p))\subseteq T^{\prime}
, where:

T^{\prime}:=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)\}\cap\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}\}.

Let now
t\in T^{\prime}
. Then
\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}
implies that
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}={\rm id}_{B}
and
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}={\rm id}_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})}
. Let
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))
be such that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)
. Then,

\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}=\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(1\otimes u)\text{ and,}

\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{F}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}={\rm id}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes u))

since
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))
. It follows that
t\in T(L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p))
.
∎

Theorem 6.15
.

Suppose that
\beta
be is a
2
-ergodic action,
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 3
,
{\rm Irr}(G)
and
{\rm Irr}(H)
are infinite and
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite. The following holds.

(1)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
has no amenable direct summand and:

\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))=\mathbb{C}1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes\left({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\cap\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\right).

(2)

For all non-zero projection
p\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
one has:

T({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p)=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)\}\cap\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}\},

where
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is the unique projection such that
p=1\otimes q
.

(3)

For all non-zero projection
p\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
the following are equivalent:

(a)

H
is Kac and there exists a positive and invertible element
Q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)

such that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(Q^{it})
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, where
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is the unique projection such that
p=1\otimes q
.

(b)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
is finite.

(c)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
is semifinite.

Proof.

We write
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F),1\leq\gamma\leq K}{*}L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})
and since
L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})}{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
(Lemma
6.13
) we have in particular that the inclusion
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\subset L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})
is entirely non-trivial, for
1\leq\gamma\leq K
.

(1)
. Note that we always have
\mathbb{C}1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\cap\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G)))\subseteq\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
. If
K=1
statement
(1)
follows directly from Lemma
6.14
(since
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 3
). Hence we may and will assume that
K\geq 2
so that we can apply Theorem
6.12
to deduce that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
has no amenable direct summand. Let
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})))
. If there exists
1\leq\kappa\leq K
such that
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
then, since
u\in\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
, it follows from Lemma
6.10
that
u
is in the center of
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
. We deduce that
u\in\mathbb{C}1_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)}\otimes({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\cap\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G)))
by Lemma
6.14
. Hence, we may and will assume that
N_{\kappa}=1
for all
\kappa
. Hence,
B=\mathbb{C}^{K}
and
K\geq 3
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})))
. By Theorem
6.12
,
u\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. In particular,
u\in\mathcal{Z}(L_{1}(\mathbb{G}))\cap\mathcal{Z}(L_{2}(\mathbb{G}))
. For each
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, we can again apply Theorem
6.12
to the amalgamated free product
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})=(\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\underset{\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{*}({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
to deduce:

\mathcal{Z}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))\cap\beta_{\kappa}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F).

In particular,
u\in(\beta_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))\cap(\beta_{2}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))=\mathbb{C}1\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
, by Lemma
2.21
. Then, there exists
v\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
such that
u=1\otimes v
and since
u
is central in
\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
, we have
v\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
. Hence,
\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq\mathbb{C}1\otimes\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
.

(2)
. Once again, we may and will assume that
K\geq 2
, By Lemma
6.14
. Let
p=1\otimes q
be a projection in
\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
, with
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. Note that
p
is in the centralizer of
h_{\mathbb{G}}
and
q
is in the centralizer of
h_{G}
and:

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p\simeq\underset{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q,1\leq\gamma\leq K}{*}L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p.

Since
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p\nprec_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p}{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
(Lemma
6.13
) we have
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}(L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p)\subset L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p
for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
, by Lemma
6.10
. Define:

T^{\prime}:=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)\}\cap\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}\}.

Let
t\in T^{\prime}
and let
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q))
such that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(u)
. Since
\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}
one has
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}={\rm id}
and
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\gamma}}={\rm id}
for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. Arguing as in the end of the proof of Lemma
6.14
, we deduce that
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\gamma}}|_{L(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{\gamma})(1\otimes p)}={\rm Ad}(v)
, where

v:=(1\otimes u)=(\beta_{\gamma}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes u)\in\mathcal{U}(L_{\gamma}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p))\subset\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)),\text{ for all }\gamma\in\{1,\dots,K\}.

Hence
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\gamma}}|_{L(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{\gamma})(1\otimes p)}
is actually implemented by the same unitary in
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. Since the isomorphism
M_{N_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p\simeq L_{\gamma}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)
intertwines
\psi_{\gamma}^{-1}\otimes h_{\gamma}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}
, it intertwines the modular automorphisms
\sigma_{t}^{\psi_{\gamma}^{-1}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}={\rm id}_{M_{N_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}
and
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)}
and since the isomorphism is the inclusion on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
,
{\rm id}_{M_{N_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{C})}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}
and hence
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}
is implemented by the same unitary in
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. Since
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}=\underset{1\leq\gamma\leq K}{*}\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}|_{L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p}
, we deduce that
t\in T({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p)
.

Let us prove the converse inclusion. Let
t\in T({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p)
. Then, there exists
v\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p)
such that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}={\rm Ad}(v)
. Since
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}({L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})})=\sigma_{t}^{h_{\gamma}}(L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G}))=L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})
, we have
v\in\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}(L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p)\subset L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p
, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. If there exists
1\leq\kappa\leq K
such that
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
then it follows that
t\in T(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p)
so
t\in T^{\prime}
, by Lemma
6.14
. Hence, we may and will assume that
N_{\kappa}=1
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. In that case,
B=\mathbb{C}^{K}
,
K\geq 3
and
\psi
is the uniform trace since it is the unique
\delta
-form on
\mathbb{C}^{K}
. Viewing, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
,

L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p=(\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)\underset{\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}{*}({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)

and observing that:

{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}\beta_{\kappa}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q\text{ and,}

\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q\nprec_{\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F),

we may apply Lemma
6.10
to deduce that
v\in(\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)\cap({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)\subset L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})q
, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. In particular,
v\in\beta_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q\cap\beta_{2}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q=\mathbb{C}1\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, by Lemma
2.21
. Write
v=1\otimes u
, where
u\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
. Since,
\sigma_{t}^{\psi}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm Ad}(1\otimes u)
we deduce that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm Ad}(u)
and
u\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
(since
F
is Kac). We also have
\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{F}}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{1}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes u))={\rm id}
, which implies that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{H}}={\rm id}
.

(3)
. We fix a projection
p\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
and we write
p=1\otimes q
, where
q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is a projection.

(a)\Rightarrow(b)
. Assume that
H
is Kac and there exists
Q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
positive and invertible such that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(Q^{it})
. Then
\psi
and
\psi_{\kappa}^{-1}
are traces so

\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}={\rm id}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes Q^{it}))

since
Q\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
. Also,
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm id}\otimes\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(1\otimes Q^{it})
. Hence,
\sigma_{t}^{\varphi_{\kappa}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p}={\rm Ad}(Q_{1}^{it})
, where
Q_{1}:=(1\otimes Q)=(\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes Q)\in L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})p
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
. Arguing as in the end of the proof of
(2)
, we deduce that
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\kappa}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})p}
is implemented by the same unitary
Q_{1}^{it}\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
and so
\sigma_{t}^{h_{\mathbb{G}}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}
is also implemented by
Q_{1}^{it}\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. It follows that the faithful state
\tau=h_{\mathbb{G}}(p)^{-1}h_{\mathbb{G}}(Q_{1}^{-1}\cdot)|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p}
is a trace on
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
.

(b)\Rightarrow(c)
is obvious.

(c)\Rightarrow(a)
. Assume that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
is semifinite. Hence,
T({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p)=\mathbb{R}
and it follows from
(2)
that
H
is Kac. In particular,
\psi
is a tracial
\delta
-form so it is the uniform trace on
B
i.e.
\psi=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\kappa}\frac{1}{N_{\kappa}}{\rm Tr}_{\kappa}
(and
\psi_{\gamma}
is tracial, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
). Moreover, since
L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})p
has expectation,
L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p
is semifinite, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
, so
L_{\gamma}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)=M_{N_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})p
also is. Write
L_{\gamma}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})(1\otimes p)=(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q)\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q}{*}(M_{N_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
and apply Theorem
6.12

(3)
to deduce that there exists a faithful normal semifinite trace
\omega
on
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
such that
\omega\circ({\rm id}\otimes E_{F})
is tracial on
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
. Note that, since
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
is finite dimensional,
\omega
is actually finite and up to renormalization, we may and will assume that
\omega
is a faithful normal tracial state on
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q
. Then, there exists
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
positive and invertible such that
\omega=(\psi\otimes h_{F})(Q_{0}\cdot)
. Write
Q_{0}=\sum_{\gamma,r}e^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes Q_{\gamma}
, where
Q_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
is positive and invertible. In particular,
Q:=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\gamma}Q_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)q)
is positive and invertible and, for all
x\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q
, one has:

\displaystyle\omega\circ({\rm id}\otimes E_{F})(1\otimes x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\gamma,r}\frac{1}{N_{\gamma}}({\rm Tr}_{\gamma}\otimes h_{F})(e^{\gamma}_{rr}\otimes Q_{\gamma}E_{F}(x))=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\gamma}h_{F}(Q_{\gamma}E_{F}(x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\gamma}h_{F}(E_{F}(Q_{\gamma}x))=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\gamma}h_{G}(Q_{\gamma}x)=h_{G}(Qx).

Note that the modular group of
h_{G}(Q\cdot)|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}
is given by
Q^{it}\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}(\cdot)Q^{-it}
. Since
\omega\circ({\rm id}\otimes E_{F})
is tracial, the faithful state
h_{G}(Q\cdot)|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}
also is hence
Q^{it}\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}(\cdot)|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}Q^{-it}={\rm id}
and
\sigma_{t}^{h_{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)q}={\rm Ad}(Q^{-it})
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.∎

Remark 6.16
.

Note that condition
(a)
in statement
(3)
of the previous Theorem implies in particular that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is a finite von Neumann algebra.

Example 6.17
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.15
,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a factor when the amalgam
F
is trivial. However, coming back to the example
\mathbb{G}:=\widehat{\Gamma}\wr_{*,\beta}\widehat{\Lambda}
given in Section
3.3.4
, Remark
3.13
implies that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=L(\Gamma)^{*|\Lambda|}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\Lambda)
whenever
\Lambda
is abelian. Hence,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is never a factor, as soon as
\Lambda
is a non-trivial abelian finite group.

Let us compute the invariant
T^{\tau}_{\rm Int}(\mathbb{G}):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})),\,\,\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}={\rm Ad}(u)\}
as defined in
[
KS24
]
. We also recall the following notation from
[
KS24
]
, for any CQG
H
:

T^{\tau}(H):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\tau_{t}^{H}={\rm id}\}.

Proposition 6.18
.

If
\beta
is
2
-ergodic,
{\dim}(B)\geq 3
, both
{\rm Irr}(G)
and
{\rm Irr}(H)
are infinite and
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite then
T^{\tau}_{\rm Int}(\mathbb{G})=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))\,\,\tau_{t}^{G}={\rm Ad}(u)\}\cap T^{\tau}(H)
.

Proof.

Let
t\in T^{\tau}_{\rm Int}(\mathbb{G})
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
such that
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}={\rm Ad}(u_{0})
. Since
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}
preserves the subalgebras
L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, and
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))^{\prime\prime}\subset L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
we deduce that
u_{0}\in L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
.

First assume that, for some
1\leq\kappa\leq K
,
N_{\kappa}\geq 2
. Identifying
L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
with
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
and
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}:=\tau_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}
with
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}|_{L_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})}
we can write
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}={\rm Ad}(w)
, where
w=v\otimes u_{0}
for some
v\in\mathcal{U}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C}))
. Viewing
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
as an amalgamated free product and since, by construction,
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}
leaves globally invariant both subalgebras
B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
and
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
we deduce that there exists
u
in the amalgam
M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
such that
\widetilde{\tau}^{\kappa}_{t}={\rm Ad}(u)
. In particular,
\sigma_{-t}^{\psi_{\kappa}}\otimes\tau_{t}^{H}\otimes{\rm id}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm id})(u))
(since
\tau_{t}^{F}
is trivial, because
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is finite dimensional). Lemma
2.20
gives that
u\in\mathbb{C}1\otimes\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
and
\tau_{t}^{H}={\rm id}
. Hence, viewing
u\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
we have
\tau_{t}^{G}={\rm Ad}(u)
.

Now assume that
N_{\kappa}=1
, for all
1\leq\kappa\leq K
so that
B=\mathbb{C}^{K}
,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})={\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
,
K\geq 3
and
\psi
is the uniform trace since it is the unique
\delta
-form on
\mathbb{C}^{K}
. Viewing, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
,

L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})=(\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\underset{\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{*}({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))

and since
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}
leaves globally invariant both
\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
, we may apply Lemma
6.10
to deduce that
u_{0}\in(\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\cap({\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F))\subset L_{\gamma}(\mathbb{G})
, for all
1\leq\gamma\leq K
. In particular,
u_{0}\in\beta_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\cap\beta_{2}(\mathbb{C}^{K})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)=\mathbb{C}1\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
, by Lemma
2.21
. Write
u_{0}=1\otimes u
, where
u\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
. Since

\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}|_{\mathbb{C}^{K}\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)}=\sigma_{-t}^{\psi}\otimes\tau_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm Ad}(1\otimes u)={\rm id}\otimes{\rm Ad}(u)

we deduce that
\tau_{t}^{h_{G}}={\rm Ad}(u)
and
u\in\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F))
(since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is finite dimensional). We also have:

\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}|_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}=\tau_{t}^{H}\otimes\tau_{t}^{F}={\rm Ad}((\beta_{1}\otimes{\rm id})(1\otimes u))={\rm id},

which implies that
\tau_{t}^{H}={\rm id}
. This shows the first inclusion.

Let now
t\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\tau_{t}^{H}={\rm id}
and
u\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(F)))
such that
\tau_{t}^{G}={\rm Ad}(u)
. By construction of
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}
, we can argue as the in proof of the
T
-invariant computation in Theorem
6.15
to deduce that
\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}={\rm Ad}(u)
.∎

Remark 6.19
.

Under the hypothesis of the previous Proposition and if
F
is moreover trivial then
T^{\tau}_{\rm Int}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}(G)\cap T^{\tau}(H)=T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})
. In particular, the Vaes
T
-invariant
[
Vae05
]

T_{V}(\mathbb{G}):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\exists u\in\mathcal{U}({\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))\,\,\tau_{t}^{\mathbb{G}}={\rm Ad}(u)\text{ and }\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(u)=u\otimes u\}

satisfies

T_{V}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}_{\rm Int}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}(G)\cap T^{\tau}(H)
.

6.3.
Primeness and absence of Cartan subalgebras

In this subsection we prove the following result, which is a more general form of Theorem
C
.

Theorem 6.20
.

Suppose that
\beta
be is a
2
-ergodic action,
{\rm dim}(B)\geq 3
,
{\rm Irr}(G)
and
{\rm Irr}(H)
are infinite,
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)\cap\mathcal{Z}({\rm L}^{\infty}(G))=\mathbb{C}1
. Then
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a non-amenable factor of type
{\rm II}_{1}
or
{\rm III}
satisfying the following:

(1)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is prime.

(2)

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
does not have any Cartan subalgebra.

Proof.

The fact that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a non-amenable factor of type
{\rm II}_{1}
or
{\rm III}
follows from Theorem
6.15
. Using the notations of Remark
5.9
, we write
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
as an amalgamated free product:

{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\simeq M_{K}\simeq M_{K-1}\underset{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}{\ast}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)).

To simplify the notations we write
M
for
M_{K-1}
. We then have the following Claim.

Claim.

One has
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
and
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
.

Proof of the Claim.
Since
{\rm Irr}(G)
is infinite, the von Neumann algebra
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)
is diffuse
[
FT24
, Lemma 2.4]
and since the Haar state of
G
is almost periodic, its centralizer
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)^{h_{G}}
is still diffuse. Let
(u_{n})_{n}
be a sequence of unitaries in
{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)^{h_{G}}
going to
0

\sigma
-weakly. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))*_{B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)}M
we may apply Lemma
6.6
to deduce that

({\rm id}_{B}\otimes E_{F}^{\prime\prime})(x(1\otimes u_{n})y)\rightarrow 0\,\,\text{ in norm for all }x,y\in B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G).

Since
\nu_{K}(1\otimes u_{n})
is in the centralizer of
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
for all
n
, we may apply Lemma
6.8
to deduce that
E_{M}(x\nu_{K}(1\otimes u_{n})y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. In particular,
E_{M}(x\rho^{K}_{ij}(u_{n})y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
and
1\leq i,j\leq N_{\kappa}
. Note that the map

\rho_{K}\,:\,{\rm L}^{\infty}(G)\rightarrow M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),\quad a\mapsto\sum_{i,j}e^{K}_{ij}\otimes\rho^{K}_{ij}(a)

is a unital
*
-homomorphism so that
\rho_{K}(u_{n})
is unitary in
M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Moreover,

E_{M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M}(X\rho_{K}(u_{n})Y)=\sum_{i,j,r,s}e^{K}_{ij}\otimes E_{M}(X_{ir}\rho^{\kappa}_{rs}(u_{n})Y_{sj})\rightarrow 0\,\,\sigma\text{-strongly}

for all
X,Y\in M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Since
M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is finite or of type
{\rm III}
we have

M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes M.

Hence,
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
.

Now, since
{\rm Irr}(H)
is infinite and arguing as in the beginning of the proof, we have a sequence of unitaries
(v_{n})_{n}
in
{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)^{h_{H}}
going to
0

\sigma
-weakly so
1\otimes v_{n}\otimes 1\in M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
is going to
0

\sigma
-weakly and, by Lemma
6.6
, we deduce that
(E_{K}\otimes{\rm id}_{F})(x(1\otimes v_{n}\otimes 1)y)\rightarrow 0
in norm, for all
x,y\in M_{N_{K}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(H)\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(F)
. Next, since
\mu(v_{n}\otimes 1)\in M
is in the centralizer of
h_{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}}
for all
n
, we can apply Lemma
6.8
and deduce that
E_{\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))}(x\mu(v_{n}\otimes 1)y)\rightarrow 0

\sigma
-strongly for all
x,y\in{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Note that
\mu(v_{n}\otimes 1)
is actually a sequence of unitaries in
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is finite or type
{\rm III}
, we deduce that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
.
∎

End of the proof of Theorem
6.20
.
(1)
. By contradiction, assume that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=P\otimes Q
, where
P
and
Q
are two diffuse factors. Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is either finite or type
{\rm III}
we may and will assume that both
P
and
Q
are either finite or of type
{\rm III}
. Moreover, since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is not amenable one of them, let say
P
, is not amenable. Note that, since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=P\otimes Q
, there is a normal faithful conditional expectation from
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
to
Q
(and to
P
) and since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
has expectation, we deduce that both
Q,P\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
have expectation. Since
Q
is diffuse, we may apply
[
HU16a
, Lemma 2.1]
to get a f.n.s.
\omega\in Q_{*}
with diffuse centralizer
A:=Q^{\omega}\subset Q
. Note that
A\subset Q
has expectation by Takesaki’s Theorem hence
A\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
also has expectation. Also,
P\subset Q^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset A^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset A^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Since
P
is not amenable and has expectation,
A^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is not amenable either. Since
A
is finite, diffuse and has expectation we may apply
[
HU16b
, Theorem 4.4]
to deduce that either
A\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
or
A\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
. Applying now Lemma
6.9
we deduce that
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
or
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
. Since
P\subset A^{\prime}\cap{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
, Remark
6.3

(4)
implies that
P\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
or
P\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
. Applying again Lemma
6.9
we get that
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(P)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))
or
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(P)^{\prime\prime}\prec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M
. However,
P,Q\subset\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(P)^{\prime\prime}
which implies that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(P)^{\prime\prime}
so that, with Remark
6.3

(4)
and the Claim, we obtain a contradiction.

(2)
. By contradiction, let
A\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
be a Cartan subalgebra (with expectation). Since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
has expectation,
A\subset{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
has expectation. Note that
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(A)^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}
. Hence, the Claim implies that

\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}\nu_{K}(B\otimes{\rm L}^{\infty}(G))\text{ and }\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}\nprec_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}M.

Moreover, since
{\rm L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is not amenable and has expectation,
\mathcal{N}_{{\rm L}^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})}(A)^{\prime\prime}
is not amenable either. We get a contradiction with Lemma
6.11
.∎

7.
KK-theory

In this section, we study
K
-amenability and we use our free product decomposition of the C*-algebra of a free wreath product as well as the one of the block-extended C*-algebra and the exact sequences from
[
FG18
,
FG20
]
to compute
K
-theory.

Proposition 7.1
.

For a compact quantum group
G
, the class of the unit
[1]\in K_{0}(C(G))
is never
0
or of torsion. If
G
is K-amenable, then this is also true for
[1]\in K_{0}(C_{r}(G))
.

Proof.

We use the counit, which is a
*
-morphism
\varepsilon:C(G)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, which gives
\varepsilon_{\ast}:K_{0}(C(G))\rightarrow K_{0}(\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
, sending
[1]
to
[1]
, which is the generator of the K-theory group of
\mathbb{C}
. Therefore the class cannot be of torsion. If
G
is K-amenable, the same holds for the reduced C*-algebra, because
\lambda_{\ast}
, which is an isomorphism in that case, sends the class
[1]
to
[1]
too.
∎

We will need the following Lemma about the Morita equivalence between a unital C*-algebra
A
and its amplification
M_{N}(A)
. For any unital C*-algebra
A
, we define the KK-theory class

m_{A}\in KK(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes A,A)=[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A,{\rm id},0].

It is the inverse to the class of the non unital map
i:A\rightarrow M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes A
sending
a
to
e_{11}\otimes a
.

Lemma 7.2
.

For any element
\gamma=[H,\eta,F]\in KK(A,B)
, the Kasparov product of
m_{A}
and
\gamma
is given by
m_{A}\otimes\gamma=[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H,{\rm id}\otimes\eta,1\otimes F]\in KK(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes A,B).

Proof.

The Kasparov product of
m_{A}
and
\gamma
can be taken as
[(\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A)\otimes_{A}H,{\rm id}\otimes_{\eta}1,\tilde{F}]
, where
\tilde{F}
is an adequate
F
-connection making it a Kasparov triple. There is a unitary isomorphism

\displaystyle U:(\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A)\otimes_{A}H

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H

\displaystyle(f\otimes a)\otimes_{\eta}\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto f\otimes\eta(a)\xi.

Under this isomorphism, the map
{\rm id}\otimes_{\eta}1
corresponds to the map
U({\rm id}\otimes_{\eta}1)U^{*}
which sends any element
b\otimes a\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes A
to
b\otimes\eta(a)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H)
. We then fix
\tilde{F}
to be the operator
U(1\otimes F)U^{*}
, and we only have to check that
\tilde{F}
is a
F
-connection and that it defines a Kasparov module.
First, for any
b\otimes a\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes A
, we have

[({\rm id}\otimes\eta)(b\otimes a),1\otimes F]=b\otimes[\eta(a),F],

which is a compact operator because
[\eta(a),F]
is, by hypothesis, and
\mathbb{C}^{N}
is finite dimensional. The same way,
(1\otimes F)^{2}({\rm id}\otimes\eta)(b\otimes a)=b\otimes F^{2}\eta(a)
and

(1\otimes F-1\otimes F^{*})({\rm id}\otimes\eta)(b\otimes a)=b\otimes(F-F^{*})\eta(a)

are also compact operators. Lastly, we have to check the connection properties. For any
x\in(\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A)
, we define the adjointable map
T_{x}\in\mathcal{L}(H,(\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes A)\otimes_{A}H),\xi\mapsto x\otimes_{\eta}\xi
. Then if
x=f\otimes a
, the map
UT_{x}
becomes
\xi\mapsto f\otimes\eta(a)\xi
hence:

T_{x}F-\tilde{F}T_{x}=T_{x}F-U^{*}(1\otimes F)UT_{x}=U^{*}(UT_{x}F-(1\otimes F)UT_{x}).

For any
\xi
, we have
(T_{x}F-\tilde{F}T_{x})\xi=U^{*}(f\otimes(\eta(a)F\xi-F\eta(a)\xi))
, which is a compact operator by hypothesis. The other property can be shown the same way. Thus this indeed defines a Kasparov product for
m_{A}\otimes\gamma
, which corresponds to
[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H,{\rm id}\otimes\eta,1\otimes F]
.
∎

For the remainder of the section, we assume the action
\beta:H\curvearrowright B
to be ergodic and we write
\mathbb{G}=G\wr_{*,\beta,F}H
.

Concerning
K
-amenability, we prove the following. The symbol
\bullet
is used to denote either the full or reduced C*-algebra of a CQG or amalgamated free product of C*-algebras.

Theorem 7.3
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

G
and
H
are K-amenable.

(2)

\mathbb{G}
is K-amenable.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
.
Assume first that
G
and
H
are K-amenable, then we can apply
[
FG18
, Theorem 5.1]
to the amalgamated free product

C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\simeq(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\underset{B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F)}{\ast_{\bullet}}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H\times F)),

to get that the full algebra
C_{\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
is K-equivalent to the reduced
C_{r,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
for all
\kappa
, through the canonical map
\widetilde{\lambda_{\kappa}}
and as
C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})\simeq M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
, the same is true for the map
\lambda_{\kappa}
such that
\widetilde{\lambda_{\kappa}}={\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{\kappa}
. We then apply it to

C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})\simeq\underset{C_{\bullet}(H\times F)}{\ast_{\bullet}}C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}),

to get that
\mathbb{G}
is K-amenable.

(2)\Rightarrow(1).

Assume that
\mathbb{G}
is K-amenable i.e. there exists
\gamma\in KK(C_{r}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{C})
such that
[\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}]\otimes\gamma=[\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}}]\in KK(C(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{C})
. Then, since
H
is a dual quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
, it is also K-amenable. We then fix any
1\leq\kappa\leq K
, and we consider the morphism
\pi:C(G)\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})
defined by
\pi:a\mapsto\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{11})\rho_{\kappa}(a)
. Note that
({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}})\circ\pi=\varepsilon_{G}(\cdot)e_{11}^{\kappa}
. By Theorem
4.5
we have
({\rm id}_{B}\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})\rho=h_{G}(\cdot)1_{B}
. In particular,
({\rm id}\otimes h_{\mathbb{G}})\rho_{\kappa}=h_{G}(\cdot)1
. It follows that there exists a unique unital
*
-homomorphism
\rho_{r,\kappa}\,:\,C_{r}(G)\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
such that
({\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{\mathbb{G}})\rho_{\kappa}=\rho_{r,\kappa}\lambda_{G}
. Viewing
\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{11})
as a projection in
(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes{\rm Pol}(H))\cap\rho_{r,\kappa}(C_{r}(G))^{\prime}
, the map
\rho_{r,\kappa}(C_{r}(G))\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
,
x\mapsto\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{11})x
is a (non-unital)
*
-homomorphism. Hence
\pi_{r}:C_{r}(G)\rightarrow M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{r}(\mathbb{G})
defined by
\pi_{r}(a):=\beta_{\kappa}(e^{\kappa}_{11})\rho_{r,\kappa}(a)
is such that
\pi_{r}\circ\lambda_{G}=({\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{\mathbb{G}})\circ\pi
. Let us show that
\tilde{\gamma}:=[\pi_{r}]\otimes m_{C_{r}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma\in KK(C_{r}(G),\mathbb{C})
satisfies
[\lambda_{G}]\otimes\tilde{\gamma}=[\varepsilon_{G}]
. Using Lemma
7.2
we find:

\displaystyle[\lambda_{G}]\otimes\tilde{\gamma}

\displaystyle=[\lambda_{G}]\otimes[\pi_{r}]\otimes m_{C_{r}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma=[\pi_{r}\circ\lambda_{G}]\otimes m_{C_{r}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma

\displaystyle=[({\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{\mathbb{G}})\circ\pi]\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H,{\rm id}\otimes\eta,1\otimes F]

\displaystyle=[\pi]\otimes[{\rm id}\otimes\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}]\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H,{\rm id}\otimes\eta,1\otimes F]

\displaystyle=[\pi]\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes H,({\rm id}\otimes\eta\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}),1\otimes F]=[\pi]\otimes m_{C(\mathbb{G})}\otimes[H,\eta\lambda_{\mathbb{G}},F]

\displaystyle=[\pi]\otimes m_{C(\mathbb{G})}\otimes[\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}]\otimes[H,\eta,F]=[\pi]\otimes m_{C(\mathbb{G})}\otimes[\lambda_{\mathbb{G}}]\otimes\gamma=[\pi]\otimes m_{C(\mathbb{G})}\otimes[\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}}]

\displaystyle=[\pi]\otimes m_{C(\mathbb{G})}\otimes[\mathbb{C},\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}},0]=[\pi]\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{N},{\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}},0]=[\mathbb{C}^{N},({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbb{G}})\circ\pi,0]

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{C}^{N},\varepsilon_{G}(\cdot)e_{11}^{\kappa},0]=[\mathbb{C},\varepsilon_{G},0]\oplus[\mathbb{C}^{N-1},0,0]=[\varepsilon_{G}],

because the action of
e_{11}^{\kappa}
is only on the first coordinate in
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, and the second summand is a degenerate module. Hence
[\lambda_{G}]\otimes\tilde{\gamma}=[\varepsilon_{G}]
and
G
is K-amenable.
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

For a compact quantum group
G
with a dual quantum subgroup
F
, and a compact quantum group
H
acting on
(B,\psi)
, and there is an exact sequence of K-theory:

\displaystyle K_{0}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F)))

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scalebox{0.85}{\mbox{$\displaystyle K_{0}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))$}}\oplus\scalebox{0.85}{\mbox{$\displaystyle K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H\times F))$}}}

\displaystyle K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))

\displaystyle K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))

\displaystyle K_{1}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\oplus K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H\times F))

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scalebox{0.85}{\mbox{$\displaystyle K_{1}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F))$}}.}

Moreover, the vertical arrow on the right is zero as soon as
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite, and the vertical arrow on the left is zero as soon as the map
\iota_{\ast}:K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(F))\rightarrow K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(G))
is injective.

Proof.

We write

M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\simeq(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\underset{B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F)}{\ast_{\bullet}}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H\times F)),

and we can immediately apply the result of
[
FG20
]
to get the exact sequences for the reduced and the full case. Now assume that
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite. Then
C(F)\simeq C_{r}(F)\simeq{\rm Pol}(F)
is a finite dimensional C*-algebra, therefore we have immediately
K_{1}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F))=0
and so the vertical map on the right-hand side is
0
. We can also deduce from this that

K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H)\otimes C(F))\simeq K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H))\otimes K_{0}(C(F)),

and we also have in any case

K_{0}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(F))\simeq K_{0}(B)\otimes K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(F)).

Using that
\iota_{\ast}:K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(F))\rightarrow K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(G))
is injective, we see that the first horizontal map must be injective in the full case. This also implies that the left vertical map is
0
.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

In the case where
F
is trivial and we consider the maximal C*-algebras, Proposition
7.1
shows that the map
\iota_{\ast}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow K_{0}(C(G))
is injective, so the vertical arrow on the left is zero. This is also true for the reduced C*-algebras is
G
has a K-amenable dual.

We deduce the following proposition.

Proposition 7.6
.

If
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite and
\iota_{*}\,:\,K_{0}(C(F))\rightarrow K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(G))
is injective then, we have an isomorphism

K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq K_{1}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\oplus K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H)\otimes C(F)),

and a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow K_{0}(B\otimes C(F))\rightarrow K_{0}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\oplus K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H)\otimes C(F))\rightarrow K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow 0.

This shows that the group
K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
is the quotient

\displaystyle K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq K_{0}(B\otimes C_{\bullet}(G))\oplus K_{0}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H)\otimes C(F))/<[e_{11}^{\gamma}\otimes f]-[\beta_{\kappa}(e_{11}^{\gamma})\otimes f]>,

where
f\in C(F)
are projections corresponding to the generators of
K_{0}(C(F))
and
1\leq\gamma\leq K
.

We use the other isomorphism
C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})\simeq\underset{C_{\bullet}(H)\otimes C(F)}{\ast_{\bullet}}C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G})
to get another exact sequence.

Proposition 7.7
.

There is a 6-term exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K-1}K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(H\times F))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K}K_{0}(C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K}K_{1}(C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K-1}K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(H\times F)).}

Moreover, when
{\rm Irr}(F)
is finite, the map on the right is
0
, and if
F
is trivial, then the first map on the left is also
0
, in the maximal case.

Proof.

The exact sequence is a direct application of
[
FG20
]
to the amalgamated free product.
The map
K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(H\times F))\rightarrow K_{1}(C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
corresponds to
\iota_{\ast}
, while the map in the part of the isomorphism
K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet}(H\times F))\hookrightarrow K_{1}(M_{N_{\kappa}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C_{\bullet,\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
corresponds to
({\rm id}\otimes\iota)_{\ast}
, which is injective as shown in Proposition
7.4
. These maps are the same at the level of the K-theory, through the Morita equivalence. Therefore the map at the utmost right on the bottom line is injective, and the map on the right is
0
. Assume then that
F
is trivial. We have a sequence of morphisms

C(H)\xrightarrow{\iota_{\kappa}}C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G})\xrightarrow{\pi_{H}}C(H),

where
\pi_{H}
is defined in Remark
3.7
. The composition is equal to
{\rm id}_{H}
. Therefore, at the K-theoretic level, we have that the maps
K_{0}(C(H))\rightarrow K_{0}(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))
must be injective, and that is enough to show that the map on the left side is
0
.
∎

We deduce the following.

Proposition 7.8
.

If
F
is trivial then:

K_{1}(C(\mathbb{G}))\simeq K_{1}(C(H))\oplus\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K}K_{1}(B\otimes C(G))\simeq K_{1}(C(H))\oplus K_{1}(C(G))^{\oplus K^{2}}.

and,

K_{0}(C(\mathbb{G}))\simeq\bigoplus_{\kappa=1}^{K}K_{0}(C_{\kappa}(\mathbb{G}))/<\iota_{\gamma\ast}(h)-\iota_{\kappa\ast}(h)>,

for all
1\leq\kappa,\gamma\leq K
, and
g\in K_{0}(C(H))
.

The easiest cases are the ones where
H=S_{N}^{+}
or
H={\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
, in which we know the generators of the
K_{0}
group by the following proposition. We first recall the following Theorem by Voigt.

Theorem 7.9
.

[
Voi17
]

Let
(B,\psi)
be a finite dimensional C*-algebra with a
\delta
-form. The CQG
H={\rm Aut}^{+}(B,\psi)
is K-amenable and the K-theory groups of
C_{\bullet}(H)
are

K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(H))\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{K^{2}-2K+2}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d}^{2K-1},

where
d
is the gcd of the sizes of the blocks and
K
their number, and

K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(H))\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

In the case
S_{N}^{+}
, it is also proved that the family of the following set of classes of projections

\{[u_{ij}],\,1\leq i,j\leq N-1\}\cup\{1\}

is a family of generators for
K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(S_{N}^{+}))
.

Lemma 7.10
.

If
H={\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
then the class
[\beta(e_{11})]
generates the free part of
K_{0}(C(H))
. That is to say
\mathbb{Z}\simeq\langle[\beta(e_{11})]\rangle\subset K_{0}(C(H))\simeq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}
is a direct factor.

Proof.

We use the counit
\varepsilon_{H}
which is a unital
*
-morphism from
C(H)
to
\mathbb{C}
. We have that
({\rm id}\otimes\varepsilon_{H})\beta={\rm id}
. Therefore the map
\varepsilon_{H\ast}
is a left-inverse to the map
\beta_{\ast}
. Hence:

\mathbb{Z}\simeq K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}))\xrightarrow{\beta_{\ast}}K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H))\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\ast}}K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}))\simeq\mathbb{Z},

with the composition being the identity morphism. This implies that
[\beta(e_{11})]=\beta_{\ast}([e_{11}])
generates a direct factor.
∎

As a consequence we can compute the K-theory groups of any free wreath product where
G
is a finite abelian group and
H
is
{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
. We give the following proposition for quantum groups of the form
\mathbb{Z}_{s}\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
.

Theorem 7.11
.

For
s,N\in\mathbb{N}
, and any faithful state
\psi\in M_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{*}
, the compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{Z}_{s}\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
is K-amenable and the K-theory groups of
C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})
are:

K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{s}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

In the case
B=M_{N}(\mathbb{C})
, we have the isomorphism
M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(\mathbb{G})\simeq C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
. Therefore we can compute the K-theory groups of
C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})
only. The K-theory groups of
C(\mathbb{Z}_{s})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{s}
are

K_{0}(\mathbb{C}^{s})\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{s}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{s})\simeq 0.

A basis of generators for
K_{0}(\mathbb{C}^{s})\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{s}
is the classes
[e_{i}]
for
1\leq i\leq s
but we remark that we can replace the last generator by the class
[1]=\sum_{i=1}^{s}[e_{i}]
, so that
\mathbb{Z}\cdot[1]
is a direct factor in it. We immediately have that

K_{1}(C(\mathbb{G}))\simeq K_{1}(C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))\simeq K_{1}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H))\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

For the
K_{0}
group we use the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}))\rightarrow K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(\mathbb{Z}_{s}))\oplus K_{0}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C})\otimes C(H))\rightarrow K_{0}(C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))\rightarrow 0.

This gives the isomorphism

K_{0}(C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{s}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}/\langle[1]-[\beta(e_{11})]\rangle.

By the previous discussions on the generators, we know that
\mathbb{Z}[1]
and
\mathbb{Z}[\beta(e_{11})]
are direct factors, and therefore we get that
K_{0}(C(\mathbb{G}))\simeq K_{0}(C(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{s}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.
∎

We can also do the same for the dual of the free groups on
t
generators, K-amenable thanks to the results of Cuntz
[
Cun82
]
.

Proposition 7.12
.

For
t,N\in\mathbb{N}
, the compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}:=\widehat{\mathbb{F}_{t}}\wr_{*}{\rm Aut}^{+}(M_{N}(\mathbb{C}),\psi)
is K-amenable and the K-theory groups of
C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})
are

K_{0}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{N}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{1}(C_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{t+1}.

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same as the previous case. Using that:

\displaystyle K_{0}(C^{\ast}\mathbb{F}_{t})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{Z},\text{ the generator being }[1],

\displaystyle K_{1}(C^{\ast}\mathbb{F}_{t})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{t}.

We get that the two copies of
\mathbb{Z}
in
K_{0}
are identified, and the result.
∎
